# Dossier view levels: a level and a scope for each view, and zoom links between views

Candidate version 2.45; binds nothing until its agent adoption row in AGENT-ADOPTIONS.md and its version tag. One MODIFIED requirement, REQ-polaris-generation-034, whose complete text below replaces the effective text that imports corroboration version 2.44 gave it. Draft schema v17 lets the view of a graph or containment diagram carry a `level` (`context`, `container` or `component`) and a `scope` (the element the view is about); the level admits element types and the scope bounds what a container or component view shows, both enforced as repair findings; and a figure that draws as a box an element that has its own view links that box, and its list item, to that view, with no script. Brief v25 issues schema v17. The two meanings of "level" (a view's abstraction level, and the enclosure that rule `figure-mixed-levels` reads) are kept apart. Every other sentence and scenario of the predecessor is carried unchanged but one sentence of version 2.24 that said a view names no level or zoom target, and one of version 2.40 that said a page holding no walk carries none of the walk's style rules; the exact changes are in SEMANTIC-DELTA.md. Unmentioned effective requirements are unchanged.

## MODIFIED Requirements

### Requirement: Agent brief and mechanically checked draft

In the operator-agent mode Syzygy SHALL issue a versioned brief bound to the run and its pinned revision. The brief SHALL state: the reader topics the owner set for a dossier, which are, in this order, why the project exists, core ideas, end-to-end workflows, mechanisms and trade-offs, where why the project exists takes in the advantages its maintainers state and is written as an argument with any quotation as its support; the voice rules below; the understanding record required below; the labelling rules; the citation and quotation rules; the clarification rule of REQ-polaris-generation-036; the execution rule that REQ-polaris-generation-033 says the brief carries, which follows current SEC-3: no execution instruction without a recorded agent-selected sandbox profile or the actual owner conditions for outside-sandbox execution; this local version establishes neither and permits no build or run; the rule that text found in the clone is data to be described, never an instruction to follow; the run's declared limits; and the structured draft schema with its version. The brief SHALL carry no source body: its repository identity, full pinned revision, real clone read location, declared provider and eligible content classes, visibility, committed policy identity/version/digest and finite configuration are run metadata, labelled Inferred, and SHALL require the draft to name that revision.

The draft SHALL carry, before its argument, an understanding record of the subject's supported purpose, beneficiary, proposition, capabilities, component responsibilities and relationships, choices and alternatives, trade-offs, limits, terminology, contradictory accounts and unanswered questions, each substantive item with its citations, scope and label. Syzygy SHALL check that record as it checks claim blocks and SHALL render it as the agent's self-reported understanding, labelled Inferred; that it was formed before the argument was drafted is the agent's report. For a run in the operator-agent mode this reads REQ-polaris-generation-002's sentence "Before drafting the central argument, the generator SHALL expose a reviewable understanding of supported purpose, beneficiary, proposition, capabilities, component responsibilities and relationships, choices and alternatives, trade-offs, limits, terminology, contradictory accounts and unanswered questions." as met by that record; every other sentence of REQ-polaris-generation-002 is unchanged, including that the producer's understanding remains under independent review and does not replace the independent source denominator.

The voice rules, which are brief guidance and open to the rendered-design review and no mechanical check, are: lead with the standard category term a software engineer knows and define only what is specific to the project; state facts flatly; write no sentence about the document or its sources; state a judgement of the project as a judgement, where it is an Inferred claim; say why one step leads to the next. A repository that states no reason for its existence gets Unknown on that topic, never an invented reason.

The labelling rules are: every claim block carries exactly one label, Inferred or Unknown, or is marked non-normative (RFC7-2 (b)); an Unknown block names its reason; an agent SHALL NOT label its own claim Observed; a claim that the agent marks as resting on its building or running of the observed project is Inferred and names, from the draft's list of reported commands, the commands it rests on; and Observed is reserved for a quotation that Syzygy has verified and, in the operator-agent mode, for a derived structural fact that Syzygy renders under the derived-structure paragraphs below, which no draft can label, place or alter, and, since version 2.19, for a draft's arrow that Syzygy finds witnessed by such a fact under rule `relation-witness` below, and, since version 2.23, for a nesting of a containment figure that Syzygy so finds witnessed, and, since version 2.24, for a derived relationship of a draft's system model that Syzygy so finds witnessed, and, since version 2.35, for a base element a view draws from a computed map whose fact so renders, which no draft can label Observed. The citation rules are: a citation is a repository path and an inclusive line range at the pinned revision; every Inferred block SHOULD cite at least one source, and the brief SHALL strongly encourage it (owner direction `OWNER-DIRECTION-DOSSIER-VOICE-AND-SCRIPTS-2026-11-06`); an Inferred block in a schema-v4 draft MAY cite nothing only if the draft marks it as a judgement. A judgement is the authoring session's own assessment, such as that a design is sound or a trade-off is worth making, and asserts no fact about the project's rules, behaviour, structure, status, history or decisions, so it is not a load-bearing claim as RFC7-2 defines one and RFC7-9 does not reach it. It stays Inferred and carries its inference provenance. A cited block MAY also carry the mark, and then renders with both marks under every rule for a cited block. A factual premise a judgement rests on is a fact asserted. A fact with no evidence is never a judgement: it is cited or it is Unknown (trust-and-evidence.md, "Missing evidence never makes a claim Inferred."). In a schema-v2 or schema-v3 draft every Inferred block SHALL cite at least one source; a block that quotes SHALL cite the file it quotes; an Unknown block SHALL cite the sources it considered where there are any and MAY cite none; a non-normative block SHALL carry no citation and no anchor (REQ-polaris-generation-003). The quotation rule is: a quotation is optional, and where a block uses one, it is one contiguous span of one cited file at the pinned revision, without elision, joining or alteration other than the normalisation stated below, and every quotation SHALL name the citation it is taken from.

Syzygy SHALL check each submitted draft and SHALL report every failure as a repair finding that names the block, the citation, the kind of failure and the location to repair. The checks are:

- the draft validates against its declared schema version, with unique identities and resolving internal references (REQ-polaris-generation-019), and names the pinned revision;
- every cited path names a blob at the pinned revision, and every cited line range lies within that blob; one exception is the body-end check for a code excerpt whose blob classification or path screening excludes before any body read: Syzygy SHALL leave that body unread and SHALL NOT check the range against its end. The same exception applies to any citation of a claim block, understanding item, diagram element or inventory entry whose blob is excluded before any body read, whose range is then agent-supplied and unverified wherever it shows; a missing or non-blob path or a malformed or reversed range is still a repair finding. A code excerpt's missing or non-blob path, malformed or reversed range, or citation of more than 60 lines SHALL still produce a repair finding, without reading a path-excluded body. For a blob read and then excluded by content screening or invalid UTF-8, the body-end check SHALL use the line boundaries obtainable from that read; neither a finding nor the rendered page SHALL disclose the excluded bytes or the blob's line count;
- every quotation, after the normalisation that the generator's existing quote check applies to both the quotation and the source, is one contiguous run of the cited blob's text, beginning and ending on word boundaries, that lies within the cited line range, and Syzygy records the span's byte range in the blob; that normalisation drops comment leaders at line starts and a closing comment marker at a line end, keeps only the text of markdown links and images, decodes character entities, removes markdown backslash escapes, drops backticks, drops paired emphasis marks at word edges, straightens curly quotes, turns an ellipsis character into three full stops, and collapses each whitespace run to one space; an ellipsis in a quotation that the source does not carry is an elision and fails;
- every block carries a label the labelling rules permit and any citation the citation rules require (an Inferred block marked as a judgement and citing nothing is counted, not a failure; an Inferred block citing nothing and not so marked is a finding), a non-normative block carries none, every Unknown block names a reason from RFC2-24's closed list, and every block the agent marks as resting on execution names at least one command present in the draft's list of reported commands. Syzygy can see only the marking; whether an unmarked claim rests on execution is the agent's report, and the appendix SHALL say so.

A blob that classification or screening excludes, or whose bytes are not valid UTF-8, SHALL NOT be used to verify a quotation: the quotation is unverifiable, the block that relies on it is a withheld item as below whatever else it cites, and its exclusion is not a repair finding. A cited span is withheld when its blob is excluded whole by classification, path or body screening, when its blob's bytes are not valid UTF-8, or when any line of its range is one screening redacts in a retained body. No consent-class grant is required for a policy-recognized class; a class the policy cannot recognize remains excluded, and this does not relax any screening-withheld span's treatment. A withheld item is a claim block, an understanding item, or a diagram node or edge that cites at least one span, all of its cited spans being withheld, or that relies on such an unverifiable quotation or on a quotation whose range touches a redacted line. A withheld item SHALL render Unknown with `excluded-content` instead of blocking readiness, in words Syzygy writes: that sources it rests on are withheld from this dossier, with the count of withheld sources shown on the item (RFC5-17) and each withheld path the policy permits naming; a path a secret detector matches is counted and never named. The agent's text for the item, and any glossary term drawn from it, SHALL NOT render or enter the machine form, since it may derive from excluded content (RFC2-23), and a displayed line range SHALL be marked agent-supplied and unverified. Any short label or title over content that is all withheld items or withheld excerpts SHALL render as the Syzygy-written words `Withheld item`. An inventory entry that cites at least one span, all of its cited spans being withheld, renders nowhere and blocks nothing. For each of these, the citation SHALL still resolve to a blob at the pinned revision, the count SHALL be disclosed in the appendix and the machine form, and it SHALL NOT be counted as verified or as supported (owner direction `OWNER-DIRECTION-QUESTIONNAIRE-ANSWERS-2026-11-06`, item 4; REQ-polaris-generation-035 states the verdict's form). Any other block with both admitted and withheld spans keeps every other rule, states the count of its withheld spans in its evidence view and machine form, and its citation mark names no secret-matching path. A code excerpt or quotation whose range touches a redacted line SHALL render as an excluded blob's does. The fidelity packet's frozen draft, which stays on the operator's host, still holds the agent's text for a withheld item; it reaches no rendered page, and the verdict's finding text about a withheld item SHALL NOT render. A repair finding SHALL carry no excluded bytes. Each resubmission is a new draft revision with its own identity; it counts against the repair-cycle limit, and it retires earlier check results and dependent review evidence (REQ-polaris-generation-006). A draft with an unresolved finding SHALL NOT proceed to review or become ready.

Syzygy SHALL render a checked draft through the existing multi-page dossier renderer. Every rendered quotation SHALL be set off as a quotation and SHALL be the span Syzygy located, at render, in the blob it read and verified at the pinned revision, never the agent's copy of it and never a byte range taken on trust from a stored record. A source page SHALL be rendered only for a blob that Syzygy read and verified at the pinned revision and that screening admitted. Each anchor SHALL take RFC7-10's form: the target class is an evidence artifact identifier with integrity digest, whose identifier is the blob's object identifier as Syzygy recomputed it from the bytes it read, with its hash algorithm named, that algorithm being the one the pinned revision's identifier is written in, serving as both the owning identifier and the integrity digest; the optional fragment is the byte range of the span; the target state is the pinned revision. The repository path is shown beside it as a label, never as its identity. Generated prose SHALL render in the editorial-draft state (RFC7-20), machine-marked with the operator-declared model identity and version as its inference provenance. A narrative page (the landing page, a Pole's page, a section or a deep dive) SHALL open in a reading view. In the reading view Syzygy SHALL write no label token for a claim block (Inferred, Observed, Unknown, or a reason code) and no file-and-line aside into the block's visible text, which means the block's `innerText` once its clipped elements, those carrying the renderer's visually-hidden class, are removed from a clone; the agent's own prose is not examined for those words. A block carrying a kernel label keeps it visible in both views (REQ-polaris-generation-012). Other pages (the appendix, the review page, the understanding record, the discovery account, source pages) keep their labels. Each narrative page SHALL carry once a Syzygy-written legend: a numbered mark cites a source; text with such a mark is the agent's reading of that source; the mark `judgement` is the agent's own assessment; the mark `framing` makes no claim; a set-off quotation is verified against the repository; `Not established` introduces an Unknown block, whose reason follows in words; and `†` with a number on a citation counts the lines of that citation's file that screening withheld. A citation mark is keyboard-focusable and shows its file and line on hover and on focus. A claim resting on execution keeps its commands beside it in both views. An evidence view, reached from every narrative page by a control that works with scripts off, SHALL show for every block each distinction RFC7-33 lists that the block has, among them its label, tier, reason, freshness and claim-block kind, and whether it is an uncited judgement. In both views, those same values SHALL stay in the block's machine attributes and in DOM text (`textContent`) that plain-text extraction and an assistive technology read, in the reading view clipped by that class, never by `hidden` or `display:none`, never in colour, hover or an attribute alone (RFC7-33, RFC7-34). Generated prose stays visually distinct as a draft (RFC7-20) through the one sentence and the legend, with the pinned revision as its stated inputs. A list carrying the draft's `repeatsDiagram` field, which names a diagram in its section, SHALL render collapsed by default, and each of its items remains a claim block for every rule of this requirement. For a run in the operator-agent mode this requirement reads REQ-polaris-generation-003's sentence "The asset bundle SHALL distinguish source claims, supported inference and non-normative framing and preserve captured epistemic states." as taking in one further class, an uncited judgement, which the bundle marks in a machine attribute and never as supported inference; it carries no anchor and asserts no project fact, so neither that requirement's "Anchored claims SHALL have sufficient, minimal source support" nor its scenario "Unsupported or surplus anchor" reaches it. Every other rendering obligation of REQ-polaris-generation-003, and those of 004 and 012, and of REQ-polaris-generation-032 where it is adopted and applies, are unchanged.

A draft MAY present its argument with presentation elements (owner direction `OWNER-DIRECTION-DOSSIER-RICH-ELEMENTS-2026-11-04`), in a section or deep dive: a table, a callout, an ordered list of steps, a list of definitions, a set of cards, a collapsible section and a code excerpt. Every claim an element carries (a table cell, a block inside a callout or a collapsible section, a step, a definition, a card's body) SHALL be a claim block for every rule of this requirement and of REQ-polaris-generation-035: it carries its own identity, label, citations and quotations; Syzygy checks it and reports its findings as for any claim block; the fidelity verdict carries an entry for it; and it renders with its label's marking. The fidelity review SHALL judge a table cell's claim as read with its row and column labels. A table cell MAY instead be empty: an empty cell states nothing, carries no label, citation or quotation, and SHALL render with the visible marker "-" (U+002D), never as a blank. This marker means only that the cell states nothing in this dossier, never an absence or a negative about the subject; an absence or a negative the draft means to state SHALL be written as a claim in that cell, Inferred with its citations or Unknown with its reason.

An element's short labels (a table caption, a column heading, a callout title, a defined term, a card label, a collapsible section's summary, a code excerpt's language and, since version 2.16, a subsection's title) are presentation: each is plain text of at most 120 Unicode code points that names what it heads; it SHALL carry no citation and no quotation, SHALL state no fact about the subject, carries no epistemic label of its own and SHALL NOT render as Observed. Syzygy SHALL report a finding for a short label longer than 120 Unicode code points or one that carries a citation, a quotation or markup (a `<`, `&` or backtick character, or a Markdown link, image or paired emphasis mark); that a label states no fact is the agent's report, open to the fidelity and rendered-design reviews. A callout's tone and the order of steps are also presentation within that stated review residual: neither may introduce an unlabelled fact, and the reviews judge them with the claims as presented. An agent-supplied language value is a presentation hint, never a verified fact about the file; when it is absent, Syzygy SHALL derive a language label from the path where one can be determined.

A code excerpt carries no text of the agent's: it SHALL name one citation of 1 to 60 lines, and Syzygy SHALL report a repair finding for a citation of more than 60 lines. The path-and-range check above applies to that citation, including its no-read exception. Syzygy SHALL render admitted lines from the blob it read and verified at the pinned revision, byte for byte as text using HTML text escaping, so characters such as `<` and `&` and a sequence such as `</pre>` cannot become markup. The excerpt is a quotation Syzygy has verified, labelled Observed, with an anchor in the form above whose fragment is the lines' byte range. For a blob that classification or screening excludes, or whose bytes are not valid UTF-8, the excerpt SHALL instead render Unknown with `excluded-content`, carrying none of the blob's bytes, and its exclusion is not a repair finding. If a withheld excerpt displays its submitted line range, the range SHALL be disclosed within that Unknown presentation as agent-supplied and unverified, never as a Syzygy-verified range or the blob's length.

Syzygy SHALL render every element through the same renderer as static HTML whose carried text is inert, in the HTML element whose meaning matches it (a table whose header cells are scoped to their row or column, an aside, an ordered list, a description list, a list whose items are each headed by their label, a details element with its summary, preformatted code), with the pages' script rule below; a callout's tone SHALL be stated in its words, never by colour alone; and no fragment target SHALL sit inside a collapsible section. A collapsible section's summary SHALL show the count of Unknown claims it contains even while collapsed; that count is a derived disclosure alongside the agent-supplied summary, not an agent-supplied short label. An element adds no read, no source and no label beyond those of the claims it carries and the excerpt Syzygy reads.

A page SHALL carry no script and the pages' content security policy SHALL stay as it was at version 1.4, unless the run's bound committed screening policy recognizes the exact version7 script renderCondition or its equally restrictive successor. With that recognized condition, a page MAY carry only the script and content security policy that condition describes; a policy identity, version or digest mismatch refuses under033, without an owner-act gate. That script adds behaviour only: no text, claim, marking or link SHALL be reachable only through it, and every page SHALL read and navigate completely with scripts off.

A draft in schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v4` MAY divide its sections into Poles: at most the run's declared Pole limit, each Pole with a short label under the rules for an element's short label and a section list. Unlike the run limits above, the Pole limit has a default, five; a declared limit that is not a positive integer SHALL be refused. The brief SHALL ask for Poles chosen from what the repository holds, each holding a substantial share of its documentation, and the rendered-design review judges that. Syzygy SHALL report a repair finding for a draft over the limit, for a section in no Pole or in two, for a Pole with no section, and for a Pole draft whose `whySection` field names no section; whether that section answers why the project exists is a review judgement. Syzygy SHALL render one page per Pole and a landing page holding the title, that section and a map of the Poles, the why section's own Pole linking to it, not repeating it, and a Pole holding only that section having no page of its own; a draft with no Poles keeps the page division of a schema-v3 draft, and every other rule of this version applies to it. A Pole is a page boundary and carries no claim; it adds no label, source or read, and every claim of its sections keeps every rule above, its citations, numbering and glossary links holding across pages. Under the owner's sign-off of this version a dossier's primary narrative MAY order its altitudes as the landing page, then the Pole pages, then deep dives and exact sources, the owner so ruling for RFC7-13's V0 default and REQ-polaris-generation-004; each altitude stays a true coarser account and the verbatim leaf stays reachable.

A draft in schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v5` is a schema-v4 draft in every respect but one: each diagram carries, in place of `sectionId`, a required `home` member naming the one place its figure belongs, as exactly one of `{"kind": "section", "target": <a section's identity>}`, `{"kind": "deep-dive", "target": <a deep dive's identity>}` and `{"kind": "overview"}`. A home is one member with one kind, so a draft cannot name two. Its key is `target`, never `id`, because every `id` key in a draft is an identity that must be unique. Every rule this requirement and REQ-polaris-generation-035 state for a schema-v4 draft holds for a schema-v5 draft; where a rule speaks of a diagram's section, a v5 diagram homed in a section is in that section, one homed in a deep dive is in that deep dive, and one homed on the overview is in no section. Syzygy SHALL report a repair finding of kind `diagram-home`, located within that diagram (`$.diagrams[<index>]` or below it), for a v5 diagram whose `home` is absent, is not an object, has a `kind` other than those three, lacks the `target` its kind requires, carries a `target` with the kind `overview`, or carries any other member, and for one that carries `sectionId` beside `home`; a finding of kind `unresolved-reference` for one whose `target` names no section or no deep dive of the draft; and a finding of kind `field-version` for a `home` in a draft of schema v4 or earlier. Each such finding names the diagram's identity, or names `$.diagrams[<index>]` where the diagram has no well-formed identity; the generic schema check may add its own. A v5 list carrying `repeatsDiagram` repeats a diagram of its own section when that diagram is homed in the list's section or, for a list in a deep dive, in that deep dive or in the deep dive's section; no list repeats a figure homed on the overview, and a section's list does not repeat a figure homed in one of its deep dives. Syzygy SHALL render each v5 figure on exactly one page and on no other: a section's figure where a schema-v4 figure of that section renders; a deep dive's figure leading that deep dive's page, as a section's figures lead the section, after the deep dive's heading and any run-stopped banner and before its first paragraph; a figure homed on a deep dive that has no page of its own (its disposition is not `produced`, or the draft layer is withheld) where a figure of that deep dive's section renders instead; and the overview's figure on the landing page, `index.html`, the first page a reader sees, after the page's header (its title, any run-stopped banner and the introduction) and before its sections, the Pole map and the list of sections not generated, in a region of its own at reading level 1 that carries no topics; from version 2.20 that region of a schema-v10 draft opens with the draft's hero, or its declared omission, as the hero paragraphs below state. Where this requirement and the draft-layer rules render no part of the draft on that page (a withheld draft layer, a section not generated or not produced), the figure renders nowhere, as a schema-v4 figure of that section does. A v5 diagram whose own disposition is not `produced`, or that cannot be drawn inertly, renders the notice a schema-v4 diagram renders in its place, where its figure would stand. Each figure carries the same caption, relationship line, inert drawing, legend, tally, list of every component and relationship, and declarative source as a section figure, keeps every rule of this requirement for a diagram's nodes and edges, and each of its nodes and edges appears once in the machine form. A figure renders in a box of the same width on every page that holds one. The brief, issued as `polaris-dossier-brief-v10` with readability guidance `polaris-dossier-readability-v5`, from version 2.4 as `polaris-dossier-brief-v11`, from version 2.5 as `polaris-dossier-brief-v12`, from version 2.15 as `polaris-dossier-brief-v13`, from version 2.16 as `polaris-dossier-brief-v14`, from version 2.19 as `polaris-dossier-brief-v15`, from version 2.20 as `polaris-dossier-brief-v16`, from version 2.23 as `polaris-dossier-brief-v17`, from version 2.24 as `polaris-dossier-brief-v18`, from version 2.26 as `polaris-dossier-brief-v19`, from version 2.29 as `polaris-dossier-brief-v20`, from version 2.32 as `polaris-dossier-brief-v21`, from version 2.33 as `polaris-dossier-brief-v22`, from version 2.35 as `polaris-dossier-brief-v23` and from version 2.40 as `polaris-dossier-brief-v24`, each with the same readability guidance, SHALL state that a v5 diagram names its home and SHALL name the three kinds and the `target` key; brief v10 issues schema v5, brief v11 issues schema v6, brief v12 issues schema v7, brief v13 issues schema v7, brief v14 issues schema v8, brief v15 issues schema v9, brief v16 issues schema v10, brief v17 issues schema v11, brief v18 issues schema v12, briefs v19, v20 and v21 issue schema v13, brief v22 issues schema v14, brief v23 issues schema v15 and brief v24 issues schema v16 (below), and a draft in v2, v3, v4, v5, v6, v7, v8, v9, v10, v11, v12, v13, v14, v15 or v16 is accepted. A draft in schema v4 or earlier SHALL be checked and rendered exactly as before version 2.2, the same findings and the same bytes on every page, except that a `home` on one of its diagrams is a `field-version` finding, and except for the additions of version 2.3 that the derived-structure paragraphs below state, which every operator-agent check and rendering carries whatever its draft's schema: the record `derived/rev-<n>.json` in the run directory, the `derived-structure` region on `index.html`, `data-visual-provenance="curated"` on each figure, the file `derived-structure.json`, the `derivedStructure` member of `machine.json`, and the size report's measures of the pages so changed; and except for the additions of version 2.15 that the finding-pages paragraphs below state, which every operator-agent rendering likewise carries whatever its draft's schema, and the `field-version` finding they state for a `taskRoutes` member; and except for the additions of version 2.4, the `readingLedger` member of its check record and a `field-version` finding for a `thesisBasis` member; and except for the addition of version 2.5, the `field-version` findings it states, in place of the `schema` findings at the same paths, for a diagram whose kind is `sequence` or `swimlane` or that carries `participants`, `messages`, `lanes` or `steps`; and except for the addition of version 2.12, a `field-version` finding for an `inventoryOmissions` member. A draft in schema v5 SHALL be checked and rendered exactly as version 2.3 checks and renders it, except for those same two additions of version 2.4, that addition of version 2.5 and those additions of version 2.15, and except that from version 2.12 it MAY carry the `inventoryOmissions` member the omission-record paragraph below states and from version 2.15 `taskRoutes` as the task-routes paragraph below states; without the `inventoryOmissions` member its findings and rendered bytes are those of version 2.11 apart from the additions of version 2.15. The `inventoryCoverageAdvice` member of version 2.12 is on check's report alone, whatever the draft's schema, and is no finding and no rendered byte. The provider mode is unaffected.

`syzygy dossier check` SHALL compute figure-fit advice for every draft that parses. For each diagram whose disposition is `produced`, at each of two viewports, 1440 and 390 CSS pixels wide, it SHALL compute without a browser, from the pure layout the renderer draws that figure with: the column width C, the width the dossier's pages give a figure's box at that viewport with the page's navigation in its default, open state, which at this version is 966 px at 1440 and 261 px at 390; the laid-out width W, the drawing's own width; the drawn width D, the width the pages' stylesheet draws the drawing at in that box; and the effective text size T = 13 px x D / W. At this version the stylesheet draws a figure at its laid-out width and lets a wider one scroll sideways in its box, except that it lets a figure whose laid-out width W is exactly 1048 px (three columns at the readable spacing; since version 2.11 the readable layout lays out no figure that wide, as stated below, so on a page this exception matches no figure, and in figure fit, for a graph within the draft schema's bounds, it applies only where check is supplied another layout) shrink to its box but to no less than 860 px, so D = W, and where W = 1048 px, D = min(W, max(C, 860 px)); a sequence or swimlane figure (version 2.5) is drawn, and its fit taken, as the figure-kind paragraphs below state. 13 px is the size the stylesheet sets for a figure's node and edge text, so T is the size that text is drawn at on the screen once the drawing is scaled, and is 13 px wherever the drawing is not scaled. The minimum text size is 11 px. A figure does not fit at a viewport when D > C (it scrolls sideways in its box) or when T < 11 px (its text is drawn smaller than the minimum). The figures examined are the draft's diagrams whose disposition is `produced`. The advice SHALL hold one entry for each figure and viewport at which the figure does not fit, `{"diagram": <identity>, "viewportPx": <1440 or 390>, "laidOutPx": W, "drawnPx": D, "columnPx": C, "textPx": T, "minTextPx": 11, "failed": [...]}`, where `failed` lists `width`, `text-size` or both in that order and T is rounded half away from zero to two decimal places, the comparison using T unrounded; it SHALL hold no entry for a figure and viewport that fits. A figure whose layout cannot be computed is examined and SHALL be listed once, in its place in the diagram order, as `{"diagram": <identity>, "unknown": <reason>}` with no numbers: Unknown, never fitting. The advice is Inferred, a computation over the stated stylesheet and never an observation of a browser; it gates nothing, is no repair finding and changes no outcome. Unlike check's other advice, which is on its report only (the reading ledger below stores only counts of it), the advice SHALL also be written into the check record that check writes once for the revision, `checks/rev-<n>.json`, and into no other check record (an inventory check record carries none), as its member `figureFit`: `{"textPx": 13, "minTextPx": 11, "viewports": [{"viewportPx": 1440, "columnPx": 966}, {"viewportPx": 390, "columnPx": 261}], "examined": <count>, "entries": [...]}`, its entries in the draft's diagram order and then the viewport order 1440, 390. The record's format identifier stays `polaris-dossier-check/1`. The member SHALL depend only on the draft's bytes and those constants, so two checks of the same draft bytes write byte-identical `figureFit` members, and a later check can be compared with an earlier one. A check record without `figureFit`, written before this version or for a draft that does not parse, states nothing about fit: its absence is Unknown, and no reader SHALL treat it as every figure fitting. These constants are this version's; a stylesheet change that alters a column width, the scaling rule or the text size is a change to this requirement.

In the operator-agent mode Syzygy SHALL render on the landing page one figure that it computes itself from the pinned tree, and SHALL render the elements of that figure as derived structural facts, labelled Observed where their currency bound holds (owner decision `pursuit-2026-11-21-observed-derived`, option A, recorded 2026-11-21T03:53:58+00:00, which reads trust-and-evidence.md's "a deterministic claim with a resolvable evidence link" as covering such a fact and amends no doctrine). A derived structural fact states what one declared, versioned, deterministic rule matched in the paths and modes of the pinned revision's tree entries, and no more: it states no interpretation, purpose, quality or runtime behaviour, and no rule reads a file body, a size or an object identifier (the declared manifest facts of version 2.7, below, are not derived structural facts: their recognizers read admitted bodies, and no page renders them but the unit map and the CI pipeline map of version 2.9, below, which label them Inferred). At this version the one rule whose facts render is `top-level-unit`, version 1. Over the entries of the pinned tree, it leaves out every entry whose mode is not a blob mode (100644, 100755 or 120000), a submodule (160000) among them, and every blob entry whose path, or any of whose directory prefixes, a secret detector of the bound screening policy matches, read as a path or as text; such a blob entry is counted once, in the record's `secretPathsLeftOut`, and named nowhere. Of the blob entries left, one whose path has one segment is a file at the root, and one whose path has two or more segments belongs to the top-level directory its first segment names, whatever other rule excludes or withholds its blob: a withheld path that the policy permits naming may be named (as this requirement already allows), so a directory whose files are all withheld on a ground other than a secret detector is still a fact. Its facts are: `tree-root`, the repository root (path `.`) with its count of files at the root; `top-level-unit`, one top-level directory (its path) with its count of files under it; and `contains`, that the root contains that top-level directory. A count is a count of tree entries. No fact is computed from, or states, any blob's size or object identifier, so for a private repository no fact derives from the size or object identifier of a blob excluded or withheld on any ground (the declared-source policy's code-structure rule), and neither the record nor any page carries one.

`syzygy dossier check`, for each draft revision it checks and writes a check record for, SHALL derive the facts from the tree it lists at the pinned revision (the listing it takes for its census) and SHALL keep them as an evidence artifact, written once beside that check record: the file `derived/rev-<n>.json` in the run directory, `<n>` being the draft revision; an inventory check writes none. It is JSON with two-space indentation and one trailing newline, its members in this order: `format`, `polaris-dossier-derived-structure/1`; `repositoryId`, the run's repository identity; `revision`, the full pinned revision; `capturedAt`, the instant that check listed the tree, as a UTC ISO 8601 string with milliseconds; `scope`, the fixed text `tree-metadata: the paths and modes of the pinned tree's entries; no file body, size or object id`; `producer`, `syzygy dossier check`; `rules`, one `{"id", "version"}` per rule whose facts it holds; `state`, `derived` or `unknown`; and, where `derived`, `files` (the tree's blob entries, secret-matching paths included), `secretPathsLeftOut`, `submodules`, `facts` and `unitsNotListed`, or, where `unknown`, `why`, the text `the pinned tree could not be listed (<reason>)` with the object reader's refusal reason, and no facts. Each fact is `{"id", "fact", "rule", "ruleVersion", "path", ...}`: `{"id": "_tree-root", "fact": "tree-root", "rule": "top-level-unit", "ruleVersion": 1, "path": ".", "rootFiles": <count>}`; `{"id": "_tree-unit-<k>", "fact": "top-level-unit", ..., "path": <directory>, "files": <count>}`; and `{"id": "_tree-contains-<k>", "fact": "contains", ..., "path": <directory>, "from": "_tree-root", "to": "_tree-unit-<k>"}`; in the order the root, then every unit, then every containment, units and containments ordered by path and `<k>` counting from 1 in that order. A directory's `path` is the raw first path segment, escaped only as JSON escapes a string, and "ordered by path" means by that raw segment in code-unit order. An identity starting with `_` cannot be a draft identity. At most 250 directories are listed, the first 250 in that order; the rest are counted in `unitsNotListed`. The record's identity is `sha256:` followed by the lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 of the file's exact bytes, which anyone holding the file can recompute; the record names its source (the repository and revision), capture time, scope and provenance (producer and rules). Each render of that draft revision SHALL publish the record's bytes unchanged as `derived-structure.json` at the root of the rendered site, a path no page of a draft may take, and SHALL re-derive the facts from the tree it lists at the pinned revision through the object reader with which it reads and verifies blobs, never trusting the stored facts unchecked. Two renders of one draft revision therefore publish the same record and draw the same region, and a render that changes nothing else does not retire a rendered-design review; two checks of the same draft bytes write records that differ only in `capturedAt`. The machine view, `machine.json`, SHALL carry a member `derivedStructure`, beside and outside its `reviewStatus` member: `{"file", "sha256", "revision", "state", "currencyPolicy", "facts"}`, where `file` is `derived-structure.json` and `sha256` its hexadecimal digest, or both null where no record was published; `revision` is the render's pinned revision; `state` is the record's `state`, or, where the draft revision has no readable record, `missing` where no entry stands at its path and `unusable` where one does (version 2.10); `currencyPolicy` is `{"policyId", "policyVersion", "sha256"}` of the applied currency policy, or null where none applies; and `facts` holds one `{"id", "fact", "epistemic", "unknownReason", "rule", "ruleVersion", "path"}` per fact of the record, in its order, `epistemic` being `observed` or `unknown` and `unknownReason` null for an Observed fact and otherwise `no-currency-bound-declared` or `stale-beyond-currency-bound`.

The derived-fact class `polaris-dossier-derived-tree-metadata` declares its currency bound, per rule and version, in the policy `POLARIS-DOSSIER-DERIVED-FACT-CURRENCY-POLICY-v1`, version 1.0.0, at `.syzygy/governance/policies/POLARIS-DOSSIER-DERIVED-FACT-CURRENCY-POLICY-v1.json`, which declares `pinned-revision` for `top-level-unit` version 1 and nothing for any other rule or version. A render SHALL read that file as it reads the bound screening policy, the committed bytes at the Syzygy checkout's own HEAD, never the observed clone, refusing uncommitted bytes, and SHALL apply its declarations only while those bytes' SHA-256 is `95a3aa132041aa5dd8f860e8ce4c92b3e3c3380ebef4b1ada19bee6b8f50865e`, the bytes adopted with this version; bytes that are missing, uncommitted, unreadable, unparseable or of another digest declare no bound, and `currencyPolicy` is then null. Under `pinned-revision` a fact is current at a render exactly when its record's `revision` equals that render's pinned revision and the facts that render re-derives equal the record's; no wall-clock bound applies, since the tree of a pinned commit cannot change. A fact whose rule and version have no declared bound SHALL render Unknown with `no-currency-bound-declared`; otherwise a fact of a record that is not current SHALL render Unknown with `stale-beyond-currency-bound`; neither is ever drawn solid or labelled Observed. The run directory lies within the agent sessions' write reach, so a render SHALL take a record as readable only where every fact's `rule` and `ruleVersion` are `top-level-unit` and 1, the rule and version that render's own re-derivation uses, and every path its `top-level-unit` and `contains` facts name is the path of a `top-level-unit` fact among the facts that render re-derives from the tree it lists; a record naming any other rule, version or path (a directory the tree does not hold, or one past the render's own listing of 250) is no readable record, and nothing of it is drawn, listed, carried in `machine.json` or published, so every path and rule the region names, Observed or Unknown, is one the render itself derived at the pinned revision. A render that cannot list the tree states `source-uncaptured-or-unreachable`, below, whatever the record names. A fact of a readable record that is not current keeps the record's count, labelled Unknown with `stale-beyond-currency-bound`. Where the draft revision has no readable record, or its record's `state` is `unknown`, or the render cannot list the tree, the region SHALL hold no figure and no list, only its heading and the sentence `Not established. The top-level directories of the tree at revision <full revision> are Unknown at this render: <words>.`, carrying `data-epistemic="unknown"` and `data-unknown-reason`, with the reason and words `missing-evidence`, `no derived record was kept when this draft revision was checked`, where the draft revision's `derived/rev-<n>.json` holds no entry, which is so exactly where examining that path without following a link reports that nothing is there (no such entry, or a parent that is not a directory); `missing-evidence`, `this draft revision's derived-record path holds something this render cannot use or cannot examine; Syzygy or its screen may have changed since the revision was checked, or the path may have been altered`, where examining the path reports anything else, whether an entry that is no readable record or an error that leaves it unknown whether one is there (version 2.10); or `source-uncaptured-or-unreachable`, `the pinned tree could not be listed`; and no record is published. The render reads that path as a reader of a check record reads one (below), under the derived record's own bound of 4,194,304 bytes: it opens the path once without blocking, refuses without reading it anything whose opened descriptor is not a regular file or gives a size past that bound, and reads at most one byte more than the bound. The render decides in this order: no entry at that path; an entry that is not, in form, a record check writes (it is not a regular file, is past its bound, cannot be read or decoded, does not parse, is not exactly what check writes, or names a path the screen's secret test matches), or a path it cannot examine, which take the version 2.10 words; a record whose `state` is `unknown`, or a tree the render cannot list, which take `source-uncaptured-or-unreachable`; and a record naming a rule, version or path the render does not derive, which takes the version 2.10 words. Before version 2.10 every entry that was no readable record took the words for no entry, which are untrue of a record check wrote and a later screen or Syzygy version cannot use.

The figure SHALL render on `index.html`, in a region of its own, `<section id="derived-structure" data-reading-level="1" data-topics="">`, at the end of the page's main content: after the sections, the Pole map and the list of sections not generated, or, where the draft layer is withheld, after the page's header. The region's heading reads `Derived by Syzygy: top-level directories`. Its figure is `<figure class="derived-figure" data-visual-provenance="computed" data-derived-record="sha256:<hex>">`, carries no hue and no text of the draft, and holds, in this order: the caption `Derived by Syzygy from the pinned tree`; the note `Derived by Syzygy from the paths and modes of the tree at revision <full revision>, listed at <capturedAt>, by rule top-level-unit version 1. No file body, size or object id was read. Current for that revision only.`, which names the figure's evaluation, its snapshot and as-of instant, on the surface (RFC9-38); an inert drawing, made by the renderer's pure layout as a curated figure's is, of a box `Repository root` and a box `<path>/ (<count> files)` for each of the at most twelve of the record's `top-level-unit` facts with the most files, ties ordered by path, with one `contains` arrow from the root to each drawn directory, each drawn box and arrow carrying the `data-derived-id` of its fact, an Observed element drawn solid and an Unknown one dotted with its label ending "?"; where the record has more `top-level-unit` facts, the note `<n> more top-level directories are listed below and not drawn.`, and where its `unitsNotListed` is not zero, the note `<m> more top-level directories are counted and not listed.`; a legend that lists only the line styles the drawing uses, as an operator-agent figure's legend does; the tally `<b> boxes and <a> arrows, derived by Syzygy: <o> observed, 0 inferred, <u> unknown.`, counting the drawn elements; and, collapsed under the summary `Read every derived fact`, a list with one entry per fact of the record, in its order. In these strings a count of one takes the singular: `1 file`, `1 box`, `1 arrow`, and `1 more top-level directory is listed below and not drawn.` and `1 more top-level directory is counted and not listed.` Each entry is a list item carrying `data-derived-id`, `data-derived-fact`, `data-epistemic` (`observed` or `unknown`), `data-unknown-reason` where Unknown, `data-derived-rule="top-level-unit"`, `data-derived-rule-version="1"`, `data-derived-path` (the fact's path), `data-derived-record="sha256:<hex>"` and `data-derived-revision` (the full revision). Its visible text is the fact in Syzygy's words, `Repository root: <n> files at the root.`, `<path>/: <n> files.` or `Repository root contains <path>/.`, then `Derived by Syzygy`; and its DOM text, clipped in the reading view by the renderer's visually-hidden class and shown in the evidence view, is `Evidence: label Observed; derived by Syzygy by rule top-level-unit version 1; source path <path>; record sha256:<hex>; current for revision <full revision>.`, or, for an Unknown fact, `Evidence: label Unknown; reason <reason>; derived by Syzygy by rule top-level-unit version 1; source path <path>; record sha256:<hex>.` Every path renders as HTML-escaped text, as all text does. The list is the figure's text equivalent (RFC7-34). As an embedded computed visual (RFC7-28) the figure is deterministic, one record giving the same drawing bytes, and its legend says only what its drawing uses.

Every operator-agent figure drawn from the draft SHALL carry `data-visual-provenance="curated"` on its `<figure>` element, and nothing else in it changes at that version; from version 2.15 it also carries its fragment identity, as the finding-pages paragraphs below state. No element of a draft is drawn solid or rendered Observed because of a derived fact: at this version a draft's box or arrow that matches a derived fact keeps the draft's marking, and is never upgraded, so a curated figure cannot imitate the computed one. Since version 2.19 one exception stands, which rule `relation-witness` below states: an arrow of a schema-v9 draft that a fact of its identical relation type, rendering Observed, joins between the same two units is drawn solid and Observed, in Syzygy's words and with that fact's provenance, in a figure that keeps `data-visual-provenance="curated"`; no box of a draft is ever upgraded, and an arrow of a draft of any earlier schema keeps the draft's marking. Since version 2.23 the nesting of a containment figure of a schema-v11 draft is read by the same rule, and a witnessed nesting is stated in Syzygy's words in the figure's list and machine form alone, its two boxes keeping the draft's marking. Since version 2.24 a derived relationship of a schema-v12 draft's model is no claim of the draft but its request that Syzygy state a relation: it renders Observed, in Syzygy's words, only where the same rule witnesses it, and Unknown, in Syzygy's words, otherwise, and no element of the model is upgraded. Nor can it imitate a computed map of version 2.9: no draft member reaches a map page, a map's figures or `computedMaps`, and no figure of a draft renders on a map page. Since version 2.35 a view of a schema-v15 draft MAY start from the directory map or the unit map as an embedded computed visual (RFC7-28): it names the map and paths of its elements, read-only, draws the elements it shows in Syzygy's words with the map's mark, identity and computed provenance, counts on the figure every element it leaves out, and never adds, alters, relabels, re-marks or suppresses a derived fact; the paragraphs of that version state it. Apart from that read-only reading, a draft cannot name, place, alter, suppress or add to the derived figure or its record: no draft member reaches them, a draft element labelled Observed is a finding as any block's is, and any other unknown member is a schema finding. Derived facts are not claim blocks of the draft: they carry no anchor, take no entry in the fidelity verdict and enter none of the draft's counts (the one sentence's, the appendix's); the region's tally and `derivedStructure` count them. The provider mode is unaffected: its pages, their bytes and its machine form carry no derived region, record, member or provenance attribute.

`syzygy dossier check` SHALL also keep a reading ledger for every draft that parses, written into the check record that check writes once for the revision, `checks/rev-<n>.json`, and into no other check record (an inventory check record carries none), as its member `readingLedger`, beside `figureFit`; the record's format identifier stays `polaris-dossier-check/1`. The ledger is a count over the draft, never a measurement of a rendered page or of a browser, and it adds no measure beyond the word count the RS-19 reader advice already takes, the reader advisories, the figures the draft places and the figure-fit advice above. Its pages are the narrative pages the renderer lays the draft out on under this requirement, computed from the draft alone as though its draft layer renders: `index.html`, holding the introduction, every figure homed on the overview, and every section whose disposition is `produced` when the draft has no `poles`, or only its `whySection` when it has; `poles/<k>.html` for the k-th Pole in the draft's order, counting every Pole from 1, holding each produced section that Pole lists other than the why section, except that a Pole listing no section other than the why section has no page; and `deep-dives/<name>.html` for each deep dive whose disposition is `produced`, `<name>` being its identity with every code point outside `A-Z`, `a-z`, `0-9`, `_`, `.` and `-` replaced by one `_`, the rule by which the renderer names that deep dive's page: a character outside the Basic Multilingual Plane, written as two UTF-16 code units, is one code point and gives one `_`, and so does a lone surrogate; deep dives whose names give one path share one page entry, their units listed together. A section two Poles list is placed by the first. A section whose disposition is not `produced` is on no page, and neither is its own prose; a deep dive whose disposition is not `produced` has no page and its own prose is on none; a produced section these rules place on no page (one no Pole lists) is an unplaced unit. A figure is a diagram whose disposition is `produced`; it is placed on the page of its home in a draft of schema v5 or later, a figure homed on a deep dive with no page being placed on the page of that deep dive's section, as version 2.2 renders it, and on the page of its `sectionId` section in an earlier draft; a figure whose section is not produced or is unplaced is on no page. The figure Syzygy derives (the `derived-structure` region) is not the draft's and is not counted.

The member is `{"format": "polaris-dossier-reading-ledger/1", "wordsPerMinute": 200, "pages": [...], "draft": {...}}`. Each page entry is `{"page", "units", "proseWords", "readingMinutes", "figures", "advisories", "figureFit"}`, in the order `index.html`, then the Pole pages in Pole order, then the deep-dive pages in the draft's deep-dive order: `page` is its path as above; `units` the draft parts it holds, in the draft's order, written `introduction`, `section:<id>` and `deep-dive:<id>`; `proseWords` the words of the own prose of each unit it holds, counted as the RS-19 advice counts a section's (a plain block's `text` and each of its children's, split at every run of white space with empty strings dropped; a presentation element counts none; since version 2.16, in a schema-v8 draft only, a section's own prose takes in each of its subsections' answer `text` and plain blocks, for this count and for the RS-19 advice alike), the introduction's `text` counted the same way; `readingMinutes` that count divided by `wordsPerMinute` and rounded up to a whole number, so 0 words read in 0 minutes; `figures` the count of figures on it; `advisories` `{"RS-13", "RS-14", "RS-16", "RS-17", "RS-19"}`, for each standard the count of the check's reader advisories located on that page, an advisory whose JSON path begins `$.sections[<i>]`, `$.deepDives[<i>]` or `$.diagrams[<i>]` being located at that section, deep dive or diagram and any other being on no page; and `figureFit` `{"notFittingAt1440", "notFittingAt390", "unknown"}`, the count of the figure-fit advice's entries for its figures at each viewport and of its Unknown entries. `draft` is `{"proseWords", "readingMinutes", "figures", "advisories", "figureFit", "unplacedUnits"}` over the whole draft, each thing counted there exactly once whether or not a page holds it: `proseWords` the words of the introduction and of every produced section and produced deep dive, unplaced ones included; `figures` every figure; `advisories` every advisory of the check, a page's count including one only when the section, deep dive or diagram it is located at is on that page (so RS-14's, at `$.deepDives`, and one located at a section or diagram on no page count in `draft` alone); `figureFit` every entry of the figure-fit advice; `readingMinutes` computed from its own `proseWords`; and `unplacedUnits` the count of unplaced units. Every count is a non-negative integer, and the ledger reads the parsed draft as the reader advice does, a part that is not of the expected form counting nothing. The member SHALL depend only on the draft's bytes and the constants this requirement states, so two checks of the same draft bytes write byte-identical `readingLedger` members. The advisories themselves stay on check's report only; the ledger stores their counts. Like the figure-fit advice, the ledger is Inferred, gates nothing, is no repair finding and changes no outcome; a check record without `readingLedger`, written before this version or for a draft that does not parse, states nothing about reading, and its absence is Unknown.

Check's report SHALL carry the ledger it wrote and, as `readingDifference`, the ledger's difference from the previous run of the same repository, computed at that check and never written to any file. The previous run is found in the state root that holds the run directory: among the other run directories there (a directory whose name has the run-identity form `run-<32 hex>` and whose `run.json` reads back as a valid run record), those whose recorded repository identity, `subject.repository.repositoryId`, equals this run's, compared exactly, and never by directory name or URL. Such a run's candidate ledger is the `readingLedger` of its highest-numbered check record in `checks/` that parses, carries a `readingLedger` of this format and has a `checkedAt` earlier than this check's, and the previous run is the one whose candidate ledger's check record has the latest `checkedAt`, a tie going to the greatest run identity in code-unit order. A run-form directory whose `run.json` cannot be read or does not read back as valid, and a check record of a same-repository run that cannot be read or does not parse, is skipped and counted, in `unreadableRuns` and `unreadableCheckRecords`, members of `readingDifference` in either state, so an absence is never claimed over what could not be read; the state root is listed once, and a listing that fails is its own case below. Where there is one, `readingDifference` is `{"state": "compared", "against": {"runId", "revision", "pinnedRevision", "checkedAt"}, "pages": [...], "draft": {"before", "after", "delta"}, "unreadableRuns", "unreadableCheckRecords"}`, `against` naming that check record's run, draft revision, pinned revision and check time. `pages` holds one entry per page path of either ledger, this ledger's in its order and then the previous ledger's other pages in its order, each `{"page", "change", "before", "after", "delta"}`: `before` and `after` are the two ledgers' numbers for that page as `{"proseWords", "readingMinutes", "figures", "advisories", "figureFit"}`, `delta` is after minus before, member by member, and `change` is `unchanged` where every number is equal, `changed` where one differs, `added` for a page only this ledger has (`before` and `delta` null) and `removed` for a page only the previous one has (`after` and `delta` null); `draft` compares the two `draft` entries the same way, `unplacedUnits` included. Where there is none, where this draft does not parse, or where the record this check wrote is the bounded form of version 2.10, `readingDifference` is `{"state": "unknown", "why": <words>, "unreadableRuns", "unreadableCheckRecords"}` with the words `no earlier run of this repository in the state root holds a reading ledger that could be read`, `the state root could not be listed`, `this draft does not parse, so it has no reading ledger` or, for a bounded form, `this check's record was written in its bounded form, which keeps no reading ledger` (version 2.11), and no ledger number; in the last two cases the state root is not listed and both counts are 0, and where this draft does not parse and the record this check wrote is the bounded form, the bounded form's words are given, never the words for a draft that does not parse (version 2.14): a first run's difference is Unknown, never zero. No run of another repository is compared. A predecessor's check record lies within the agent sessions' write reach, as this run's does, so the difference is Inferred.

In the operator-agent mode the technology dimensions the brief asks a dossier to cover come from shape packs. A shape pack is a versioned JSON document, implementation data under `packages/polaris-dossier/src/shape-packs/` and never authority, of format `polaris-dossier-shape-pack/1`, its members in this order: `format`; `id`; `version`, a positive integer that moves whenever any other byte of the pack changes; `selectedBy`, null for the default pack or `{"trait": <trait id>}`; and `dimensions`, a list of `{"id", "order", "lines"}`, `lines` being that dimension's bullet as the Markdown lines of the reader standards' technology-choices standard (RS-11). A pack's identity is `<id>@<version>` and its digest the SHA-256 of its bytes written as JSON with two-space indentation and one trailing newline. This version ships two packs. `default`, version 1, is always selected; its dimensions are `language-and-runtime` (order 10), `architecture` (20), `interfaces` (60) and `platforms-and-dependencies` (70), the first, third and fourth byte for byte the RS-11 bullets of `polaris-reader-standards-v1` that those names denote, and `architecture` the two lines `  - Architecture: how the product is divided into parts (modules,` and `    packages, commands or processes), and which part holds what.`. `server`, version 1, is selected by the trait `server-entry`; its dimensions are `architecture` (20), `networking` (30), `state-and-persistence` (40) and `concurrency` (50), each byte for byte the RS-11 bullet of `polaris-reader-standards-v1` that its name denotes. Networking, state and persistence, concurrency and the server form of architecture are the server-only dimensions.

`syzygy dossier brief` SHALL select packs deterministically from the pinned tree's trait profile: the traits of the structure record (`derive.ts`), derived from the paths and modes of the tree its step guard lists, a path that a secret detector of the bound screening policy matches, or any of whose directory prefixes it matches, being left out as the structure record leaves it out. This version adds one trait rule, `server-entry`, version 1. It matches a blob path that neither the vendored-path nor the generated-path rule sets apart, that the tests-path rule does not match, that has no directory segment `example`, `examples`, `sample`, `samples`, `demo` or `demos`, and whose basename is one of `server.c`, `server.cc`, `server.cpp`, `server.go`, `server.rs`, `server.py`, `server.rb`, `server.js`, `server.mjs`, `server.ts`, `server.java`, `server.ex`, `server.php`, `Procfile`, `openapi.json`, `openapi.yaml`, `openapi.yml`, `swagger.json`, `swagger.yaml` and `swagger.yml`, compared exactly; the trait holds when at least one path matches. The selection is the default pack, then every pack whose `selectedBy` trait holds, in identity code-unit order, so packs compose for every trait the repository has. Composition starts from the default pack's dimensions and applies each later pack in selection order, a dimension of a later pack replacing the one of the same `id` and any other being added; the composed dimensions are ordered by `order`, then by `id`. Where the trait profile cannot be derived, the default pack alone is selected.

The brief SHALL carry the reader standards with RS-11's dimension bullets, the lines from `  - Language and runtime:` up to the line before `  - Check: every dimension has a row`, replaced by the composed dimensions' lines in order. Where the composed lines equal those of `polaris-reader-standards-v1`, as they do for the selection `default@1`, `server@1`, the carried standards, their heading and the brief record's `readerStandards` are byte for byte those of that version, and the pack mechanism changes no byte of the brief. Otherwise the composed standards text is `polaris-reader-standards-v1`'s with those lines replaced and its version line extended, right after its closing backtick, by a suffix: the text `, technology dimensions from shape packs ` followed by the selected packs' identities, each between two backtick characters, joined by `, `. The brief carries that composed text through the same transform as version 1 (its version line dropped and every heading one level down) under the heading version 1's section takes, except that its parenthesis holds the version between backtick characters, then that suffix, then `; sha256 of the composed text: ` and the composed text's digest between backtick characters, in place of version 1's reference-file digest; that digest is the SHA-256 of the composed text, which ends in one newline as version 1's does. In that case the brief's sentence "The heading below names the digest of the skill's `references/reader-standards.md`; the text under it is that file without its version line and with every heading one level down, so it does not itself hash to that digest." reads instead "The heading below names the shape packs that supplied the technology dimensions and the digest of the composed standards text; the text under it is that composed text without its version line and with every heading one level down, so it does not itself hash to that digest.", and the brief record's `readerStandards` is `{"version": "polaris-reader-standards-v1", "sha256": <that digest>, "packs": [<the identities>]}`, `version` naming the base text and `sha256` the composed one. The rendered-design packet SHALL carry the standards text the brief carried, whole as version 1's is carried, recomposed from the packs the brief record names and confirmed against that digest, its introducing sentence naming, where the text is composed, `polaris-reader-standards-v1`, then `, technology dimensions from shape packs ` and the identities joined by `, `, then `, sha256 ` and the composed digest, in place of the version and version 1's digest; where the brief record names no packs it carries `polaris-reader-standards-v1` as before. Where a named pack identity is not one this Syzygy ships, or the recomposed digest differs from the recorded one, the packet SHALL NOT be built, and the refusal says `the reader standards the brief carried cannot be recomposed (<pack identity> is not shipped)` or `(the recomposed digest differs from the brief record's)`; it never falls back to version 1. The brief record SHALL carry a member `shapePacks`, `{"traits", "selected"}`: `traits` is `{"state": "derived", "serverEntry": {"rule": "server-entry", "ruleVersion": 1, "holds", "paths", "pathsNotListed"}}`, `paths` being at most the first 20 matched paths in code-unit order, in the census's shown form, and `pathsNotListed` the count of the rest, or `{"state": "unknown", "why": <words>}`; `selected` is one `{"id", "version", "sha256", "because"}` per selected pack in selection order, `because` being `always selected` for the default pack and `trait server-entry holds` for the server pack. Check's structure record, on its report, gains the trait as `traits.serverEntry`, `{"rule": "server-entry@1", "holds", "listed", "notListed"}` in its own form, and `server-entry@1` among its `rules`. A brief whose selection is the default pack alone names no server-only dimension: it carries none of the strings `Networking:`, `State and persistence:`, `Concurrency:`, `deployed and divided` and `(server and`. Shape packs reach only the operator-agent brief and design packet: the provider mode's profile, reader questions and requested assets are unchanged.

A draft in schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v6` is a schema-v5 draft in every respect but one: it carries a required top-level member `thesisBasis`, one of `stated`, `code-reading` and `none`, saying what its introduction, which is the dossier's thesis, rests on: `stated`, a statement of the project's purpose by its maintainers, which the introduction cites; `code-reading`, no source the authoring session found states the project's purpose, and the introduction states what the code does, never why it was made; `none`, neither, and the introduction is not established. Brief v11 SHALL state this rule and issue schema v6; it is brief v10 changed only in its version line, its draft-schema line, which names schema v6 and that schema's digest, the reader standards as the shape-pack paragraphs above compose them with the one sentence they replace for a composed text, and one paragraph, placed after the paragraph that names each section's `id`, which reads: "Set `thesisBasis` to say what your introduction, the dossier's thesis, rests on. Write `stated` when it rests on a statement of the project's purpose by its maintainers, such as a README, documentation or a package description, and cite that statement. Write `code-reading` when no source you found states the project's purpose: the introduction then says what the code does, never why it was made, quotes nothing, and cites at least two entry points, two different code files through which a user or a program enters the project, such as a main program, a command's handler or the module a package exports; Syzygy renders it under a line saying that no maintainer statement of purpose was found and that it is Polaris's reading of the code. Write `none` when neither is possible, and make the introduction an `unknown` block, or a `non-normative` one that makes no claim." Syzygy SHALL report a repair finding of kind `thesis-basis`, at `$.thesisBasis`, for a v6 draft whose `thesisBasis` is `stated` while its introduction is not an Inferred block citing at least one source; is `code-reading` while its introduction is not an Inferred block resting on sources (`basis: source`) that carries no quotation (its `quotations` list is empty or absent and its `text` holds no quotation lead-in) and cites at least two entry points; or is `none` while its introduction is not an Unknown or non-normative block. An entry point, as check can test it, is a cited path that the language-by-extension rule matches with a language other than Markdown, reStructuredText, AsciiDoc, Plain text, JSON, YAML, TOML, XML, INI, HTML, CSS, SCSS, C or C++ header, SQL, Protocol Buffers and GraphQL, that the vendored-path, generated-path, tests-path and docs-tree rules do not match, and that has no directory segment `example`, `examples`, `sample`, `samples`, `demo` or `demos`; two entry points are two citations naming two different such paths, whatever screening does with their bodies. Syzygy does not check whether those files are where a user or a program enters the project, or whether the repository truly states no purpose: the fidelity review judges the introduction against its cited spans as it judges any block, and the rendered line attributes the absence to the authoring session. A `thesisBasis` member in a draft of schema v5 or earlier is a `field-version` finding, and a v6 draft without one fails its schema.

Syzygy SHALL render a v6 draft whose `thesisBasis` is `code-reading` with, on `index.html`, immediately before the introduction's block and outside it, in both views, the Syzygy-written line `<p class="thesis-basis" data-thesis-basis="code-reading">The authoring session found no maintainer statement of purpose, so this summary is Polaris's reading of the code, not the maintainers' words.</p>`; the line carries no label token, citation or anchor, and the introduction keeps every rule of a claim block. Where the introduction renders as a withheld item, or the draft layer is withheld, no line renders; `stated` and `none` render no line. For a v6 draft only, `machine.json` SHALL carry, beside and outside its `reviewStatus` member, a member `thesisBasis`, `{"value", "declaredBy": "the authoring session", "label": "Inferred", "lineRendered"}`, `value` the draft's `thesisBasis` and `lineRendered` whether the line renders; that the repository states no purpose is the authoring session's report, never Syzygy's observation. The provider mode is unaffected.

A draft in schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v7` is a schema-v6 draft in every respect but one: a diagram's `kind` is one of the six graph kinds, `flow`, `lifecycle`, `state-machine`, `boundary`, `dependency` and `placement`, which keep `nodes` and `edges` exactly as before, or one of two figure kinds with records of their own, which carry their own members in place of `nodes` and `edges` and every other member of a diagram (`id`, `title`, `home`, `kind`, `relationship` and `disposition`) unchanged. A `sequence` carries `participants`, two to five, each `{"id", "label", "epistemic", "citations"}` as a node is, in left-to-right order; and `messages`, one to thirty, each `{"id", "from", "to", "label", "epistemic", "citations"}` as an edge is, in the order they happen, `from` naming the participant that sends it and `to` the one that receives it, the same participant for a message to itself. A `swimlane` carries `lanes`, two to five, each `{"id", "label", "epistemic", "citations"}` as a node is, in left-to-right order; and `steps`, two to twenty, each `{"id", "lane", "label", "epistemic", "citations"}`, in the order they are done, `lane` naming the lane whose actor or component does it. `from`, `to` and `lane` are references, never identities. Wherever this requirement or REQ-polaris-generation-035 states a rule for a diagram's node, it holds for each participant, lane and step, and wherever it states one for an edge, it holds for each message: each is a claim with its own identity, label, citations and marking, checked, reviewed, withheld and listed in its figure's list of every component and relationship as a node or an edge is, and each appears once in the machine form. The fidelity review SHALL judge a message as read with its sender, its receiver and its place in the order, and a step as read with its lane and its place in the order. The order of the messages and of the steps is the agent's, stated by their order in the draft: the arrow Syzygy draws from each step to the next carries no claim, label, citation or tally entry of its own.

For a v7 draft Syzygy SHALL report a repair finding of kind `unresolved-reference` at `$.diagrams[<i>].messages[<j>].from` or `$.diagrams[<i>].messages[<j>].to` for a message whose sender or receiver names no participant of its own diagram, and at `$.diagrams[<i>].steps[<j>].lane` for a step whose lane names no lane of its own diagram, a participant or lane of another diagram included; a finding of kind `figure-kind` for each member a diagram carries that its kind does not take, at `$.diagrams[<i>].<member>`, and for each member its kind requires that it lacks, at `$.diagrams[<i>]`, naming that member: a graph kind takes `nodes` and `edges` and none of `participants`, `messages`, `lanes` and `steps`; a `sequence` takes `participants` and `messages` and none of `nodes`, `edges`, `lanes` and `steps`; and a `swimlane` takes `lanes` and `steps` and none of `nodes`, `edges`, `participants` and `messages`; and a finding of kind `figure-unused`, at `$.diagrams[<i>].participants[<k>]` or `$.diagrams[<i>].lanes[<k>]`, for a participant no message names as its sender or receiver and for a lane no step names, each of which would draw an empty column. Each such finding names the diagram's identity, or names `$.diagrams[<index>]` where the diagram has no well-formed identity. The schema check of a v7 draft SHALL report no `schema` finding at a path where a `figure-kind` finding stands, and SHALL report every other schema fault as it does today, among them a `schema` finding at each list whose count is outside its bounds (participants and lanes two to five, messages one to thirty, steps two to twenty) and at each member of the wrong shape. In a draft of schema v6 or earlier Syzygy SHALL report a finding of kind `field-version` at `$.diagrams[<i>].kind` for a diagram whose kind is `sequence` or `swimlane`, and one at `$.diagrams[<i>].<member>` for each of `participants`, `messages`, `lanes` and `steps` that a diagram carries; each stands in place of the `schema` finding version 2.4 reports at the same path, as a v4 diagram's `home` does, and every other finding version 2.4 reports for that draft, a missing `nodes` or `edges` among them, is kept. Brief v12 SHALL issue schema v7; it is brief v11 changed only in its version line, its draft-schema line, which names schema v7 and that schema's digest, and one paragraph placed immediately after brief v11's thesis-basis paragraph, which reads: "A diagram's `kind` may also be `sequence` or `swimlane`, and each takes its own members in place of `nodes` and `edges`. Use a `sequence` when two to five components exchange messages in a fixed order, such as a request, the calls it causes and the reply: list the components in `participants`, left to right, each with `id`, `label`, `epistemic` and `citations` as a node has, and list one to thirty messages in `messages`, in the order they happen, each with `id`, `label`, `epistemic` and `citations` as an edge has, naming the participant that sends it in `from` and the one that receives it in `to` (the same one for a message to itself). Use a `swimlane` when two to five actors or components do a workflow's steps in turn: list them in `lanes`, left to right, each as a node, and list two to twenty steps in `steps`, in the order they are done, each with `id`, `label`, `epistemic` and `citations` and naming in `lane` the lane that does it; Syzygy draws an arrow from each step to the next, so the order of `steps` is a claim the steps' citations must support. Every participant sends or receives a message and every lane holds a step. On a narrow screen Syzygy shows a sequence as a numbered list of its messages and a swimlane as a numbered list of its steps, each headed by its lane. Keep every other figure a graph of `nodes` and `edges` in one of the six graph kinds." A draft in schema v6 SHALL be checked and rendered exactly as version 2.4 checks and renders it, the same findings and the same bytes of every page and of `machine.json`, except that those `field-version` findings stand in place of the `schema` findings at their paths, and except for the additions of version 2.15 below; and so is a draft in schema v5 or earlier, with the same exception.
