Each graph-kind diagram of a v7 draft SHALL be checked, laid out, measured for fit and rendered byte for byte as the same diagram in a v6 draft, except in the findings about its members: where a graph-kind diagram of a v7 draft lacks `nodes` or `edges`, the `figure-kind` finding at `$.diagrams[<i>]` naming that member stands in place of the `schema` finding a v6 draft reports at `$.diagrams[<i>].nodes` or `$.diagrams[<i>].edges`, and no `schema` finding is reported at that member's path; and where it carries `participants`, `messages`, `lanes` or `steps`, the `figure-kind` finding at that member's path stands in place of the `field-version` finding a v6 draft reports there. Such a diagram does not pass its check, so no page or `machine.json` byte depends on this exception.

Each of the two kinds has its own pure layout, deterministic for the same record, that reads identities, references and counts and never a label's width. With n participants or lanes in draft order, column i (counting from 0) is 176 px wide at x = 24 + 184 x i, and the drawing's laid-out width is W = 40 + 184 x n px, so 408, 592, 776 or 960 px. A sequence of m messages draws each participant as a box 56 px high at the top of its column (y = 24), its lifeline running down the column's centre from the box's foot to the drawing's foot, and message k (counting from 0) as an arrow at y = 128 + 72 x k from the sender's lifeline to the receiver's or, for a message to itself, as a loop that leaves its lifeline, reaches 32 px to the right and returns 24 px lower; each message's label is centred above its arrow, between the two lifelines or, for a loop, on its lifeline; its height is H = 104 + 72 x m px. A swimlane of s steps draws each lane as a band its column wide and the drawing's height, headed by a box 56 px high at y = 24, and step k (counting from 0) as a box 176 px wide and 56 px high in its lane's column at y = 104 + 88 x k, with an arrow from each step's foot to the next step's head, straight within one lane and curved between two; its height is H = 96 + 88 x s px. A message's label wraps at 22 characters, and a participant's, lane's or step's at 19, each to at most two lines; a longer label ends in `…`, as a graph figure's does, and its whole text stands in the figure's list. Text in either drawing is the 13 px figure text, and each box and arrow is drawn in its marking's line style, as a graph figure's are. Either drawing passes the inertness check a graph figure's drawing passes, and one that does not renders the notice a graph figure renders in its place. These constants are this version's; a change that alters W, the layout or the phone form's rule below is a change to this requirement.

Syzygy SHALL render a produced sequence or swimlane figure on the page and in the place that a graph figure with the same home takes, in a box of the same width, as a `<figure>` carrying `data-figure-kind="sequence"` or `data-figure-kind="swimlane"` beside the attributes a graph figure on that page carries, version 2.3's `data-visual-provenance="curated"` among them. It holds, in order: its caption; its relationship line, whose eyebrow names its kind; its two forms, in `<div class="figure-forms" data-figure-width="<W>">`, which holds the drawing in `<div class="diagram-scroll figure-drawing">` and then the phone form, `<ol class="figure-stepper">`; the legend of the line styles its drawing uses; its tally, `{P} participants and {M} messages, as marked:` or `{L} lanes and {S} steps, as marked:`, each noun singular for a count of one (`1 participant`, `1 message`; a swimlane has at least two lanes and two steps), followed, as a graph figure's tally is, by ` {O} observed, {I} inferred, {U} unknown.` counted over its elements, each once; and the collapsed list of every component and relationship, holding the participants or lanes, then the messages or steps, in draft order, and the declarative source. The phone form is an ordered list with one item per message, or per step, in draft order. A message's item is `<li class="{marking}"><span class="stepper-head">{sender} → {receiver}</span> {label}</li>` and a step's `<li class="{marking}"><span class="stepper-head">{lane}</span> {label}</li>`, where `{marking}` is the element's mark (`inferred` or `unknown`) and each label is the participant's, lane's, message's or step's whole label, escaped as HTML text and never wrapped or cut, followed by ` ?` for an Unknown element as in the drawing, and a withheld element's label is replaced by the same Syzygy words as in the drawing and the list. The stylesheet sets the list's text at 13 px and draws each item with a left border, solid, dashed or dotted by its mark, as the legend's line styles are. The phone form carries no identity, citation, anchor or label token, because each element's claim is its entry in the list of every component and relationship. The pages' stylesheet SHALL display exactly one of the two forms at any width of the `figure-forms` box, with no script: the drawing while the box is at least W px wide, and the phone form, with the drawing not displayed, while it is narrower, by a container size query on that box; the phone form wraps its text within the box (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`), so neither form scrolls sideways. At this version's column widths a figure of either kind shows its drawing at 1440 px, W being at most 960 and the column 966, and its phone form at 390 px, the column being 261 and W at least 408, where a swimlane reads as a numbered stepper. These style rules SHALL be carried by a page on which a produced figure of either kind renders its two forms, and by no other page (a notice in a figure's place carries none), so a page without such a figure keeps its bytes apart from the additions of version 2.15; since version 2.23 the rules among them that display one of the two forms by the container size query on the `figure-forms` box SHALL be carried by a page on which a produced sequence, swimlane or containment figure renders its two forms, and by no other page, and the stepper's other rules as this sentence first states.

Figure fit SHALL examine every produced sequence and swimlane figure and, at each viewport, SHALL take the form the stylesheet displays in a box C wide: where W <= C, the drawing, with D = W and T = 13 px; where W > C, the phone form, whose laid-out and drawn widths are C, since it fills its box and wraps, and whose text is never scaled, so T = 13 px, the size the stylesheet sets for it. Such a figure therefore fits at 1440 and at 390 and has no entry; one whose layout cannot be computed, as where the draft holds an `unresolved-reference`, `figure-kind` or `figure-unused` finding for it or a count outside its bounds, is listed Unknown as any figure is. Each such figure's declarative source SHALL be derived from its record alone, by position and never by the draft's identities, one statement to a line, each line indented two spaces for each level it is nested and the text ending in a newline: a sequence as `sequenceDiagram`, then `participant p<i> as <label>` for each participant in order, then `p<i>-->>p<j>: <label>` for each message in order, with no `class` or `classDef` line (a sequence diagram takes none, and every message of a draft is drawn dotted); a swimlane as `flowchart TB`, then, for each lane in order, `subgraph l<i>["<label>"]`, its steps in draft order as `s<k>["<label>"]` one level deeper, and `end`, then `s<k> --> s<k+1>` for each step and the next, then one `class` line listing the steps of each mark that has any, inferred before unknown, and the two `classDef` lines, each as a graph figure's source writes them; `<i>` and `<k>` count from 0 in draft order, an Unknown element's label is followed by ` (unknown)`, and every label is escaped as a graph figure's is. `machine.json` SHALL carry, for a render holding at least one sequence or swimlane figure and only then, a member `figureStructures`, one entry per such figure rendered, in draft diagram order: `{"diagram", "kind": "sequence", "page", "laidOutPx", "participants": [<identity>, ...], "messages": [{"id", "from", "to"}, ...]}` or `{"diagram", "kind": "swimlane", "page", "laidOutPx", "lanes": [<identity>, ...], "steps": [{"id", "lane"}, ...]}`, each list in draft order; labels, marks and citations stay in its claims, each element once. A sequence or swimlane figure is a figure for the reading ledger as any diagram is. The provider mode is unaffected: its draft schema and diagram kinds, its pages, their bytes and its machine form are unchanged.

A draft in schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v8` (version 2.16) is a schema-v7 draft in every respect but one: a section MAY carry a member `subsections`, an ordered list of two to twelve subsections that divides what the section says into the questions it answers, in reading order. Each subsection is `{"id", "title", "answer", "paragraphs"}`: `id` an identity, unique among every identity of the draft as any `id` is; `title` a short label naming the question the subsection answers, under every rule for an element's short label; `answer` one claim block in a paragraph form the introduction may take (never a presentation element), labelled Inferred or Unknown and never non-normative, whose `text` is one sentence that answers that question; and `paragraphs`, zero to thirty, its content, each in every respect what a section's paragraph may be. A section's own `paragraphs` stay required and open the section, before its first subsection. A subsection's answer and every claim its paragraphs carry are claims of the section for every rule of this requirement and of REQ-polaris-generation-035: each is checked, withheld, reviewed, covered, counted, listed in `machine.json` and rendered as a claim of the section's own paragraphs is, and wherever a rule speaks of a section's paragraphs or its content it holds for its subsections' answers and paragraphs, which follow the section's own paragraphs in draft order, each subsection's answer before its paragraphs. A subsection has no disposition of its own: it renders exactly when its section's content renders. No figure is homed in a subsection, and a diagram homed in the section keeps the place version 2.2 gives it. A deep dive carries no subsections at this version. Every rule this requirement and REQ-polaris-generation-035 state for a schema-v7 draft holds for a schema-v8 draft.

For a v8 draft Syzygy SHALL report a repair finding of kind `subsection-answer` at `$.sections[<i>].subsections[<j>].answer` for each subsection whose answer is non-normative, and for each whose answer's `text` is not one sentence by rule `one-sentence-answer`, version 1, which reads that text alone. In this rule a terminator is a full stop, question mark or exclamation mark (U+002E, U+003F, U+0021), a closer is a closing parenthesis, a closing square bracket, an apostrophe or a right single quotation mark (U+0029, U+005D, U+0027, U+2019), a quote is a straight double quote (U+0022), and white space is a character that the RS-19 word count splits at, the class `\s` of ECMAScript regular expressions with the `u` flag. The rule removes the characters between the first and second quote, between the third and fourth, and so on, keeping the quotes themselves and leaving an unpaired last quote and what follows it in place, and then removes leading and trailing white space. What remains is one sentence when it ends with a terminator followed by zero or more closers, or with a quote that closes a pair, and holds no terminator that zero or more closers and then a white-space character follow. So an abbreviation written with a full stop and followed by a space ends a sentence by this rule, and the brief says so; a text ending with a closed quotation is one sentence whether or not a terminator precedes the closing quote. The finding names the subsection's identity, or names `$.sections[<i>].subsections[<j>]` where the subsection has no well-formed identity. A subsection title that breaks the short-label rule raises the finding an element's short label raises, at `$.sections[<i>].subsections[<j>].title`; a `subsections` list of fewer than two or more than twelve, or a subsection's `paragraphs` of more than thirty, is a `schema` finding at that list. In a draft of schema v7 or earlier Syzygy SHALL report a finding of kind `field-version` at `$.sections[<i>].subsections` for each section that carries `subsections`, in place of the `schema` finding version 2.15 reports at that path, and every other finding version 2.15 reports for that draft.

Syzygy SHALL render a produced section of a v8 draft that carries subsections where and as it renders any section, with its subsections following the section's own paragraphs and preceding its list of deep-dive links, in draft order, each as `<section class="subsection" id="subsection-<id>"><h3><title></h3><div class="subsection-answer"><answer></div><paragraphs></section>`: `<id>` the subsection's identity; `<title>` its title as text; `<answer>` its answer rendered as the introduction's claim block is, a withheld answer rendering as a withheld item does in that place; and `<paragraphs>` its paragraphs rendered as the section's own are. A subsection's title is a short label over its answer and its paragraphs' claims, and over only withheld ones it reads as any such label does. Each page that renders at least one subsection SHALL carry an on-page contents list, on `index.html` immediately before the first section that page renders and on a Pole page immediately after the Pole's heading and any stopped-run banner, `<nav class="page-contents" aria-label="On this page"><p class="page-contents-title">On this page</p><ol><items></ol></nav>`, with one item for each section the page renders, in page order (on a Pole page the why section's back-link to `index.html` is not a section the page renders and has no item), `<li><a href="#section-<section id>"><section title></a></li>`, the item of a section that renders subsections holding, after its link, `<ol>` with one item for each of them, `<li><a href="#subsection-<id>"><title></a></li>`, each title as its heading shows it. A section that renders no content of the draft (one not generated, one whose disposition is not `produced`, and every section where the draft layer is withheld) renders no subsection and its item, where it has one, holds no list; a page that renders no subsection carries no contents list. The list carries no script and is not sticky, the sticky rule staying scoped to the site navigation; no fragment it names sits inside a collapsible section. The stylesheet rules for subsections and the contents list are carried only by a page that renders a subsection, as the figure-kind rules of version 2.5 are carried only by a page holding such a figure. A subsection and the contents list carry no `data-reading-level` and no topic attribute of their own; how they read is the rendered-design review's to judge.

Brief v14 SHALL issue schema v8; it is brief v13 changed only in its version line, its draft-schema line, which names schema v8 and that schema's digest, and one paragraph placed immediately after the figure-kinds paragraph brief v13 carries from brief v12, which reads: "A section may also carry `subsections`, two to twelve, in reading order, when it answers more than one question. Each subsection has `id`, `title`, `answer` and `paragraphs`: `title` names the question in a short label; `answer` is one Inferred or Unknown claim block, with the members that form of a paragraph takes, whose `text` is one sentence that answers the question; and `paragraphs`, zero to thirty, hold what supports that answer, as a section's paragraphs do. Check counts a full stop, question mark or exclamation mark that a space follows, outside a quotation, as the end of a sentence, so write `for example`, not `e.g.`, in an answer. A section's own paragraphs open it, before its subsections. Syzygy renders each subsection under its own heading with its answer first, and lists the page's sections and subsections at the top of the page. A figure is never homed in a subsection, and a deep dive has none." Every statement above of what brief v13 carries, equals or differs by holds of brief v14 apart from those three differences.

A draft in schema v7 or earlier SHALL be checked and rendered exactly as version 2.15 checks and renders it, the same findings and the same bytes of every page, of `machine.json` and of every record check writes, except that a `field-version` finding stands in place of the `schema` finding at a section's `subsections`. A schema-v8 draft none of whose sections carries `subsections` SHALL be checked and rendered as the same draft in schema v7 is, the same findings and the same bytes of every page and of `machine.json`, other than where a page or a record names the draft's schema version. The provider mode is unaffected: no provider draft carries a subsection, and the rendered bytes the provider golden pins do not change.

A draft in schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v9` (version 2.19) is a schema-v8 draft in every respect but two, both on graph diagrams, the diagrams whose kind takes `nodes` and `edges`. Every edge of such a diagram SHALL carry a member `relation`, the one relation type the arrow asserts, which is one of the eleven of this closed list: `contains`, `declares-dependency`, `needs-job`, `depends-on-service`, `calls`, `imports`, `reads`, `writes`, `sends-to`, `starts` and `relates-to`. Every node of such a diagram MAY carry a member `unit`, naming the one unit of the pinned repository the node stands for, as exactly one of these six forms: `{"kind": "repository-root"}`; `{"kind": "top-level-directory", "path": <a directory name>}`; `{"kind": "package-manifest", "path": <a repository path>}`; `{"kind": "dependency-name", "name": <a name>}`; `{"kind": "workflow-job", "path": <a repository path>, "job": <a job key>}`; and `{"kind": "compose-service", "path": <a repository path>, "service": <a service key>}`, each repository path a string a citation's path may be and each directory name, name, job key and service key a string of 1 to 256 code units. A unit is compared by its kind and the exact code units of its members, never by any label, and is never rendered. A node's label, mark and citations, and an edge's, are the draft's under every rule of this requirement; neither member sets or changes a label, so the draft still labels nothing Observed. A v9 graph edge without `relation`, a `relation` outside the list, and a `unit` of any other form or carrying any other member are each a `schema` finding, at the edge's `relation` or at the node's `unit`. A sequence or swimlane figure (version 2.5) is unchanged: its participants, messages, lanes and steps carry neither member, and none is ever witnessed. Every rule this requirement and REQ-polaris-generation-035 state for a schema-v8 draft holds for a schema-v9 draft.

For a v9 draft Syzygy SHALL report a repair finding of kind `unit-reference` at `$.diagrams[<i>].nodes[<j>].unit`, naming the node's identity, for a `top-level-directory` unit whose `path` holds `/` or is `.` or `..`; for a `package-manifest` unit whose path's last segment is none of `package.json`, `Cargo.toml`, `pyproject.toml` and `go.mod`; for a `workflow-job` unit whose path is not of the form the `github-actions-workflow` path rule of version 2.7 names; for a `compose-service` unit whose path's last segment is none of the eight the `compose-file` path rule of version 2.14 names, or whose path is a workflow path; and for a `package-manifest`, `workflow-job` or `compose-service` unit whose path names no blob at the pinned revision. Its detail names the unit's kind and the rule it fails, and never the unit's path or name. A `top-level-directory` unit naming a directory the tree does not hold is no such finding: whether the tree holds it is for rule `relation-witness` to decide. A `package-manifest`, `workflow-job` or `compose-service` unit whose path, or any of whose directory prefixes, a secret detector of the bound screening policy matches, read as a path or as text, or whose path the screen's path outcome denies, is never tested for a blob: it raises no `unit-reference` finding for that test, whether or not such a blob exists, and rule `relation-witness` decides an edge with such an end `unknown` (`coverage-partial`) wherever the order below reaches its coverage test. No finding, page, `machine.json` member or member of a check record or check report carries a unit's value; the frozen draft revision holds it as the agent wrote it, as it holds every member of the draft.

Rule `relation-witness` version 1 decides, for each edge of a produced graph diagram of a v9 draft, one witness state, `witnessed`, `challenged` or `unknown`, from the edge's relation, the units of its two end nodes and the facts Syzygy derives for the revision, and from nothing else the draft carries, never from a label or a model. A relation is witnessed only by facts of one kind, between units of two stated kinds, by the rules its row names: `contains`, by a `contains` fact of rule `top-level-unit` (version 2.3), from a `repository-root` unit to a `top-level-directory` unit; `declares-dependency`, by a `dependency` fact (version 2.7) of the package recognizer whose path rule names the source unit's path, from a `package-manifest` unit to a `dependency-name` unit; `needs-job`, by a `job-needs` fact of `github-actions-workflow`, from a `workflow-job` unit to a `workflow-job` unit; and `depends-on-service`, by a `service-depends-on` fact of `compose-file` (version 2.14), from a `compose-service` unit to a `compose-service` unit. The other seven relation types have no witnessing fact at this version, and no fact of one kind ever witnesses a relation another row names. A fact joins two units where: for `contains`, it runs from the structure record's `tree-root` fact to the `top-level-unit` fact whose `path` equals the target unit's `path`; for `declares-dependency`, its `path` equals the source unit's `path` and its `name` the target unit's `name`, whatever its section and group; and for `needs-job` and `depends-on-service`, its `path` equals both units' `path`, and its `from` and `to` equal the source unit's and the target unit's `job` or `service`. Equality is of exact code units; nothing is case-folded or normalised. The rule states only that a fact of one kind joins two named units, as that fact's own rule matched; it reads no file body, size or object identifier beyond what that fact's rule reads, so for a private repository it derives nothing from the size or object identifier of a blob excluded or withheld on any ground.

A rule witnesses only while it is in the eligibility table of rule `relation-witness`, and a change to that table is a new version of the rule, stated by a later version of this requirement. At version 1 the table holds one row, `top-level-unit` version 1, whose facts render Observed under the currency policy of version 2.3; that row names no evidence record, resting on version 2.3's adoption of those facts and their declared currency bound, so the test below does not reach it; none of the ten manifest recognizers of versions 2.7 and 2.14 is in it, so at this version only a `contains` arrow can be witnessed or challenged, and no declared manifest fact witnesses or challenges any arrow. A recognizer becomes eligible only by a later version of this requirement that adds its row, which it may do only once all of these hold: the recognizer has the golden fixtures and the recorded mutation run that the label paragraph of version 2.7 below requires, the run made at a commit at which every module the record names, among them the recognizer's own module, its reader's and the shared fact modules, has the SHA-256 the record gives it; the derived-fact currency policy declares a bound for that rule and version; and that same later version labels the recognizer's facts Observed under that bound, naming the record by path and SHA-256, and states the evidence text its witnessed arrows carry. The implementation SHALL carry the table as data, each row naming its rule and version and, for a recognizer, its record's path and SHA-256, with a test of the hermetic tier that fails where a module a named record lists no longer has the SHA-256 the record gives it.

The rule decides each edge in this order, the first case that applies giving its state and, for `unknown`, its reason: an edge the draft labels Unknown, or one rendered as a withheld item, is `unknown` (`edge-not-inferred`); an edge whose relation has no witnessing fact, `unknown` (`no-witness-rule`); an edge one of whose end nodes carries no `unit`, `unknown` (`endpoint-without-unit`); an edge one of whose end nodes carries a unit that draws a `unit-reference` finding, `unknown` (`endpoint-unit-invalid`), so one error gives one finding; an edge one of whose end nodes carries a unit of another kind than the relation's row takes at that end, `unknown` (`endpoint-kind-mismatch`); an edge whose relation's rule (for `declares-dependency`, the recognizer whose path rule names the source unit's path) is not in the eligibility table, `unknown` (`recognizer-not-eligible`); an edge whose facts could not be derived, the record the relation's row reads (the structure record for `contains`, the `manifestFacts` member for a recognizer's relation) being in `state` `unknown`, `unknown` (`facts-unknown`), a record the row does not read never deciding it; an edge whose two units a fact of the witnessing kind joins, `witnessed`, naming that fact (the first such fact in its record's order); an edge for which coverage is not complete, `unknown` (`coverage-partial`); and otherwise `challenged`. Coverage is complete for `contains` exactly where the structure record is `derived` with `secretPathsLeftOut`, `submodules` and `unitsNotListed` all zero, since a top-level directory that is a submodule, or holds only submodules, has no `top-level-unit` fact though the tree holds it; and for the relation of an eligible recognizer exactly where the `manifestFacts` member is `derived` with `factsNotListed` zero and the source unit's path has a listed file entry of that recognizer that is Inferred with `valuesLeftOut` zero. Anything else is partial, so a fact that a path left out, a submodule, an unlisted unit or a dropped value could hold is never taken as absent.

`syzygy dossier check` SHALL decide the states of every edge of a v9 draft that has no `schema` finding, from the structure record and the `manifestFacts` member it derives for that revision. After every finding version 2.16 reports for the draft it SHALL report the `unit-reference` findings, in draft order of the nodes, and then a repair finding of kind `relation-challenged` at `$.diagrams[<i>].edges[<j>]` for each `challenged` edge, one per edge, in draft order, its detail naming the edge's identity, its relation, the fact kind and the rule and version searched, in the words `no <fact> fact joins this edge's two units in what rule <rule> version <version> derived at the pinned revision; correct the arrow, its relation or its units`, and never a unit's path or name. A draft with such a finding does not proceed to review until a resubmitted revision passes. An `unknown` state is never a finding and never advice. Check reads no currency policy for this rule, and writes no member of its record or its report for the states but those findings.

Each render SHALL decide the states again, by the same rule, from the facts it re-derives itself: the derived structure the region on `index.html` states at that render and the render's manifest member (version 2.9). An edge is witnessed at a render only where the fact that joins its units renders Observed there; where that fact renders Unknown (`no-currency-bound-declared` or `stale-beyond-currency-bound`), the edge is `unknown` with the reason `fact-not-current`, decided in place of `witnessed`. A witnessed edge renders, in the drawing and in the figure's list of every component and relationship, as Syzygy's statement of that fact and not as the draft's: its arrow is drawn solid and carries `data-epistemic="observed"`, and its label is Syzygy's words for the relation, `contains`, `declares a dependency on`, `needs` or `depends on`, never the edge's `label`; its list item reads `<from> → <to>: <words>`, `<from>` and `<to>` being the end nodes' labels as rendered, then `Witnessed by Syzygy`, with the DOM text, clipped in the reading view by the renderer's visually-hidden class and shown in the evidence view, `Evidence: label Observed; witnessed by fact <fact id> (<fact kind>), derived by Syzygy by rule <rule> version <version> at revision <full revision>; record sha256:<hex>.` for a fact of the structure record; and the arrow and the item each carry `data-relation`, `data-witness-fact` (the fact's identity), `data-witness-rule` and `data-witness-rule-version`. The edge's own label text and citations are not rendered for it. On the page and in `machine.json` a witnessed edge is a derived fact and no claim of the draft: it carries no claim anchor, its `data-witness-*` attributes being its provenance as `data-derived-id` is a derived fact's (RFC7-28), and it enters none of the draft's counts (the page's one sentence and the appendix's, Unknown counts among them), its figure's tally counting it as Syzygy's Observed element. The fidelity review reads the draft before any render, so the edge takes its fidelity-verdict entry as every edge does, and that entry governs the run as any edge's does; it changes nothing the page shows for the witnessed edge, which carries none of the agent's words. Every other edge of a v9 draft renders as version 2.16 renders an edge, with the draft's marking, its arrow and its list item carrying `data-relation` alone. The figure keeps `data-visual-provenance="curated"`; its legend lists the solid style where a witnessed arrow is drawn, as it lists any line style its drawing uses, and its tally counts a witnessed arrow as observed. No box of a draft is ever drawn solid or labelled Observed. `machine.json` carries each edge of a v9 draft as the page renders it, with two members more, `relation` and `witness`, `{"state", "reason", "fact", "rule", "ruleVersion"}`: `reason` null for a `witnessed` or `challenged` edge, and `fact`, `rule` and `ruleVersion` the witnessing fact's identity, rule and version for a `witnessed` edge and null otherwise; a witnessed edge's entry is labelled `observed` and its text is Syzygy's words. A `challenged` state at a render, which only a difference between what the render derives and what check derived can give, keeps the draft's marking on the page and is carried in `machine.json` as it is.

Version 2.44 makes rule `relation-witness` version 2: version 1 with one row more, for `imports`, and one witness state more, `corroborated`. Every statement of this requirement about rule `relation-witness` version 1 holds for version 2 except as this paragraph and the next state, and from version 2.44 every decision this requirement assigns to rule `relation-witness`, of a graph edge, of a cited or derived relationship and of a nesting or a `parent`, is made by version 2, at check and at each render; a literal finding detail that names version 1, as the `relationship-not-derivable` detail of version 2.24 does, is kept verbatim. The `imports` row is a corroborating row: `imports` is corroborated only by an import fact (version 2.39) whose `outcome` is `edge`, of rule `typescript-relative-import` or `typescript-package-import` version 1, from a `package-manifest` unit to a `package-manifest` unit; a fact joins two units where its `from` equals the source unit's `path` and its `to` the target unit's `path`, in exact code units. The row states only that a TypeScript file of the source unit holds a top-level static import or re-export statement whose operand names, by the finite rules of version 2.39, a file or a package of the target unit; never that the code is compiled, loaded, reached or called, and never that a unit does not import another. Every form version 2.39 gives an `edge` fact corroborates, `import`, `import-type`, `side-effect`, `re-export` and `re-export-type` alike, so a type-only import, which compilation erases, corroborates as a value import does, and the evidence text below names the fact's form so that a reader can tell them apart. The row is not in the eligibility table and its two rules do not enter it at this version, since an import fact is Inferred whatever evidence exists: a corroborated edge is never Observed, and an `imports` edge becomes `witnessed` only by a later version of this requirement that adds those rules to the eligibility table under every condition the eligibility paragraph above states. For an `imports` edge the order above reads so: the cases `no-witness-rule` and `recognizer-not-eligible` never apply; the record the row reads is the `importFacts` member, and an edge is `unknown` (`facts-unknown`) where that member is absent or in `state` `unknown`; an edge whose two units an `edge` fact joins is `corroborated`, naming that fact (the first such fact in the member's `facts` order), in place of `witnessed`; and every other `imports` edge is `unknown` (`coverage-partial`), the reason naming the coverage of the relation and not the member's, never `challenged`, whatever the member's `coverage`, since the member reads `.ts` files alone and leaves every other language unevaluated, and a fact its `facts` list leaves out (`factsNotListed`) could hold the statement. So no `imports` edge draws a `relation-challenged` finding, and the absence of an import fact is never read as the absence of an import. A `same-unit`, `external`, `no-unit` or `unknown` statement corroborates nothing, and the `edges` list, which names no fact, is not read. Check decides the `imports` edges from the `importFacts` member it derives for the revision. Each render decides them from the `importFacts` member of the check record of the draft revision it renders, read as it reads that record under the read bound of version 2.10, and never scans a body for them; a record written before version 2.39, or written in its bounded form, holds no such member, so its `imports` edges are `unknown` (`facts-unknown`). A derived relationship (version 2.24) still names only a relation that a row of the eligibility table joins, so a derived `imports` relationship still draws `relationship-not-derivable`. Brief v15's sentence that only `contains` can be witnessed stays true: corroboration is not witnessing, and no brief changes.

A `corroborated` edge is the draft's claim and renders as version 2.16 renders an edge, with the draft's marking, label and citations, counted in every count of the draft as any edge is, its figure's tally, legend and line style unchanged; its arrow and its list item carry `data-relation`, `data-witness-state="corroborated"`, `data-witness-fact` (the import fact's identity), `data-witness-rule` and `data-witness-rule-version`, and its list item gains, after the draft's text, the DOM text, clipped in the reading view by the renderer's visually-hidden class and shown in the evidence view, `Evidence: label Inferred, as the draft marks it; corroborated by import fact <fact id>, a lexical <form> statement found by Syzygy by rule <rule> version <version> at revision <full revision>; import facts sha256:<hex>.`, `<form>` being the fact's `form` and `<hex>` the SHA-256 of the `importFacts` member the render read, its bytes as the check record holds them, which version 2.39 makes a function of the run and the pinned tree and never of the draft. No path, line, operand or unit path of the import fact is rendered. A corroborated cited relationship of a v12 or later draft renders in its first figure (version 2.24) as this paragraph states; in each later figure that draws it, its list item takes the form version 2.24 gives such an item, `<from> → <to>: <label>, as shown in <a href="<href>"><title></a>`, with no claim anchor, no citation and no evidence text, and that item and the relationship's arrow there carry the `data-relation` and `data-witness-*` attributes above, so every figure that draws it shows the one state decided for it. For the parity of version 2.24, a render of a migrated draft reads the `importFacts` member of the check of that draft in the run; where neither that check nor the check of the draft it was migrated from has a refused read, version 2.39 makes the two members byte-identical, so every `imports` edge and the relationship it migrates to take one state and one fact, the evidence text and its digest are the same bytes, and the parity holds for them; where either check has a refused read, no parity is stated for a figure that draws an `imports` edge, or for that figure's entry. `machine.json` carries such an edge's `witness` as `{"state": "corroborated", "reason": null, "fact", "rule", "ruleVersion"}`, the import fact's identity, rule and version, its entry labelled as the draft marks it, and every other edge as before. These are the bytes version 2.44 changes. In the operator-agent mode: on a page that draws an `imports` edge whose state version 1 gave as `unknown` (`no-witness-rule`), that edge's arrow and list item where an import fact corroborates it, with the evidence text, and that edge's `witness` member in `machine.json` (`corroborated`, or `unknown` with the reason version 2 gives), with the page's measures in the size report. No check record, finding, schema, brief, figure-fit member, reading ledger, `dossier.json` byte or provider-mode byte moves, and a draft holding no `imports` edge renders byte for byte as before.

Brief v15 SHALL issue schema v9; it is brief v14 changed only in its version line, its draft-schema line, which names schema v9 and that schema's digest, and one paragraph placed immediately after the subsections paragraph brief v14 carries, which reads: "Every arrow of a graph diagram (an item of `edges`) carries `relation`, the one relation it asserts: `contains`, `declares-dependency`, `needs-job`, `depends-on-service`, `calls`, `imports`, `reads`, `writes`, `sends-to`, `starts` or `relates-to`, the last where none of the others fits. A node may carry `unit`, the one part of the repository it is: `{"kind": "repository-root"}`, `{"kind": "top-level-directory", "path": "<directory name>"}`, `{"kind": "package-manifest", "path": "<path of a package.json, Cargo.toml, pyproject.toml or go.mod>"}`, `{"kind": "dependency-name", "name": "<name as declared>"}`, `{"kind": "workflow-job", "path": "<workflow path>", "job": "<job key>"}` or `{"kind": "compose-service", "path": "<Compose file path>", "service": "<service key>"}`. Give a node a unit only when it is exactly that unit; Syzygy never matches by label. Where Syzygy derives a fact of an arrow's exact relation between its two nodes' units, it draws the arrow solid as Observed, in its own words; where its derivation is complete and holds no such fact, check reports a finding to repair. Today only `contains`, from the repository root to a top-level directory, can be witnessed." Every statement above of what brief v14 carries, equals or differs by holds of brief v15 apart from those three differences.

A draft in schema v8 or earlier SHALL be checked and rendered exactly as version 2.16 checks and renders it, the same findings and the same bytes of every page, of `machine.json` and of every record check writes, except that a `field-version` finding stands in place of the `schema` finding at a graph diagram edge's `relation` or a graph diagram node's `unit`; wherever this requirement says that a draft of an earlier schema is checked or rendered exactly as an earlier version checks or renders it, that exception holds too. The provider mode is unaffected: the provider stages' closed draft schema gains neither member, and the rendered bytes the provider golden pins do not change.

A draft in schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v10` (version 2.20) is a schema-v9 draft in every respect but one: it SHALL carry a top-level member `hero`, which is exactly one of two forms. `{"figure": <a diagram's identity>}` names the draft's hero, the one figure the landing page leads with, which SHALL be a diagram of the draft whose `home` is `{"kind": "overview"}` and whose `kind` is one of the six graph kinds, `flow`, `lifecycle`, `state-machine`, `boundary`, `dependency` and `placement`. `{"omitted": <a claim block>}` declares that the draft names no hero, and the block says why: one claim block in a paragraph form the introduction may take (never a presentation element), labelled Inferred or Unknown and never non-normative, its `id` an identity unique among every identity of the draft as any `id` is. The v10 schema document admits in `omitted` exactly the claim-block forms the introduction may take, with any of their labels, so that the label rule is the `hero` finding's alone: a non-normative omission raises that finding and no `schema` finding, while a presentation element, or any other value that is no such claim block, at `$.hero.omitted` is a `schema` finding there. The omission's block is a claim of the overview for every rule of this requirement and of REQ-polaris-generation-035: it is checked, withheld, reviewed, covered, counted, listed in `machine.json` and rendered as the introduction is, and it takes its own entry in the fidelity verdict. Whether the hero shows the project's main parts and what surrounds them, and whether an omission's reason holds, are review judgements, never a check. A hero is a figure under every rule of this requirement for a figure homed on the overview, and neither form adds a label, a source or a read. Every rule this requirement and REQ-polaris-generation-035 state for a schema-v9 draft holds for a schema-v10 draft.

For a v10 draft Syzygy SHALL report these repair findings, each with the literal detail given. A finding of kind `hero` at `$.hero` where `hero` is absent, with the detail `` a v10 draft names its hero figure or declares it omitted with a reason ``; and where it is present but is not an object, or does not carry exactly one of the members `figure` and `omitted` and no other member, with the detail `` `hero` is exactly one of {"figure": <diagram id>} and {"omitted": <claim block>} ``. A finding of kind `hero` at `$.hero.figure` where `figure` is not a string, with the detail `` `hero.figure` names a diagram by its identity ``. A finding of kind `unresolved-reference` at `$.hero.figure` where `figure` is a string naming no diagram of the draft, with the detail `` no diagram has the identity named by `hero.figure` ``. Where `figure` names a diagram, a finding of kind `hero` at `$.hero.figure` where that diagram's `home` is not `{"kind": "overview"}`, with the detail `` <identity>: the hero is a figure homed on the overview ``, and another where its `kind` is not one of the six graph kinds, with the detail `` <identity>: the hero is a figure of a graph kind ``, `<identity>` being the diagram's identity; a diagram that fails both raises both, the home finding first. A finding of kind `hero` at `$.hero.omitted` where the omission's block is non-normative, with the detail `` an omitted hero's reason is an Inferred or Unknown claim block ``. At each of the paths `$.hero` and `$.hero.figure` the `hero` finding stands in place of any `schema` finding the generic schema check would report at that same path; an omission's block that is no well-formed claim block raises the findings any malformed claim block raises, at its path. These findings follow every finding version 2.19 reports for the draft, in the order they are stated here. A draft with such a finding does not proceed to review until a resubmitted revision passes. A hero whose diagram has a disposition other than `produced` raises no finding for that: it renders as the next paragraph states.

On the landing page of a v10 draft the header holds, in this order, the title, any run-stopped banner, the thesis-basis line where version 2.4 renders one, the introduction, which is the dossier's thesis, and the draft's open questions (its `unresolved` items) where it has any, each as version 2.19 renders it there. The region that holds the figures homed on the overview SHALL immediately follow that header whenever this requirement and the draft-layer rules render the introduction, and SHALL open with the hero, before every other figure homed on the overview, which follow in draft order as version 2.2 renders them; so the hero comes after the thesis and any open questions, and before anything else on the page. A named hero renders as that figure renders under version 2.19, carrying on its outermost element one attribute more, `data-hero="figure"`, and appears nowhere else; where its own disposition is not `produced` or it cannot be drawn inertly, the notice a schema-v4 diagram renders in its place stands first in the region and carries that attribute. A declared omission renders first in the region as the element `<div data-hero="omitted">`, carrying no other attribute, no role and no accessible name of its own, which holds the Syzygy-written line `<p class="hero-omitted">The authoring session left out the overview figure, for the reason that follows.</p>`, which carries no label token, citation or anchor, and after it the omission's claim block, rendered as the introduction's block is, in both views. Where the draft layer is withheld, or the run stopped before the draft was written, neither renders and the region renders as version 2.19 renders it. `machine.json` SHALL carry, for a v10 draft only, beside and outside its `reviewStatus` member, a member `hero`, `{"form", "diagram", "rendered"}`: `form` `figure` or `omitted`; `diagram` the named diagram's identity for `figure` and null for `omitted`, and null for either form where the draft layer is withheld, so that no identity from a withheld draft enters the machine form; and `rendered` true exactly where the landing page's region of overview figures opens with an element carrying `data-hero`, which is the hero, its notice or the omission, so `rendered` is true where the notice stands in the hero's place and says nothing of whether the figure was drawn. The omission's block is listed among the claims as any claim of the overview is. The reading ledger of a v10 draft places the omission's block on `index.html` with the introduction and counts it as it counts the introduction; a named hero is a figure homed on the overview and is placed as one.

Brief v16 SHALL issue schema v10; it is brief v15 changed only in its version line, its draft-schema line, which names schema v10 and that schema's digest, and one paragraph placed immediately after the relation-types paragraph brief v15 carries, which reads: "Name the dossier's hero in `hero`: `{"figure": "<diagram id>"}` for one diagram whose `home` is `{"kind": "overview"}` and whose `kind` is `flow`, `lifecycle`, `state-machine`, `boundary`, `dependency` or `placement`, showing the project's main parts and what surrounds them. Syzygy draws it first on the overview, after the introduction and any open questions. Where no such figure would be true, write `{"omitted": <block>}` instead, one inferred or unknown block saying why; the overview shows that reason where the figure would stand. A draft that does neither is refused." Every statement above of what brief v15 carries, equals or differs by holds of brief v16 apart from those three differences.

A draft in schema v9 or earlier SHALL be checked and rendered exactly as version 2.19 checks and renders it, the same findings and the same bytes of every page, of `machine.json` and of every record check writes, except that a `field-version` finding at `$.hero`, with the detail `` `hero` needs draft schema polaris-dossier-local-draft-v10 ``, stands in place of the `schema` finding at that path for a draft that carries `hero`; wherever this requirement says that a draft of an earlier schema is checked or rendered exactly as an earlier version checks or renders it, that exception holds too. No figure of such a draft carries `data-hero`, and its `machine.json` carries no `hero` member. The provider mode is unaffected: the provider stages' closed draft schema gains no `hero`, and the rendered bytes the provider golden pins do not change.

From version 2.22 (the reader's edition) every operator-agent rendering, whatever its draft's schema, puts the subject before the run. The run pages are the pages about the run itself: `understanding.html`, `discovery.html`, `clarifications.html`, `executions.html` and `review.html`, each where the run renders it, then `size-report.html` and `appendix.html`; every other page is on the reading path. Each page's navigation SHALL list, in this order, `index.html` with the word "Overview", `find-by-task.html`, `name-index.html`, each Pole page in Pole order, `contents.html`, each deep dive's page, `glossary.html` and `sources/index.html`, and SHALL end with one collapsible entry, `<details class="about-this-run">`, whose summary reads "About this run" and which holds one list linking every run page the render holds, in the order above; it is open on a page it links and closed on every other page. No run page is linked from a page's navigation outside that entry. The contents page SHALL end with the heading `<h2 id="about-this-run">About this run</h2>`, the sentence "How this dossier was made, apart from what it says about the project." and a list linking every run page in the same order, and SHALL link no run page before that heading. The run pages are rendered as before and are reached from every page, so nothing is deleted, and the appendix stays the last page of the reading order. The main content of `index.html` SHALL hold, in this order: its header; the region of figures homed on the overview, where one renders; the Pole map, where the draft has Poles; the sections the landing page renders, preceded by the contents list version 2.16 places before them where it renders one; the list of sections not generated, where there is one; and the `derived-structure` region. So where no figure is homed on the overview, the Pole map immediately follows the header. A disclosure that qualifies a claim, its Unknown marking and reason, a withheld item's words or a screening outcome, SHALL stay inside that claim's element on the page that makes the claim, and none moves to a run page. Wherever this requirement states that a render's pages, `dossier.json`, `machine.json` or size report equal those an earlier version writes, or makes a change to a byte of one a falsifier, the changes of version 2.22 are excepted: the order of each page's navigation, the fold's class and summary, the contents page's order and its closing heading and sentence, the index's place in the reading order and in the `pages` lists of `dossier.json` and `machine.json`, and the order of the landing page's main content. The provider mode is unaffected: its pages keep their navigation and contents, and the rendered bytes the provider golden pins do not change.

A draft in schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v11` (version 2.23) is a schema-v10 draft in every respect but one: a diagram's `kind` may also be `containment`, a figure kind with a record of its own, which carries the member `groups` in place of `nodes` and `edges` and every other member of a diagram (`id`, `title`, `home`, `kind`, `relationship` and `disposition`) unchanged. `groups` is the list of the figure's top-level groups, one to five, in left-to-right order. Each group is `{"id", "label", "epistemic", "citations", "children"}` and MAY carry `unit`: `id`, `label`, `epistemic` and `citations` as a node's are; `children` the list of the groups drawn inside it, zero to eight, in top-to-bottom order, each a group of the same form; and `unit` one of the six forms a v9 node's `unit` takes, `{"kind": "repository-root"}`; `{"kind": "top-level-directory", "path": <a directory name>}`; `{"kind": "package-manifest", "path": <a repository path>}`; `{"kind": "dependency-name", "name": <a name>}`; `{"kind": "workflow-job", "path": <a repository path>, "job": <a job key>}`; and `{"kind": "compose-service", "path": <a repository path>, "service": <a service key>}`, under every rule version 2.19 states for a node's unit. A top-level group is at level 1 and a child of a group at level n is at level n + 1. A containment holds at most 40 groups in all, nests at most three levels, and nests at least one group in another. The nesting of a group in its parent is the agent's claim that the parent contains it, made by the child group's own claim and carrying the child's marking; no group names another as a reference, so a containment has no `from`, `to` or `lane` member, and Syzygy draws no line between groups, the nesting being the only relation its record states. Wherever this requirement or REQ-polaris-generation-035 states a rule for a diagram's node, it holds for each group: each is a claim with its own identity, label, citations and marking, checked, reviewed, withheld and listed in its figure's list of every component and relationship as a node is, and each appears once in the machine form. The fidelity review SHALL judge a group as read with the group it is drawn in. The v11 schema document defines `children` recursively with no bound on depth, so depth and the total are the findings below alone. A containment figure is never a hero, `hero` naming only a diagram of a graph kind (version 2.20). Every rule this requirement and REQ-polaris-generation-035 state for a schema-v10 draft holds for a schema-v11 draft.

For a v11 draft the `figure-kind` rule of version 2.5 SHALL read a `containment` as taking `groups` and none of `nodes`, `edges`, `participants`, `messages`, `lanes` and `steps`, and every other kind as taking no `groups`, and SHALL report its finding for each member a diagram carries that its kind does not take and for each member its kind requires that it lacks, as that version states. Syzygy SHALL also report, for a containment, these repair findings, each naming the diagram's identity, or `$.diagrams[<index>]` where the diagram has no well-formed identity, as `<diagram>` in its literal detail: a finding of kind `containment-depth` at the path of each group at level 4, with the detail `` <diagram>: a containment nests at most 3 levels; this group is at level 4 ``, a group below level 4 raising no `containment-depth` finding of its own; a finding of kind `containment-size` at `$.diagrams[<i>].groups` for a containment of more than 40 groups, with the detail `` <diagram>: a containment holds at most 40 groups; this one holds <n> ``, `<n>` being the count of every group at every level; and a finding of kind `containment-flat` at `$.diagrams[<i>].groups` for a containment no group of which has a child, with the detail `` <diagram>: a containment nests at least one group in another ``. A group's path is `$.diagrams[<i>].groups[<j>]` for a top-level group and the parent's path followed by `.children[<k>]` for a child. A `groups` list of fewer than one or more than five members, or a `children` list of more than eight, is a `schema` finding at that list, and every other schema fault is reported as it is today. Syzygy SHALL compute `containment-depth`, `containment-size` and `containment-flat` only for a containment whose `groups` list and every `children` list in it have no `schema` finding, so an empty `groups` list, for one, is that `schema` finding alone. For the `unit` of each group at level 1, 2 or 3 Syzygy SHALL report the `unit-reference` finding version 2.19 states for a node's, at the group's path followed by `.unit`, naming the group's identity; a group at level 4 or below raises no `unit-reference` finding. These findings follow every finding version 2.22 reports for the draft, in the order `containment-depth`, `containment-size`, `containment-flat`, `unit-reference`, then the `relation-challenged` findings below, each kind in diagram order and, within a diagram, in the preorder of its groups (a group before its children, children in their order). A draft with such a finding does not proceed to review until a resubmitted revision passes. In a draft of schema v10 or earlier Syzygy SHALL report a finding of kind `field-version` at `$.diagrams[<i>].kind` for a diagram whose kind is `containment`, with the detail `` <diagram>: the kind `containment` needs draft schema polaris-dossier-local-draft-v11 ``, and one at `$.diagrams[<i>].groups` for a diagram that carries `groups`, with the detail `` <diagram>: `groups` needs draft schema polaris-dossier-local-draft-v11 ``; each stands in place of the `schema` finding version 2.22 reports at the same path, and every other finding version 2.22 reports for that draft is kept.

Rule `relation-witness` version 1 SHALL decide, for each produced containment figure of a v11 draft that has no `schema` finding, where the draft holds no `figure-kind`, `containment-depth`, `containment-size` or `containment-flat` finding for that figure, the witness state of each nesting, reading the nesting of a child group in its parent exactly as it reads an edge of a v9 graph diagram whose relation is `contains`, whose source is the parent group and whose target is the child group, and which the draft labels as it labels the child group; every case of the order version 2.19 states applies to it unchanged, a nesting either of whose groups renders as a withheld item being `unknown` (`edge-not-inferred`) as such an edge is, and no other relation is read from a containment. A containment with any of those four findings, or in a draft with a `schema` finding, has none of its nestings decided and raises no `relation-challenged` finding. So a nesting of a `top-level-directory` group in a `repository-root` group is `witnessed` where the structure record's `contains` fact joins their units and renders Observed, and `challenged` where coverage is complete and no such fact exists; a nesting of any other pair of unit kinds, such as a `package-manifest` group in a `top-level-directory` group, is `unknown` (`endpoint-kind-mismatch`), because no row of the rule's eligibility table witnesses it at this version; and a nesting either of whose groups carries no unit is `unknown` (`endpoint-without-unit`). A `workspace-member` fact (version 2.7) states a member pattern and not a member's path, and its recognizer is not in the eligibility table, so no nesting is witnessed or challenged by one at this version. `syzygy dossier check` SHALL report a repair finding of kind `relation-challenged` at the child group's path for each `challenged` nesting, with the detail `` <diagram>: no contains fact joins the units of group <child> and the group it is drawn in, in what rule top-level-unit version 1 derived at the pinned revision; correct the nesting or its groups' units ``, `<diagram>` as above and `<child>` the child group's identity, never a unit's path or name; an `unknown` nesting is never a finding and never advice. Each render decides the states again as version 2.19 states for an edge, the reason `fact-not-current` included.

A containment has its own pure layout, deterministic for the same record, that reads its tree and counts and never a label's width. With n top-level groups in draft order, column i (counting from 0) is 176 px wide at x = 24 + 184 x i, and the drawing's laid-out width is W = 40 + 184 x n px, so 224, 408, 592, 776 or 960 px. A group at level l is a box 176 - 24 x (l - 1) px wide (176, 152 or 128) at x = 12 x (l - 1) px right of its column's left edge. A group with no children is 48 px high; a group with k children is 40 + h1 + ... + hk + 8 x (k - 1) + 12 px high, h1 to hk being its children's heights. A top-level group's top is at y = 24; a group's first child's top is 40 px below that group's top, and each further child's top is 8 px below the foot of the child before it; the drawing's height is H = 48 + the greatest height of a top-level group. A group's label stands in its box's first 40 px, or in its whole box where it has no children, wrapping at 19, 16 or 13 characters at level 1, 2 or 3, each to at most two lines; a longer label ends in `…`, as a graph figure's does, and its whole text stands in the figure's list. Text in the drawing is the 13 px figure text, each box is drawn in its marking's line style, as a graph figure's are, whatever the witness state of its nesting, and no box of a draft is ever drawn solid; the drawing holds no line, arrow or connector between boxes. The drawing passes the inertness check a graph figure's drawing passes, and one that does not renders the notice a graph figure renders in its place. These constants are this version's; a change that alters W, the layout or the phone form's rule below is a change to this requirement.

Syzygy SHALL render a produced containment figure on the page and in the place that a graph figure with the same home takes, in a box of the same width, as a `<figure>` carrying `data-figure-kind="containment"` beside the attributes a graph figure on that page carries, version 2.3's `data-visual-provenance="curated"` among them. It holds, in order, as a sequence or swimlane figure does (version 2.5): its caption; its relationship line, whose eyebrow names its kind; its two forms, in `<div class="figure-forms" data-figure-width="<W>">`, which holds the drawing in `<div class="diagram-scroll figure-drawing">` and then the phone form, `<ul class="figure-outline">`; the legend of the line styles its drawing uses; its tally, `{G} groups in {D} levels, as marked:`, D being the deepest level it reaches, followed, as a graph figure's tally is, by ` {O} observed, {I} inferred, {U} unknown.` counted over its groups, each once, and, where at least one nesting is witnessed, by ` {N} nestings witnessed by Syzygy.`, the noun singular for a count of one; and the collapsed list of every component and relationship, holding the groups in preorder, each item's text being `{label}` for a group at level 1 and `{label} (in {parent label})` for a group below it, each label as rendered, then one item for each witnessed nesting, in preorder of its child group, and then the declarative source. The phone form is an unordered list holding one item per top-level group, in draft order; a group's item is `<li class="{marking}">{label}</li>` where it has no children, and `<li class="{marking}">{label}<ul>{its children's items}</ul></li>` where it has, `{marking}` being the group's mark (`inferred` or `unknown`) and `{label}` its whole label, escaped as HTML text and never wrapped or cut, followed by ` ?` for an Unknown group as in the drawing, and a withheld group's label replaced by the same Syzygy words as in the drawing and the list. The stylesheet sets the list's text at 13 px, indents each nested list 12 px and draws each item with a left border, solid, dashed or dotted by its mark, as the legend's line styles are. The phone form carries no identity, citation, anchor or label token. The pages' stylesheet SHALL display exactly one of the two forms at any width of the `figure-forms` box, with no script, by the container size query version 2.5 states, the drawing while the box is at least W px wide and the phone form while it is narrower, the phone form wrapping within the box (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`). The rules that display one form by that query are carried as version 2.5's paragraph states, as amended, by a page on which a produced sequence, swimlane or containment figure renders its two forms and by no other page; the outline's own rules, its text size, indent and borders, SHALL be carried by a page on which a produced containment figure renders its two forms, and by no other page, so a page whose only such figures are containments carries none of the stepper's rules, and a page with no containment none of the outline's. A witnessed nesting renders as Syzygy's statement of its fact and not as the draft's, in the list and in the machine form only: its item reads `<parent> contains <child>: Witnessed by Syzygy`, `<parent>` and `<child>` being the two groups' labels as rendered, with the evidence text version 2.19 gives a witnessed edge in the DOM text clipped in the reading view, and carries `data-epistemic="observed"`, `data-relation="contains"`, `data-witness-fact`, `data-witness-rule` and `data-witness-rule-version`; it carries no claim anchor, enters none of the draft's counts, and leaves the child group's own claim, box, item and marking unchanged, so no box of a draft is upgraded. The declarative source SHALL be derived from the record alone, by position and never by the draft's identities, one statement to a line, each indented two spaces for each level it is nested and the text ending in a newline: `flowchart TB`, then, for each group in preorder, `subgraph g<k>["<label>"]` and, after its children, `end` where it has children, or `g<k>["<label>"]` where it has none, then one `class` line listing the groups of each mark that has any, inferred before unknown, and the two `classDef` lines, each as a graph figure's source writes them; `<k>` counts from 0 in preorder, an Unknown group's label is followed by ` (unknown)`, and every label is escaped as a graph figure's is. Figure fit SHALL examine every produced containment figure as it examines a sequence or swimlane figure: at each viewport the drawing where W <= C, with D = W and T = 13 px, and otherwise the phone form, whose widths are C and whose text is 13 px, so such a figure fits at 1440 and at 390 and has no entry, and one whose layout cannot be computed, as where the draft holds a `figure-kind`, `containment-depth`, `containment-size` or `containment-flat` finding for it or a count outside its bounds, is listed Unknown. `machine.json`'s `figureStructures` member SHALL also hold an entry for each containment figure rendered, in draft diagram order among the sequence and swimlane entries, and SHALL be carried for a render holding at least one figure of the three kinds and only then: `{"diagram", "kind": "containment", "page", "laidOutPx", "groups": [{"id", "parent", "level"}, ...], "nestings": [{"parent", "child", "witness"}, ...]}`, `groups` in preorder with `parent` null at level 1, `nestings` one per group below level 1 in the same order, and `witness` the `{"state", "reason", "fact", "rule", "ruleVersion"}` version 2.19 gives a v9 edge; labels, marks and citations stay in its claims, each group once. A containment figure is a figure for the reading ledger as any diagram is.

Brief v17 SHALL issue schema v11; it is brief v16 changed only in its version line, its draft-schema line, which names schema v11 and that schema's digest, and one paragraph placed immediately after the hero paragraph brief v16 carries, which reads: "A diagram's `kind` may also be `containment`, which takes `groups` in place of `nodes` and `edges`. Use it to show parts inside parts, such as packages inside a workspace or modules inside a service: list one to five top-level groups in `groups`, left to right, each with `id`, `label`, `epistemic` and `citations` as a node has and `children`, the zero to eight groups drawn inside it, top to bottom, in the same form. Nest at most three levels, at most 40 groups in all, and at least one group in another. Syzygy draws no line between groups: put a group inside another only where the second contains the first, and use a graph for any other relation. A group may carry `unit` as a node may; where both a group and the group it is in carry units, Syzygy checks the nesting as a `contains` arrow, and today only a top-level directory inside the repository root can be witnessed. On a narrow screen Syzygy shows a containment as a nested list." Every statement above of what brief v16 carries, equals or differs by holds of brief v17 apart from those three differences.

A draft in schema v10 or earlier SHALL be checked and rendered exactly as version 2.22 checks and renders it, the same findings and the same bytes of every page, of `machine.json` and of every record check writes, except for the `field-version` findings above in place of the `schema` findings at their paths; wherever this requirement says that a draft of an earlier schema is checked or rendered exactly as an earlier version checks or renders it, that exception holds too. Each sequence, swimlane and graph-kind diagram of a v11 draft SHALL be checked, laid out, measured for fit and rendered byte for byte as the same diagram in a v10 draft, except in the `figure-kind` findings about a `groups` member it carries. The provider mode is unaffected: the provider stages' closed draft schema gains no kind or member, and the rendered bytes the provider golden pins do not change.

A draft in schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v12` (version 2.24) is a schema-v11 draft in every respect but two. First, it SHALL carry a top-level member `model`, `{"elements": [...], "relationships": [...]}`, the one model of the system that its structural figures show: zero to 200 elements and zero to 400 relationships. An element is `{"id", "label", "type", "epistemic", "citations"}` and MAY carry `parent` and `unit`: `id`, `label`, `epistemic` and `citations` as a graph node's are; `type` one of the nine element types `system`, `directory`, `package`, `module`, `service`, `job`, `store`, `external` and `person`; `parent` the identity of the one element it is part of; and `unit` one of the six forms a v9 node's `unit` takes, under every rule version 2.19 states for a node's unit. A relationship is either cited, `{"id", "from", "to", "relation", "label", "epistemic", "citations"}`, a v9 graph edge's members with the same meanings, its ends naming elements; or derived, `{"id", "from", "to", "relation", "basis": "derived"}`, which carries no label, marking or citation because it is no claim of the draft: it asks Syzygy to state the relation from the facts it derives, and Syzygy states it, or says it cannot, in its own words. Each element and each cited relationship is one claim of the draft, with one identity, label, marking and set of citations wherever it is drawn, checked, reviewed, withheld and listed as a graph node or edge is, and appears once in the machine form. Second, a diagram whose kind is a graph kind or `containment` SHALL carry, in place of `nodes` and `edges` or of `groups`, a member `view` that selects from the model: a graph view is `{"elements": [<element identity>, ...], "relationships": [<relationship identity>, ...]}`, one to 40 elements and one to 80 relationships, and a containment view is `{"elements": [<element identity>, ...]}`, two to 40 elements; each list is in the order the figure draws them. A view is one level: it selects elements and relationships of the model and names no other view, level or zoom target, and its figure links to no other figure; from schema v17 (version 2.45) a view may also name its abstraction level and the element it is about, and a figure may carry zoom links to other figures, as version 2.45 states, and "one level" here means only that a view is flat, selecting from the one model and nesting no other view. A sequence or swimlane diagram is unchanged and carries no `view`, and its participants, messages, lanes and steps are not elements of the model. The figure-kind rule of version 2.5, as version 2.23 reads it, SHALL read a v12 graph kind and a v12 `containment` as taking `view` and none of `nodes`, `edges`, `groups`, `participants`, `messages`, `lanes` and `steps`, and a sequence or swimlane as taking no `view`. An element's `type` outside the list, a relationship of neither form or carrying any other member, a derived relationship whose `relation` is none a cited relationship may carry, a view list outside its bounds and a containment view carrying `relationships` are each a `schema` finding at that member. Every rule this requirement and REQ-polaris-generation-035 state for a schema-v11 draft holds for a schema-v12 draft, a view's figure being the diagram its expansion below gives.

For a v12 draft Syzygy SHALL report these repair findings, each with the literal detail given, `<element>`, `<relationship>` and `<diagram>` being the identity named, or the path where it has no well-formed identity. A finding of kind `unresolved-reference` at `$.model.elements[<j>].parent` for a `parent` that names no element, at `$.model.relationships[<j>].from` or `.to` for an end that names no element, and at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.elements[<k>]` or `$.diagrams[<i>].view.relationships[<k>]` for an entry that names no element or no relationship of the model, with the detail `` <identity>: no element of the model has this identity `` or `` <identity>: no relationship of the model has this identity ``, `<identity>` being the identity named. A finding of kind `element-type` at `$.model.elements[<j>].type` for an element whose `unit` stands for another type, the six unit kinds standing for `system` (`repository-root`), `directory` (`top-level-directory`), `package` (`package-manifest`), `external` (`dependency-name`), `job` (`workflow-job`) and `service` (`compose-service`), with the detail `` <element>: an element with a <unit kind> unit is of type <type the kind stands for> ``, and for an element of type `module`, `store` or `person` that carries a `unit`, with the detail `` <element>: an element of type <type> carries no unit ``. The `unit-reference` finding version 2.19 states for a node's unit, at `$.model.elements[<j>].unit`, naming the element's identity. A finding of kind `element-parent` at `$.model.elements[<j>].parent` for an element whose chain of parents, followed from the element one `parent` at a time until it reaches an element with no `parent` or an element it has already reached, the element itself included, takes more than two steps or ends by reaching an element already reached, so that an element naming itself, an element on a cycle of parents and an element whose chain runs into a cycle it is not on are each reported, with the detail `` <element>: an element is part of at most two elements above it and never of itself ``. A finding of kind `relationship-not-derivable` at `$.model.relationships[<j>]` for a derived relationship either of whose ends carries no unit, or whose relation and two unit kinds no row of the eligibility table of rule `relation-witness` at the version this requirement states joins, with the detail `` <relationship>: a derived relationship needs two ends whose units a row of rule relation-witness version 1 joins by its relation ``; at this version that table's only row joins a `repository-root` unit to a `top-level-directory` unit by `contains`. A finding of kind `view-duplicate` at the second and each later entry of a view list that names an identity an earlier entry of the same list names, with the detail `` <diagram>: a view names each element and each relationship once ``. A finding of kind `view-relationship` at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.relationships[<k>]` for a relationship either of whose ends the same view does not select, with the detail `` <diagram>: a view shows a relationship only where it shows both its ends ``. A finding of kind `view-shape` at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.elements` for a containment view whose expansion holds more than five top-level groups or more than eight children in any group, with the detail `` <diagram>: a containment view shows one to five outermost elements and at most eight inside any one ``. A finding of kind `model-unviewed` at `$.model.elements[<j>]` or `$.model.relationships[<j>]` for an element or relationship that no diagram's view selects, with the detail `` <element or relationship>: every element and relationship of the model is shown by at least one view ``, a view counting whatever its diagram's disposition. The `containment-flat` finding version 2.23 states for a containment SHALL be reported for a containment view's expansion, at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.elements`, with the detail version 2.23 gives; `containment-depth` and `containment-size` are never reported for a view, because they cannot arise there: the `element-parent` bound keeps every expansion within three levels and the view list's bound keeps it within 40 groups. Syzygy SHALL compute each finding above only where the members it reads have no `schema` finding: `element-type` for an element whose `type` and `unit` have none; `unit-reference` for an element whose `unit` has none; `element-parent` only where no element's `parent` has a `schema` or `unresolved-reference` finding; `relationship-not-derivable` for a relationship of one of the two forms whose ends resolve; `view-duplicate`, `view-relationship`, `view-shape` and `containment-flat` for a view whose lists have no `schema` finding, `view-shape` and `containment-flat` only where the draft holds no `element-parent` finding and no `unresolved-reference` finding at a `parent` or in that view; and `model-unviewed` only where no view list has a `schema` finding. These findings follow every finding version 2.23 reports for the draft, in this order: the model's elements in list order, each element's findings in the order `unresolved-reference`, `element-type`, `unit-reference`, `element-parent`; then its relationships in list order, `unresolved-reference` then `relationship-not-derivable`; then each diagram's view in diagram order, its findings in the order `unresolved-reference`, `view-duplicate`, `view-relationship`, `view-shape`, `containment-flat`, each kind in list order; then `model-unviewed` in model order; then the `relation-challenged` findings below. A draft with such a finding does not proceed to review until a resubmitted revision passes. In a draft of schema v11 or earlier Syzygy SHALL report a finding of kind `field-version` at `$.model` for a draft that carries `model`, with the detail `` `model` needs draft schema polaris-dossier-local-draft-v12 ``, and one at `$.diagrams[<i>].view` for a diagram that carries `view`, with the detail `` <diagram>: `view` needs draft schema polaris-dossier-local-draft-v12 ``; each stands in place of the `schema` finding version 2.23 reports at the same path, and every other finding version 2.23 reports for that draft is kept.

Rule `relation-witness` version 1 SHALL decide, for a v12 draft with no `schema`, `unresolved-reference`, `element-type` or `element-parent` finding, the witness state, once, of each relationship of the model that the view of a produced graph diagram selects, as version 2.19 decides an edge only in a produced graph diagram, reading a cited relationship exactly as version 2.19 reads a v9 graph edge whose ends are its two elements, and a derived relationship as it reads such an edge that the draft labels Inferred; and, once, of each element's `parent` that the view of a produced containment diagram shows, that is, whose element and parent that view both selects, where the view holds no `view-shape` or `containment-flat` finding, reading it exactly as version 2.23 reads the nesting of a child group in its parent group, as version 2.23 decides a nesting only in a produced containment figure. A `parent` no such view shows, and a cited relationship no such view selects, is decided by no rule and is never a finding: it stays the agent's claim, carried in `machine.json` with `witness` null for a relationship, and reviewed with its element or as its relationship; a derived relationship no such view selects renders nowhere.
