Every case of the order version 2.19 states applies unchanged, a relationship or parent either of whose elements renders as a withheld item being `unknown` (`edge-not-inferred`). `syzygy dossier check` SHALL report a repair finding of kind `relation-challenged` at `$.model.relationships[<j>]` for each `challenged` relationship, cited or derived, with the detail version 2.19 gives a challenged edge, naming the relationship's identity, and at `$.model.elements[<j>].parent` for each `challenged` parent, with the detail version 2.23 gives a challenged nesting, `<diagram>` replaced by `model` and `<child>` by the element's identity; in model order, relationships before parents. An `unknown` state is never a finding and never advice. Each render decides the states again as version 2.19 states for an edge, the reason `fact-not-current` included, and every view that shows a relationship or a nesting shows the one state decided for it.

Syzygy SHALL render a diagram of a v12 draft that carries a `view` exactly as it renders the schema-v11 diagram with the same `id`, `title`, `home`, `kind`, `relationship` and `disposition` whose figure members are the view's expansion, with the exceptions this paragraph states. A graph view's expansion has one node for each element the view selects, in the view's order, carrying the element's `id`, `label`, `epistemic`, `citations` and any `unit`, and one edge for each relationship the view selects, in the view's order; a cited relationship's edge carries its `id`, `from`, `to`, `relation`, `label`, `epistemic` and `citations`. A derived relationship's edge carries its `id`, `from`, `to` and `relation`, as `label` Syzygy's words for its relation, the words version 2.19 gives a witnessed edge (`contains`, `declares a dependency on`, `needs` or `depends on`, and the relation's own name for any other), and no citation; it is no claim of the draft. Where its state is `witnessed` it renders as version 2.19 renders a witnessed edge, in Syzygy's words and with the fact's provenance. Otherwise it renders as the expansion's figure renders an Unknown edge carrying that label: its arrow drawn as an Unknown arrow is, with the label an Unknown edge's label takes in the drawing, and carrying `data-epistemic="unknown"` and `data-relation`; its line in the declarative source the one such an edge takes; and its entry among the figure's entries in `machine.json` the one such an edge takes, labelled `unknown`, its text Syzygy's words, with no citation, and carrying `relation` and `witness` as a v9 edge's entry does and `"basis": "derived"`. Its list item reads `<from> → <to>: Syzygy could not derive this relation (<reason>)`, `<from>` and `<to>` the end elements' labels as rendered, and carries `data-relation` and `data-unknown-reason="<reason>"`, `<reason>` being the state's reason (`challenged` where it is challenged). Witnessed or not, a derived relationship's arrow and item carry no claim anchor, no citation and no entry in the fidelity verdict, it counts in the figure's tally as the state it renders in, and it enters none of the draft's own counts. A containment view's expansion has one group for each element the view selects, carrying the element's `id`, `label`, `epistemic`, `citations` and any `unit`; a group's children are the selected elements whose `parent` is that group's element, in the view's order, and the top-level groups are the selected elements whose `parent` is absent or not selected, in the view's order. An element or cited relationship is drawn in every figure whose view selects it with the same label text, before each layout's own wrapping and cutting, and the same marking and line style, and, where more than one view selects it, carries `data-element="<identity>"` on its box, or `data-relationship="<identity>"` on its arrow, in each figure that draws it, so a figure of elements and relationships no other view selects is its expansion's byte for byte. Its claim renders once, in its first figure: the first figure, in draft diagram order, whose view selects it and that renders as a `<figure>` on a page, a notice standing in a figure's place not counting. That figure carries its list item, claim anchor and citations as the expansion's figure would, and its one entry in the fidelity verdict is judged as read with its model parent, where it has one, whichever figure draws it. In each later such figure its list item instead reads `<label>, as shown in <a href="<href>"><title></a>` for an element of a graph figure or at level 1 of a containment figure, `<label> (in <parent label>), as shown in <a href="<href>"><title></a>` for an element below level 1 of a containment figure, `<parent label>` being its parent's label as that figure renders it, and `<from> → <to>: <label>, as shown in <a href="<href>"><title></a>` for a cited relationship; each label and the title are as the first figure renders them, a withheld item's label being Syzygy's words for it there, escaped as HTML text; `<title>` is the first figure's diagram title; and `<href>` is `#figure-<diagram>` where the first figure is on the same page, and otherwise the first figure's page path written relative to the page holding the item, followed by `#figure-<diagram>` (`index.html` or `deep-dives/<name>.html` from `index.html`, `../index.html` or `../deep-dives/<name>.html` from a page under `poles/` or `deep-dives/`, and so on). Such an item carries no claim anchor and no citation. A relationship Syzygy finds witnessed is no claim of the draft: it renders the arrow and list item version 2.19 gives a witnessed edge in every figure that draws it, and never this form. Where no figure that selects an element or relationship renders, its claim renders nowhere, as a node of a diagram that does not render does. Every count of the draft's claims, the page's one sentence and the appendix's among them, counts an element or cited relationship once, as its one claim; only a figure's tally counts it in each figure that draws it. `machine.json` SHALL carry, for a v12 draft, a member `model`: `{"elements": [{"id", "type", "parent", "views"}, ...], "relationships": [{"id", "from", "to", "relation", "basis", "views", "witness"}, ...]}`, in model order, `parent` null where absent, `basis` `cited` or `derived`, `views` the identities of the diagrams whose view selects it, in diagram order, and `witness` the `{"state", "reason", "fact", "rule", "ruleVersion"}` version 2.19 gives an edge, null for a relationship no rule decides; labels, marks and citations stay in its claims, each element and cited relationship once, and a figure's own entries in `machine.json` are those its expansion's figure carries, each naming the element or relationship by its identity. Figure fit, the reading ledger and the figure's two forms take each view's figure as its expansion's, and the reading ledger locates an advisory whose path begins `$.model.elements[<j>]` or `$.model.relationships[<j>]` at the first diagram, in draft diagram order, whose view selects that element or relationship, and on no page where none does. An element's type is carried in `machine.json` alone and is never drawn.

Syzygy SHALL provide the migration of a draft of schema v10 or v11 to schema v12 as a pure function of the draft's bytes, deterministic and reading nothing else; check and render never apply it, and every draft is checked and rendered in its own schema. The migration sets `schemaVersion` to v12, keeps every other member, and for each graph or containment diagram in diagram order replaces its figure members with a `view`: each node of a graph diagram, in its order, becomes an element with the node's `id`, `label`, `epistemic`, `citations` and any `unit`, and each edge, in its order, a cited relationship with all of the edge's members; each group of a containment, in preorder, becomes an element with the group's members but `children`, and with `parent` naming the group it is drawn in, where it has one. Each element's `type` is the type its unit's kind stands for, or `module` where it carries no unit. Elements and relationships enter `model` in that order, merged by identity; because every identity of a schema-v10 or v11 draft is unique, no two merge, and the view lists each diagram's own elements and relationships in their order. A path into a moved member moves with it: `$.diagrams[<i>].nodes[<k>]` and the path of a group of `$.diagrams[<i>].groups` at any depth become `$.model.elements[<j>]`, and `$.diagrams[<i>].edges[<k>]` becomes `$.model.relationships[<j>]`, `<j>` the position the migration gives that element or relationship, each keeping the rest of its path. For every draft d of schema v10 or v11 that check passes and whose migrated model fits the bounds of the v12 model, check passes the migrated draft, taken as the value the migration returns; its advisories are d's, the same multiset of kind, detail and path, each path moved as above; every `<figure>` element and every notice in a figure's place on every page rendered from it, and every figure entry of its `machine.json`, are byte-identical to those of d, except a figure that draws a withheld-end edge (below) and that figure's entry, and, where d holds no withheld-end edge, so are its figure-fit member and the reading ledger of its check record; and its other bytes differ only in `machine.json`'s `model` member, in a byte that names or digests the draft's own bytes or its schema version, and, where d holds a withheld-end edge, in a byte that renders, lists or counts the relationship it migrates to or its citations. A withheld-end edge (version 2.38) is an edge of a graph diagram of d that the draft does not label Unknown and that is not itself rendered as a withheld item, either of whose end nodes renders as a withheld item. Rule `relation-witness` version 1 decides such an edge of d by the order version 2.19 states, in which only an edge rendered as a withheld item is `unknown` (`edge-not-inferred`), so it may be witnessed; and it decides the relationship the migration gives it by the case version 2.24 states, a relationship either of whose elements renders as a withheld item being `unknown` (`edge-not-inferred`), which rule is kept. So where d holds a withheld-end edge, the figure that draws it, that figure's entry, the figure-fit member and the reading ledger of the migrated draft are the bytes the rules of version 2.24 give it, the relationship carrying `witness` state `unknown` with reason `edge-not-inferred` and rendering as version 2.19 renders an edge that is not witnessed, with the draft's marking, and no parity is stated for them. A nesting of a containment is never such an edge: version 2.23 already decides one either of whose groups renders as a withheld item as `unknown` (`edge-not-inferred`), so its figure keeps the parity. No parity is stated for a draft check does not pass: its findings may differ in kind as well as path, because the model's and the views' bounds and checks are not the diagram's (a containment of six top-level groups, for example, draws a `schema` finding in schema v11 and a `view-shape` finding once migrated). For a draft of schema v10 or v11 that check passes, the migrated model's elements are its graph nodes and containment groups and its relationships are its graph edges, one for one: every identity of such a draft is unique, so the merge by identity joins none, and a nesting becomes an element's `parent`, never a relationship. So its migrated model fits the bounds of the v12 model exactly where its graph nodes and containment groups number at most 200 in all, across every diagram, and its graph edges at most 400 in all. The v10 and v11 schemas and checks bound each diagram and not the draft (up to 20 diagrams, a graph of up to 40 nodes and 80 edges, a containment of up to 40 groups), so a draft check passes may hold up to 800 nodes and groups and 1,600 edges. A draft check passes whose nodes and groups number more than 200 in all, or whose edges more than 400, migrates to a draft that check refuses with a `schema` finding at `$.model.elements` or `$.model.relationships`, as check refuses any v12 draft whose model exceeds those bounds, and no parity is stated for it; the migration itself is unchanged and still yields that draft, and no bound of either schema changes. The parity is stated over the migrated draft as the value the migration returns, check deriving its findings from that value as it derives them from a draft it has read; the migration states no serialization. Because the migration adds members (`model`, a `view` for each graph or containment diagram, each element's `type` and a nested group's `parent`), a migrated draft written to a file may pass the draft's read bound of 4,194,304 bytes (version 2.14) or 500,000 values, which check refuses before it derives any finding, though the draft it was migrated from was within them; no parity is stated for that file.

Brief v18 SHALL issue schema v12; it is brief v17 changed only in its version line, its draft-schema line, which names schema v12 and that schema's digest, and one paragraph placed immediately after the containment paragraph brief v17 carries, which reads: "Describe the system once, in `model`: its `elements`, each with `id`, `label`, `type`, `epistemic` and `citations` as a node has, at most one `parent` (the element it is part of, at most two above it) and, where it stands for a part of the repository, a `unit`; and its `relationships`, each either cited, with `from`, `to`, `relation`, `label`, `epistemic` and `citations` as an arrow has, or derived, with `from`, `to`, `relation` and `basis` `derived` alone, which asks Syzygy to state the relation from what it derives (today only a top-level directory inside the repository root). The types are system, directory, package, module, service, job, store, external and person. Then draw each graph or containment figure as a `view` that lists the identities of the elements and relationships it shows: show an element in every figure that needs it, under the same identity, rather than redrawing it, and show every element and relationship in at least one view. A view shows a relationship only with both its ends; a containment view nests an element inside its parent when it shows both. Sequence and swimlane figures keep their own members." Every statement above of what brief v17 carries, equals or differs by holds of brief v18 apart from those three differences.

A draft in schema v11 or earlier SHALL be checked and rendered exactly as version 2.23 checks and renders it, the same findings and the same bytes of every page, of `machine.json` and of every record check writes, except for the `field-version` findings above in place of the `schema` findings at their paths; wherever this requirement says that a draft of an earlier schema is checked or rendered exactly as an earlier version checks or renders it, that exception holds too. Each sequence and swimlane diagram of a v12 draft SHALL be checked, laid out, measured for fit and rendered byte for byte as the same diagram in a v11 draft. The provider mode is unaffected: the provider stages' closed draft schema gains no member, and the rendered bytes the provider golden pins do not change.

A draft in schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v13` (version 2.26) is a schema-v10 draft in every respect but two optional members, the worth-knowing mark. A claim block labelled Inferred MAY carry `worthKnowing`, a boolean; `true` marks the block as worth knowing, a fact about this project, stated in one of a section's own paragraphs and cited, that an engineer new to the project would not guess from the kind of project it is, and `false` marks nothing. Each item of `poles` MAY carry `worthKnowingNone`, a text of 1 to 8000 characters saying why no block of that Pole's sections carries the mark. The v13 schema document admits `worthKnowing` on the claim-block forms labelled Inferred and on no other form, so that the member on a block of another label, on a non-normative block or on a presentation element, a `worthKnowing` that is not a boolean and an empty `worthKnowingNone` are each a `schema` finding. For a v13 draft Syzygy SHALL report a repair finding of kind `worth-knowing` at the path of the block's `worthKnowing` member, naming the block's identity where it has one, for each block labelled Inferred that carries `worthKnowing: true` and: is not one of a section's own paragraphs, a block at `$.sections[<i>].paragraphs[<j>]`, with the detail `` only a block that is one of a section's own paragraphs carries the worth-knowing mark: not a child, an element's claim, a subsection, a deep dive, the introduction or a route step ``; cites no source, with the detail `` a worth-knowing block states a fact about the project and cites at least one source ``; or carries the judgement mark, with the detail `` a worth-knowing block states a fact about the project, never your own judgement: drop one of the two marks ``. A block that fails more than one raises each, in that order. Schema v13 descends from schema v10, not from schema v11 or v12: the containment kind of version 2.23 and the model and views of version 2.24 are not members of v13, so a v13 draft that carries one has a `schema` finding there, and a later version that joins either to the worth-knowing mark SHALL give that schema, and the brief that issues it, a new number, never v13 or v19.

For a v13 draft, and for no draft of an earlier schema, check's report SHALL carry in its `readerAdvice` a member `worthKnowing`, a list of advisories `{"at", "marked", "detail"}`; like the rest of that advice it sets no outcome, is no finding, and is written into no check record and no rendered page. It counts, for each Pole in the draft's order, the blocks carrying `worthKnowing: true` among the own paragraphs of the Pole's sections, each section counted once, in the first Pole that names it; a draft with no `poles` is one group, at `$.sections`, holding every section. A group that counts none, where its Pole carries no `worthKnowingNone`, gets one advisory with the detail `` <name> marks no block worth knowing: mark one to 3 cited facts a newcomer would not guess from the kind of project this is, or, where none meets that test, say why in its `worthKnowingNone` ``, or, for the group at `$.sections`, which has no place for a reason, `` the draft marks no block worth knowing: mark one to 3 cited facts a newcomer would not guess from the kind of project this is, where any meets that test; a draft with no Poles has no place to say why none does, so this advice stands until one is marked ``, and a group that counts more than three gets one with the detail `` <name> marks <n> blocks worth knowing, past 3: keep the ones a newcomer would least guess and drop the mark from the rest ``, where `<name>` is `the Pole "<label>"`, the Pole's label written as a JSON string, or `the draft`, `<n>` is the count, `marked` is the count and `at` is `$.poles[<index>]` or `$.sections`. Three is a ceiling, never a quota: a group that counts one to three gets no advisory, and a Pole's reason excuses a count of none, never a count above three.

From version 2.37, for a v13, v14 or v15 draft, and for no draft of an earlier schema, check's report SHALL carry in its `readerAdvice` a member `choiceFloor`, the choice floor (rule `choice-floor` version 1): a list of advisories `{"at", "onRecord", "detail"}`. Like the rest of that advice it sets no outcome, is no finding, and enters neither the check record nor its reading ledger. On record are the items of the understanding record's `choices` and `tradeOffs` labelled Inferred; an item labelled Unknown is not. The groups are those of the worth-knowing count: each Pole, at `$.poles[<i>]`, with the sections it names, each section counted in the first Pole naming it; or, for a draft that declares no Poles, the whole draft, at `$.sections`. An item is on record for a Pole when a file one of its citations names is a file named by some citation inside one of the Pole's counted sections, or inside a deep dive whose `sectionId` names one of them, at any depth (a claim's `citations`, a code excerpt's `citation`), files compared by path alone; for a draft with no Poles every item on record is on record for the draft, and an item may be on record for more than one Pole. A group with fewer than three items on record is one advisory, in the order of the groups, with `onRecord` that count and the detail "<name> has <n> choices or trade-offs on record, fewer than 3: where its source shows a decision this part makes, the alternative it passes over or what it costs, record each as a cited Inferred item of the understanding record's `choices` or `tradeOffs` citing a file its sections cite; never record one its source does not show", where <name> is `the Pole ` followed by the Pole's label as a JSON string, or `the draft`; "choices or trade-offs" is "choice or trade-off" where <n> is 1; and a draft with no Poles omits " citing a file its sections cite". A group with three or more is no advisory. A v14 or v15 draft is counted exactly as a v13 draft is. The floor counts what the record holds and is never a quota: it asks for no item its source does not show, and it counts an item whatever the review later finds of it.

On each page of a v13 draft that draws sections, the blocks carrying `worthKnowing: true` among the own paragraphs of the produced sections that page draws, each lifted only where it renders Inferred on the counted fidelity review's support, carries no judgement mark, is no withheld item and matches no secret detector, SHALL render once, in the draft's order, in one callout placed before the page's on-page contents list and its first section, and not again in their section. The landing page's callout holds the why section's marked blocks, or every section's in a draft with no `poles`; a Pole page's holds those of the sections that page draws. The callout is the element `<aside class="callout callout-important worth-knowing" id="worth-knowing" aria-label="Worth knowing"><p class="callout-title"><span class="callout-tone">Worth knowing</span> What a newcomer would not guess</p>…</aside>`, holding after that title, for each marked block, a `<div class="worth-knowing-item">` that holds the block rendered as it renders in its section, with its label, citations, anchors and every disclosure that qualifies it, and then `<p class="worth-knowing-from">In <a href="…">…</a></p>`, whose link names the block's section by its title and leads to it, as `#section-<id>` where the section is on the same page. Each marked block is a claim of the page its callout is on, and `machine.json` names that page for it. A page whose drawn sections mark no block renders no callout, except that the page of a Pole carrying `worthKnowingNone`, or for a Pole with no page of its own the landing page, renders in the callout's place the Syzygy-written element `<aside class="callout callout-note worth-knowing" id="worth-knowing" data-worth-knowing="none" aria-label="Worth knowing"><p class="callout-title"><span class="callout-tone">Worth knowing</span> None found</p><p>The authoring session reports that no fact in this part meets the test of what a newcomer would not guess (Inferred, self-reported).</p></aside>`; the Pole's reason itself is rendered on no page and in no machine form. The callout carries no script and no style of its own beyond the callout classes every page already has. Where the draft layer is withheld, no callout renders and no marked block is moved. A marked block that is not lifted, among them every Unknown and every withheld block and every block of a draft no counted review covers, renders in its section exactly as an unmarked block does, and counts toward no callout; a page whose drawn sections lift none renders no `worth-knowing-item`. A v13 draft that marks no block and gives no reason renders, apart from its schema version, as the same draft in schema v10 renders it.

Brief v19 SHALL issue schema v13; it is brief v16 changed only in its version line, its draft-schema line, which names schema v13 and that schema's digest, and one paragraph placed immediately before its task-routes heading, `## Task routes`, which reads: "Mark, in each Pole, one to three blocks worth knowing: set `worthKnowing` to true on a cited Inferred block, one of a section's own paragraphs, that states a fact about this project an engineer new to it would not guess from the kind of project it is, such as a default, a limit, an ordering or a choice unlike that of similar projects. A marked block cites at least one source and is never a judgement; check refuses one that is not a section's own paragraph, cites nothing or carries the judgement mark. Three is a ceiling, never a quota: where no fact in a Pole meets that test, mark none and say why in that Pole's `worthKnowingNone`, which Syzygy never renders. Syzygy renders the marked blocks of a page in one callout headed "Worth knowing" near its top, each linked to its section, and says on a Pole's page when none was found; check advises a Pole that marks none without a reason, or more than three. A draft with no `poles` counts as one Pole and has no place to say why it marks none." Every statement above of what brief v16 carries, equals or differs by holds of brief v19 apart from those three differences. The inventory session SHALL receive the same request: the inventory brief, from version 2.26 `polaris-dossier-inventory-brief-v4`, issues inventory schema `polaris-dossier-local-inventory-v2`, which is inventory schema `polaris-dossier-local-inventory-v1` with one more entry kind, `worth-knowing`, placed before `other` and glossed in the brief as "a fact it states about itself that an engineer new to the project would not guess from the kind of project it is, such as a default, a limit, an ordering or a choice unlike that of similar projects; look for one to three in each major part of the project, and record none where you find none, never one made to fill a quota". An inventory in schema v1 is still accepted and checked against the v1 kinds alone, so an entry of kind `worth-knowing` in one is a `schema` finding at its `kind`. A `worth-knowing` entry is covered, omitted or uncovered by the inventory-coverage rule exactly as an entry of any other kind is, so that a fact the inventory records and the draft never cites surfaces as an uncovered entry; whether the draft marks it is compared by no rule.

Brief v20 SHALL issue schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v13`. For the same run it SHALL equal brief v19 except for its `polaris-dossier-brief-v20` version line and one paragraph immediately after the existing paragraph under `## The understanding record`, before `## Voice`. That paragraph SHALL read exactly as follows.

For each term in `understanding.terminology`, write one item. Set its `id` to the term as a reader writes it, keeping capitalization and hyphens and replacing each space with one underscore. For example, use `append-only_file` for "append-only file" and `AOF` for "AOF". If the spelling has a literal underscore, the resulting ID is invalid, or the ID is already used elsewhere in the draft, choose a valid, unused ID and keep the exact term in the item's `statement`. For example, use an unused `io` as the ID for "I/O" and write "I/O" in the statement. Omit comma-separated lists. Keep `u-` if it is part of the term, as in `u-boot`, and do not add it as an ID prefix.

The successful issuance report and author brief record SHALL both name that same v20 version. The record SHALL bind the issued brief and schema by their digests. Existing draft ID acceptance, the Index, glossary rendering, the inventory brief, the schema and the provider mode remain governed by their inherited rules. The terminology paragraph is author guidance.

A draft in schema v12 or earlier SHALL be checked and rendered exactly as version 2.24 checks and renders it, the same findings and the same bytes of every page, of `machine.json` and of every record check writes, except that a `field-version` finding stands in place of the `schema` finding at each `worthKnowing` member anywhere in the draft, with the detail `` `worthKnowing` needs draft schema polaris-dossier-local-draft-v13 ``, and at each Pole's `worthKnowingNone`, with the detail `` `worthKnowingNone` needs draft schema polaris-dossier-local-draft-v13 ``; wherever this requirement says that a draft of an earlier schema is checked or rendered exactly as an earlier version checks or renders it, that exception holds too. Its report carries no `worthKnowing` advice and its pages no `worth-knowing` callout. The provider mode is unaffected: the provider stages' closed draft schema gains neither member, and the rendered bytes the provider golden pins do not change.

In the operator-agent mode `syzygy dossier check`, for each draft revision it checks and writes a check record for, SHALL also derive declared manifest facts: what ten versioned recognizers read in the manifest and infrastructure files of the pinned tree that the bound screening policy admits (five since version 2.7, and five more since version 2.14). A declared manifest fact states only what one file declares, a package or module name, a workspace member pattern, a dependency and the section that declares it, a workflow, a trigger it names, a job, and a job a job names as needed, and, since version 2.14, a service a Compose file declares, the image it names, a port it declares and a service it names as one it depends on, a Kubernetes object's kind and name, a Terraform resource's type and name and a Terraform module's name and source, a Make target, and a CMake project and target, and no more: never what any code imports, calls or does at run time, never that a declaration takes effect, resolves or is used, and never a version requirement, a size or an object identifier. It is not a derived structural fact of version 2.3: no page renders it but the unit map and the CI pipeline map of version 2.9 and the infrastructure map of version 2.14, below; it enters no `machine.json` member but their `computedMaps`, no figure but theirs, no brief, and no review packet or published file but as those pages and that member reach the rendered-design packet and the site; and no draft element is upgraded by it. They extend what Syzygy derives about the tree's structure, but they are not among the structure record's members, which stay path-only and unchanged on check's report, and no trait or pack rule reads them; they are a separate record, stored where the structure record is not: the check record that check writes once for the revision, `checks/rev-<n>.json`, carries them as its last member, `manifestFacts`, after `figureFit` and `readingLedger` where present, and no other check record carries them (an inventory check record carries none); the record's format identifier stays `polaris-dossier-check/1`, and check's report carries the member once, as it carries the rest of the check record, except that version 2.9 leaves `mechanism`-class entries out of the report's `figureFit`. They are derived from the pinned tree, its blobs, the bound screening policy and the run's content classes alone, never from the draft, so a draft that does not parse gets them too. A check record without `manifestFacts`, written before this version, states nothing about manifests, and its absence is Unknown.

The five recognizers of version 2.7, each named by its rule id at version 1, and the exact fields each reads; no other field, table, key or directive is read or yields a fact. `npm-package-manifest` reads every blob whose basename is `package.json` as one JSON text (RFC 8259) whose value is an object, and reads that object's members `name` (a string: one `package` fact), `workspaces` (an array of strings, or an object whose member `packages` is an array of strings, no other member of that object being read: one `workspace-member` fact per string) and `dependencies`, `devDependencies`, `peerDependencies` and `optionalDependencies` (each an object whose member values are strings: one `dependency` fact per member, its name the member's name, its section the field's name, its group null). `cargo-manifest` reads every blob whose basename is `Cargo.toml` as TOML v1.0.0, and reads `package.name` (a string: `package`), `workspace.members` (an array of strings: one `workspace-member` per string) and the keys of the tables `dependencies`, `dev-dependencies`, `build-dependencies` and `workspace.dependencies` (one `dependency` per key, its section the table's dotted name, its group null) and of `target.<spec>.dependencies`, `target.<spec>.dev-dependencies` and `target.<spec>.build-dependencies` (section `target.dependencies`, `target.dev-dependencies` or `target.build-dependencies`, group the `<spec>` key); a dependency's name is its key, never the `package` value that renames it. `python-project-manifest` reads every blob whose basename is `pyproject.toml` as TOML v1.0.0, and reads `project.name` (a string: `package`), `project.dependencies` (an array of strings: one `dependency` per string, section `project.dependencies`, group null), `project.optional-dependencies` (a table of arrays of strings: section `project.optional-dependencies`, group the extra's key), `dependency-groups` (a table of arrays: one `dependency` per string element, section `dependency-groups`, group the key; a table element, an include, yields no fact) and `tool.uv.workspace.members` (an array of strings: one `workspace-member` per string); `tool.poetry` and every other `tool` table are not read. The name a requirement string gives is its longest leading run matching `[A-Za-z0-9]([A-Za-z0-9._-]*[A-Za-z0-9])?`, written as declared and not normalised; a requirement string with no such leading run is a `field-type` fault. `go-module-manifest` reads every blob whose basename is `go.mod` line by line under the go.mod grammar: its `module` directive (exactly one: one `module` fact, its name the module path, unquoted where it is quoted) and its `require` directives, single-line or in a block (one `dependency` per required module path, section `require`, or `require-indirect` where the line's comment is exactly `// indirect`, group null); the directives `go`, `toolchain`, `godebug`, `replace`, `exclude`, `retract`, `tool` and `ignore`, single-line or in a block, are skipped, and any other line that is not blank or a comment is a `beyond-reader` fault. `github-actions-workflow` reads every blob whose path is exactly `.github/workflows/<name>.yml` or `.github/workflows/<name>.yaml`, three segments with `<name>` not empty, as YAML 1.2 under the core schema, one document whose root is a mapping, and reads its `name` (a string or absent: one `workflow` fact, its name the string or null), its `on` (a string, a sequence of strings or a mapping: one `workflow-trigger` fact per string or key, its event that string or key), its `jobs` (a mapping: one `job` fact per key, its job the key) and each job's `needs` (a string or a sequence of strings: one `job-needs` fact per string, from the job's key, to the string, whether or not it names a job of the file); under the core schema the key `on` is a string, never a boolean. Every key, name, pattern, event and job a fact carries is the decoded string (a quoted key or scalar without its quotes and with its escapes resolved), never the raw spelling, and is written as declared, never case-folded or normalised. A field the recognizer reads that is present with any other type than the one stated is a `field-type` fault, and so is a key that is not a string where a member is taken from it (an integer YAML key under `jobs` or `on`, say) and a `dependency-groups` element that is neither a string nor a table. The recognizers are pure functions of one admitted body and its path, each in its own module beside the structure record's.

The five recognizers of version 2.14, each named by its rule id at version 1, read declared infrastructure, and the exact fields each reads; no other field, block, directive or command is read or yields a fact. They state declared topology only: what one file declares about services, objects, resources, modules and build targets, never that any of it is deployed, runs, is reachable or is built, and never a value resolved from a variable, an environment, an include, an overlay or another file. `compose-file` reads every blob whose basename is exactly `compose.yaml`, `compose.yml`, `docker-compose.yaml`, `docker-compose.yml`, `compose.override.yaml`, `compose.override.yml`, `docker-compose.override.yaml` or `docker-compose.override.yml` and whose path is not a workflow path, as YAML 1.2 under the core schema, one document whose root is a mapping, as `github-actions-workflow` reads a workflow, and reads its `services` (a mapping each of whose values is a mapping: one `service` fact per key, its service the key) and, in each service, `image` (a string: one `service-image` fact, its service the service's key and its image the string), `ports` (a sequence: one `service-port` fact per element, its service the service's key and its port, for a string element, the string; for a plain scalar that the core schema resolves to an integer, its text as written; and for a mapping element, its member `target` (a string or such an integer, required) and its member `published` (a string or such an integer, optional), written `<published>:<target>` where `published` is present and `<target>` otherwise, no other member of that mapping being read) and `depends_on` (a sequence of strings or a mapping: one `service-depends-on` fact per string or key, from the service's key, to that string or key, whether or not it names a service of the file, the members of a mapping's values not being read); a value such as `nginx:${TAG}` is taken as written, never interpolated. `kubernetes-manifest` reads every blob whose basename ends in `.yaml` or `.yml` after at least one other character, at least one of whose directory segments (never its basename) is exactly `k8s`, `kubernetes`, `kube`, `manifests`, `deploy`, `deployment`, `deployments` or `kustomize`, none of whose directory segments is `templates` (a Helm chart's templates are not YAML), and that is neither a workflow path nor a `compose-file` candidate, as a YAML 1.2 stream under the core schema of one or more documents: a document whose root is null yields nothing; a document whose root is not a mapping (a sequence or a scalar), or is a mapping lacking a member `apiVersion` or a member `kind`, is not a Kubernetes object and yields no fact; and every other document, whose root SHALL be a mapping whose `apiVersion` and `kind` are strings, yields one `kubernetes-object` fact, its kind the `kind` and its name the string `metadata.name`, or null where `metadata` or its `name` is absent (`metadata`, where present, being a mapping); no other member, `apiVersion` included, gives a fact a value, so a file under such a directory that declares no Kubernetes object (a chart's `values.yaml`, say) is Inferred with zero facts. `terraform-configuration` reads every blob whose basename ends in `.tf` after at least one other character, as HCL native syntax, and reads only its top-level blocks: each `resource` block (exactly two labels: one `terraform-resource` fact, its type the first label and its name the second) and each `module` block (exactly one label, and a body attribute `source` whose expression is one quoted string holding no template sequence `${` or `%{`: one `terraform-module` fact, its name the label and its source that string, decoded); every other block, attribute and expression, nested blocks included, is skipped and yields nothing, and a `.tf.json`, `.tfvars` or `.tfstate` file, or a `.terraform.lock.hcl`, is no candidate. `makefile` reads every blob whose basename is exactly `Makefile`, `makefile` or `GNUmakefile` as a makefile in GNU make's syntax, and yields one `make-target` fact for each distinct target an explicit rule names: a rule is a logical line, outside a recipe and a `define` body, that is no conditional-directive line and whose first word is none of the directives its reader reads (listed below), holding a `:` that is neither inside a variable reference `$(...)` or `${...}` nor part of an assignment operator, and before which no assignment operator stands outside a variable reference (a logical line whose first such operator precedes its first such `:`, as `A := b: c`, is an assignment, never a rule), its targets being the words, separated by white space, before that `:`; a word holding `%` or `$`, and a word that begins with `.` and holds no `/` (a special target such as `.PHONY`, or a suffix rule), yields no fact; a rule whose text after its separator (`:` or `::`) holds an assignment operator before any `;` is a target-specific variable assignment and yields no fact; prerequisites and recipes are never read, a recipe line being a logical line that begins with a tab character and follows a rule's logical line or another recipe line, with only blank lines, comment lines, conditional-directive lines and recipe lines between (since version 2.16 a recipe line is a logical line, its continuation lines belonging to it, where version 2.14 said a physical line; and a conditional-directive line so placed does not end that rule's recipe context, so recipe text inside a conditional is never read as a rule); a logical line is one or more physical lines joined where a line ends in a backslash; a blank line is a physical line that is empty or holds only spaces and tabs, and is blank whether or not it begins with a tab; a conditional-directive line is a logical line, not beginning with a tab character, whose first word is `ifeq`, `ifneq`, `ifdef`, `ifndef`, `else` or `endif`, with its arguments; and a line's first word is its first run of characters other than spaces and tabs, so `vpath:` begins with no directive; a target's fact spans the logical line of the first rule naming it, and a target named inside a conditional is declared all the same. `cmake-lists` reads every blob whose basename is exactly `CMakeLists.txt` in the CMake language, and yields, for each command invocation whose name is `project`, one `cmake-project` fact, its name the invocation's first argument, and for each whose name is `add_executable`, `add_library` or `add_custom_target`, one `cmake-target` fact, its target the first argument and its `targetKind` `executable`, `library` or `custom` respectively, command names being compared without regard to ASCII case and an invocation counting wherever it stands, inside an `if`, `function` or `macro` body or not; an argument is taken without a quoted or bracket argument's delimiters and with a quoted argument's escape sequences resolved, and a variable reference or generator expression in it is never expanded. No path is a candidate of two recognizers: a workflow path is a candidate of `github-actions-workflow` alone, and a `compose-file` basename of `compose-file` alone.

The faults of the recognizers of version 2.14 are taken in the order stated below, `duplicate-key`, `syntax`, `field-type`, `beyond-reader`, as every recognizer takes them. For `compose-file` and `kubernetes-manifest` they are the YAML faults stated below, the mappings each reads being, for `compose-file`, its root, `services`, each service, each port mapping and each `depends_on` mapping, and, for `kubernetes-manifest`, each document's root and its `metadata`; a second document is `beyond-reader` for `compose-file`, as for a workflow, and is read by `kubernetes-manifest`; and a `services`, service, `image`, `ports`, port element, `target`, `published` or `depends_on` of another type than the one stated, a port mapping without `target`, a `compose-file` root that is not a mapping (a null root, as of a file of comments alone, included), and an `apiVersion`, `kind`, `metadata` or `metadata.name` of another type, are `field-type`. For `terraform-configuration`, an attribute repeated in a `module` block's body is `duplicate-key`; an unbalanced brace, bracket or parenthesis, an unterminated string, heredoc or comment, an attribute repeated in any other body, and any text that is not a block, an attribute, a comment or white space, are `syntax`; and a `resource` block with other than two labels, a `module` block with other than one, and a `module` block whose `source` is absent or is not one quoted string free of template sequences, are `field-type`; and a block nested deeper than its reader reads (at least 256 levels, below) is `beyond-reader`, its only fault of that kind. For `makefile`, a logical line that begins with a tab character, is no recipe line and is not inside a `define` body (since version 2.16, in place of version 2.14's "a recipe line before the first rule", which no line could be), a `define` with no matching `endef`, an `else`, `endif` or `endef` with nothing open, a conditional still open at the end of the text, an unbalanced variable reference, and a logical line that is none of a comment, a rule, an assignment, a directive and a recipe line and does not begin with `$(` or `${`, are `syntax`; a logical line that begins with `$(` or `${` and is neither a rule nor an assignment (a function call such as `$(eval ...)`, which may declare a rule that no reader sees without expanding it) is `beyond-reader`; an assignment to `.RECIPEPREFIX` is `beyond-reader` too, since it changes which lines are recipe lines; it has no `duplicate-key` or `field-type` fault. For `cmake-lists`, an unterminated quoted argument, bracket argument, bracket comment or parenthesis, and text that is not a command invocation, a comment or white space, are `syntax`, and a `project`, `add_executable`, `add_library` or `add_custom_target` invocation with no argument is `field-type`; it has no `duplicate-key` or `beyond-reader` fault. As for every recognizer, a text beginning with U+FEFF is `syntax`; an empty or whitespace-only `compose-file` or `kubernetes-manifest` candidate is `syntax`, as an empty workflow is, and an empty or whitespace-only `.tf` file, makefile or `CMakeLists.txt` is Inferred with zero facts. A `terraform-configuration` reader SHALL read at least `#`, `//` and `/* */` comments, blocks with identifier or quoted labels nested at least 256 deep, attributes whose expressions run across lines inside brackets, quoted templates holding `${...}` and `%{...}` sequences with strings nested in them, and heredoc templates opened by `<<ID` or `<<-ID`; a `makefile` reader, line continuation by a trailing backslash, `#` comments, the directives `include`, `-include`, `sinclude`, `override`, `export`, `unexport`, `private`, `vpath`, `undefine`, `define` and `endef`, `ifeq`, `ifneq`, `ifdef`, `ifndef`, `else` and `endif`, the assignment operators `=`, `:=`, `::=`, `:::=`, `?=`, `+=` and `!=`, and rules with the separator `:` or `::` and an inline `;` recipe; and a `cmake-lists` reader, the whole grammar of the CMake language: command invocations, bracket, quoted and unquoted arguments with nested parentheses, line and bracket comments, and line continuation in a quoted argument. The YAML reader of version 2.7 SHALL read, for `kubernetes-manifest` alone, a stream of several documents, each with its own lines, and SHALL keep, for a plain scalar it resolves to an integer, its text, which only `compose-file` reads; it refuses an alias, an anchor, an explicit tag and a merge key as `beyond-reader` for these recognizers as for a workflow, so a Compose file that uses an anchor or a merge key (`<<: *defaults`) is Unknown (`unparsed`, `beyond-reader`) at this version. These readings change no fact, fault or byte of `github-actions-workflow`.

A fact of version 2.14 spans, by the span rule below: a `service` fact, its key and value, as a `job` fact does; a `service-image` fact, its `image` key and value; a `service-port` fact, its element's own lines; a `service-depends-on` fact, its element's own lines, or, for a key of a `depends_on` mapping, that key and its value; a `kubernetes-object` fact, its document from the first through the last line holding a character of the document's own content, never its `---` marker or a following blank or comment-only line; a `terraform-resource` or `terraform-module` fact, its block from the line of its type keyword through the line of its closing brace; a `make-target` fact, the physical lines of the first rule's logical line; and a `cmake-project` or `cmake-target` fact, its invocation from the line of its command name through the line of its closing parenthesis. Which of these files are read is, again, the screen's outcome at each check: under declared-source policy 1.0.0 none of these paths is placed, so every named file of these five recognizers is `unknown-extraction-class`; under 2.0.0 a `compose-file` candidate, a `kubernetes-manifest` candidate, a `.tf` file and a file named `Makefile` are admitted as `code-content` unless the policy withholds them on another ground (under a directory whose name carries one of its withheld words, or a vendored directory, for example), and no rule of that policy places `makefile`, `GNUmakefile` or `CMakeLists.txt`, so those are Unknown (`unknown-extraction-class`) and `cmake-lists` yields no fact under either policy. A Terraform variable or state file (`.tfvars`, `.tfstate`) is a denied path under 2.0.0 and a candidate of no recognizer: it is never read, and is counted in the coverage remainder below, never named.

A recognizer's candidates are the blob entries of the pinned tree (modes 100644, 100755 and 120000) whose path its path rule names, from the listing check takes for its census; a submodule or any other entry is never a candidate. Each candidate takes exactly one disposition, decided in this order. A candidate whose path, or any of whose directory prefixes, a secret detector of the bound screening policy matches, read as a path or as text, is counted in its recognizer's `secretPathsLeftOut` and named nowhere. For the four package recognizers and the five of version 2.14, a candidate with a directory segment that the vendored-path or generated-path rule version 1 names, or that the tests-path rule version 1 matches, is counted in `setApart`, never named or read; a workflow path has no such segment by its form, and the tests-path rule is not applied to it. Every other candidate is a named file. A named file is Unknown and never read where its path is longer than 512 UTF-16 code units or carries a code point U+0000 to U+001F or U+007F (`path-not-citable`; its entry gives the path in the census's shown form, cut at 512 code units with the count left out, and every other path in the member is the raw path); where its mode is 120000 (reason `symbolic-link`); where the screen's path outcome is `denied-path` or `unknown-extraction-class` (that word); where the class the screen admits it under is one the run's content classes do not admit, by the class gate the census applies (`class-not-consented`); or where it is past the 250th file of its recognizer (the 100th, for a recognizer of version 2.14) that none of these reasons reaches, in path order (`over-read-limit`). Each remaining file is read in its own call of a pinned object reader opened on the clone check reads cited blobs from, with its object bound set to 1,048,576 bytes, re-hashed on the way down from the pinned commit, never from the working tree: a blob over that bound is refused before it is inflated whole and is Unknown (`too-large`), any other refusal makes that file Unknown (`object-read-refused`), and neither fails another file or the check. Its bytes are then Unknown where they are not valid UTF-8 (`not-utf8-text`); otherwise the screen's body screen runs over the whole text, as it runs over a cited blob, and a body it excludes is Unknown with its outcome word (`secret-detector-match` or `active-content`) and one it retains with lines withheld is Unknown (`redacted-body`; no body of these ten kinds is retained under declared-source policy 1.0.0 or 2.0.0, so this arm is reached only under a screen double or a later policy): only a whole body the screen admits is parsed, and an excluded or withheld body is never parsed or used. A body that does not parse as its recognizer requires is Unknown (`unparsed`) with one `parseFault`, the first of these that applies: `duplicate-key` (a name repeated in an object, table or mapping the recognizer reads, a table header redefining such a table included, though TOML also calls it invalid); `syntax` (not valid in its format, which an empty or whitespace-only `package.json` or workflow is, and so is any file whose text begins with U+FEFF); `field-type` (as above, and a workflow whose root is not a mapping, or a `go.mod` with no `module` directive or more than one) or `beyond-reader` (valid in its format but in a form the recognizer's reader does not read; a reader SHALL read at least comments, blank lines, bare, quoted and dotted keys, standard tables and arrays of tables, basic, literal and multi-line strings, integers, booleans, arrays across lines and inline tables in TOML, and block and flow mappings and sequences and plain, single- and double-quoted and block scalars in YAML, and MAY refuse an alias, an anchor, an explicit tag, a merge key or a second document as `beyond-reader`; which forms a recognizer's reader refuses is part of its rule, so a change to that set is a new rule version). An Unknown file yields no fact and is never reported as declaring nothing; a file that parses and declares none of the fields read (an empty `Cargo.toml` or `pyproject.toml`) is Inferred with zero facts. Before a fact is recorded, each string it takes from the body (its `name`, `pattern`, `group`, `event`, `job`, `from` or `to`, and, since version 2.14, its `service`, `image`, `port`, `kind`, `type`, `source` or `target`; never its `path`, `rule`, `section` or `targetKind`, which the rules above bound) is checked, and a fact one of whose such strings is longer than 256 UTF-16 code units, carries a code point U+0000 to U+001F or U+007F, or is matched by any secret detector the bound screen runs over text given with no path (as it screens agent-written text) is not recorded and is counted in its file's and its recognizer's `valuesLeftOut`. Which files are read is the screen's outcome at each check, never a rule of this requirement: under declared-source policy 1.0.0 none of these paths is placed, so every named file is `unknown-extraction-class`; under its successor 2.0.0 (version 2.6) a `package.json`, `Cargo.toml`, `pyproject.toml` or workflow path is admitted as `code-content` unless the policy withholds it on another ground (under a directory whose name carries one of its withheld words, or a vendored directory, for example), and no rule of that policy places `go.mod`, so every `go.mod` is Unknown (`unknown-extraction-class`) and `go-module-manifest` yields no fact under either policy. A later policy that admits more changes which files are read and nothing else.

The member is `{"format": "polaris-dossier-manifest-facts/2", "state": "derived", "screeningPolicy": {"policyId", "policyVersion", "sha256"}, "recognizers": [...], "facts": [...], "factsNotListed": <count>, "remainder": {...}}`, with the bound policy's identity, or, where the pinned tree cannot be listed, `{"format": "polaris-dossier-manifest-facts/2", "state": "unknown", "why": "the pinned tree could not be listed (<reason>)"}` with the object reader's refusal reason, and no recognizer or fact. `recognizers` holds one entry per recognizer, in the order named above: `{"rule", "ruleVersion", "label": "inferred", "candidates", "secretPathsLeftOut", "setApart", "inferred", "unknown", "valuesLeftOut", "facts", "files": [...], "filesNotListed"}`, where `candidates` equals `secretPathsLeftOut` + `setApart` + `inferred` + `unknown`, `inferred` and `unknown` count all its named files by label, those in `filesNotListed` included, and `facts` counts every fact its files yield, listed or not. `files` holds one entry per named file, first every file that was read, in path order, then every other named file, in path order; the first 250 entries in that order (100, for a recognizer of version 2.14) are listed and the rest counted in `filesNotListed`, so every file read is listed, since no recognizer reads more files than it lists: `{"path", "epistemic", "unknownReason", "parseFault", "facts", "valuesLeftOut"}`, `epistemic` being `inferred` or `unknown`, `unknownReason` null for an Inferred file and otherwise its reason word, `parseFault` null except for `unparsed`, and `facts` and `valuesLeftOut` counts for an Inferred file and null for an Unknown one, never zero. Each fact is `{"id", "fact", "rule", "ruleVersion", "path", "lines": [<first>, <last>], ...members, "epistemic": "inferred"}`, its members in this order by kind: `package` and `module`, `name`; `workspace-member`, `pattern`; `dependency`, `name`, `section`, `group`; `workflow`, `name`; `workflow-trigger`, `event`; `job`, `job`; `job-needs`, `from`, `to`; and, since version 2.14, `service`, `service`; `service-image`, `service`, `image`; `service-port`, `service`, `port`; `service-depends-on`, `from`, `to`; `kubernetes-object`, `kind`, `name`; `terraform-resource`, `type`, `name`; `terraform-module`, `name`, `source`; `make-target`, `target`; `cmake-project`, `name`; and `cmake-target`, `target`, `targetKind`. A path is the raw repository path, escaped only as JSON escapes a string, and "path order" is code-unit order of the raw path. Lines are numbered from 1 as the path-and-range check above numbers them, so `[first, last]` is a range that check accepts as a citation of the fact's path, and a fact's span runs from the first line of what declares it to the last line of its value, the last line that holds a character of the value's own text (for a table, of its last key and value), never a following blank or comment-only line: a key and its value (a `package`, `module`, `job` fact, a `dependency` declared by a key, a `workflow-trigger` named by a mapping key); the element's own lines for an array or sequence element (a `workspace-member`, a requirement string, a `workflow-trigger` or `job-needs` string) and for an `on` or `needs` given as one string; the directive's own line for a `module` or `require` line; for a `dependency` declared by a table header (`[dependencies.serde]`), the header's line through the last line of that table, and, where several dotted keys of one table declare the same dependency, the first through the last of them; and for a `workflow` fact, line 1 through the file's last line. Facts are ordered by path, then first line, then last line, then kind in the order `package`, `module`, `workspace-member`, `dependency`, `workflow`, `workflow-trigger`, `job`, `job-needs`, `service`, `service-image`, `service-port`, `service-depends-on`, `kubernetes-object`, `terraform-resource`, `terraform-module`, `make-target`, `cmake-project`, `cmake-target`, then by each member's value in the order listed, in code-unit order with null first; `id` is `_manifest-<k>`, `<k>` counting from 1 in that order (an identity starting with `_` cannot be a draft identity); the first 1,000 facts are listed and the rest counted in `factsNotListed`. The member is deterministic: its bytes are a function of the pinned tree, the bytes of the blobs read, the screen the run loads (the bound policy's bytes, the placement subject and the visibility) and the run's content classes, so two checks of one run in which the reader refuses no read write byte-identical members whatever their drafts (a refused read can differ between checks, and its file says so), and no clock, environment, locale, concurrency or traversal order enters it. Wherever Syzygy reads a draft check record back (review, render and close taking the latest passed draft revision, the reading difference reading an earlier run's records, the run's end screen counting outcomes, and every other reader), it SHALL read it as one JSON document of at most 16,777,216 bytes and 2,000,000 values, no value nested more than 16 levels below the root (the root at depth 0), and a record past any of those bounds is one that cannot be read, never counted or reported as a passed draft or as a draft check refused. At this version's caps the member, encoded as the check record encodes it, takes fewer than 13,000,000 bytes whatever the tree holds (fewer than 10,000,000 before version 2.14): its widest form has every count a safe integer, every listed fact a `dependency` whose path is 512 code units of three-byte characters and whose `name` and `group` are each 256 lone surrogates, written as six-byte escapes, and every file entry an Unknown `path-not-citable` entry whose census-form path begins with 512 control characters, and, since version 2.14, every place its `remainder` lists named in that census form, the screening policy's identifier and version each charged as 4,096 six-byte escapes. So the member alone never takes a check record past that bound, and the listing caps of version 2.7 are unchanged.

Since version 2.14 the member also states its coverage remainder: which files of the pinned tree no recognizer names as a candidate, so that the record shows what Syzygy took no declared fact from. A file is a candidate where some recognizer's path rule names it, whatever its disposition (read, Unknown, set apart, or counted as secret-matching); the remainder is every other blob entry. `remainder` is `{"files", "secretPathsLeftOut", "candidates", "notCandidates", "places": [...], "placesNotListed", "filesInPlacesNotListed", "notCandidatesInPlacesNotListed"}`: `files` counts the blob entries of the pinned tree (modes 100644, 100755 and 120000), as the structure record's census counts them; `secretPathsLeftOut` those whose path, or any of whose directory prefixes, a secret detector of the bound screening policy matches, by the same test rule `top-level-unit` applies; `candidates` the others that are a candidate of some recognizer, which is the sum, over the recognizer entries, of each entry's `candidates` less that entry's `secretPathsLeftOut`; and `notCandidates` the rest, so `files` = `secretPathsLeftOut` + `candidates` + `notCandidates`. `places` holds one entry for the repository root and one for each top-level directory holding a blob entry that the secret test leaves in, each `{"place", "files", "notCandidates"}`: `place` is null for the root and otherwise the directory's raw first segment, in the census's shown form where it is longer than 512 UTF-16 code units or carries a code point U+0000 to U+001F or U+007F; `files` counts that place's blob entries the secret test leaves in, which equals the count of the `tree-root` fact for the root and of the `top-level-unit` fact of that path for a directory, where the derived record lists it; and `notCandidates` counts those of them that are a candidate of no recognizer. The root comes first, then the directories in code-unit order of the raw segment; the first 250 directories are listed and the rest counted in `placesNotListed`, their `files` summed in `filesInPlacesNotListed` and their `notCandidates` in `notCandidatesInPlacesNotListed`. So the listed `files` and `filesInPlacesNotListed` sum to `files` less `secretPathsLeftOut`, and the listed `notCandidates` and `notCandidatesInPlacesNotListed` sum to `notCandidates`. A remainder names no path below the top level, no file, and no path or directory a secret detector matches; it counts what no rule claimed and never states what those files hold. A member of the format `polaris-dossier-manifest-facts/1`, which check wrote before this version, holds the five recognizer entries of version 2.7 and no remainder; no reader at this version takes either form from a stored check record.

In the operator-agent mode `syzygy dossier check`, for each draft revision it checks and writes a check record for, SHALL also derive lexical import facts (version 2.39): for each TypeScript file of the pinned tree whose whole body the bound screening policy admits, each top-level static import or re-export statement the file's text holds, and the unit its module operand names, collapsed to the `package-manifest` units of version 2.19, a unit being written as the raw path of its `package.json`. An import fact states only that a file's text holds a statement of a form named below whose operand names, by the finite rules below, a file or a package of a unit, or names no unit of the tree; never that the code is compiled, loaded, reached or called, that a compiler, bundler or runtime resolves the operand as these rules do, or that any configuration (a `paths` or `baseUrl` mapping, an `exports` or `imports` map, a loader) applies. Every import fact is Inferred at this version, whatever evidence exists: the label is a constant. Import facts read file bodies, so they are not derived structural facts of version 2.3: the structure record (`derived/rev-<n>.json` and `derived-structure.json`) stays path-only and byte-unchanged, and no trait or pack rule reads them. They are not declared manifest facts either, and the `manifestFacts` member, its fact identities and its coverage remainder are unchanged by them. They are stored in the check record that check writes once for the revision, `checks/rev-<n>.json`, as the member `importFacts`, after `figureFit` and `readingLedger` where present and immediately before `manifestFacts`, which stays the record's last member; no other check record carries them (an inventory check record carries none), the record's format identifier stays `polaris-dossier-check/1`, and check's report carries the member once, as it carries the rest of the check record. At version 2.39 no page, `machine.json` or `dossier.json` member, figure, brief, review packet or published file carries the member or anything taken from it, no draft element is upgraded by it, and no rule of version 2.19 reads it: an `imports` arrow keeps the witness state that version gives it. From version 2.44 rule `relation-witness` version 2 reads it, and a page and `machine.json` carry a corroborating import fact's identity and rule, as that version states; still no draft element is upgraded to Observed by it. The member is derived from the pinned tree, its blobs, the bound screening policy, the run's content classes and the same check's `manifestFacts` member alone, never from the draft, so a draft that does not parse gets it too. A check record without `importFacts`, written before this version, states nothing about imports, and its absence is Unknown. TypeScript is the one language assessed at this version; Python and every other language are unevaluated, and the member says so.

The candidates are the blob entries of the pinned tree (modes 100644, 100755 and 120000) whose path ends in `.ts`, from the listing check takes for its census (a `.d.ts` file is one; a `.tsx`, `.mts` or `.cts` file is not); a submodule or any other entry is never a candidate. Each candidate takes exactly one disposition, decided in this order, as a package recognizer's candidate takes its own under version 2.7. A candidate whose path, or any of whose directory prefixes, a secret detector of the bound screening policy matches, read as a path or as text, is counted in `secretPathsLeftOut` and named nowhere. A candidate with a directory segment that the vendored-path or generated-path rule version 1 names, or that the tests-path rule version 1 matches, is counted in `setApart`, never named or read. Every other candidate is a named file. A named file is Unknown and never read for the reasons of version 2.7, in their order and with their words (`path-not-citable`, `symbolic-link`, `denied-path`, `unknown-extraction-class`, `class-not-consented`), and, where none of those reaches it, where it is past the 2,000th such file in path order (`over-read-limit`). Each remaining file is read in its own call of the pinned object reader version 2.7 opens, with its object bound set to 262,144 bytes: a blob over that bound is refused before it is inflated whole and is Unknown (`too-large`), any other refusal makes that file Unknown (`object-read-refused`), and neither fails another file or the check. Files are read in path order, and where the bytes of a file, added to those of the files read before it, would take the bytes read for this member past 16,777,216, that file is Unknown (`over-read-limit`) and not scanned, and every later file is Unknown for the same reason and not read. A file's bytes are then Unknown where they are not valid UTF-8 (`not-utf8-text`); otherwise the screen's body screen runs over the whole text, as it runs over a cited blob and a manifest file, and a body it excludes is Unknown with its outcome word (`secret-detector-match` or `active-content`) and one it retains with lines withheld is Unknown (`redacted-body`). Only a whole body the screen admits is scanned; an excluded or withheld body is never scanned, used or named beyond its path, and a body the screen admits is never shown: no operand, name or other text of a body enters the member, its counts and the paths and lines below aside. The member reads no body of a file a statement names, and no blob the screen and the class gate would not admit as a cited blob, so it narrows no secret detector and no fail-closed rule of SEC-5. Which files are scanned is the screen's outcome at each check, never a rule of this requirement.

A scanned body is read as TypeScript tokens from its first code point, a leading U+FEFF being whitespace, and its line terminators being LF, CR, CRLF, U+2028 and U+2029. A statement of the grammar begins at a top-level `import` or `export` token, top-level meaning outside every brace, parenthesis, bracket and template substitution, and takes one of these forms, its module operand being one string literal, single- or double-quoted, holding no backslash and no line terminator: `import`, an import clause, `from` and the operand (form `import`, or `import-type` where the clause begins with `type` followed by `{`, `*` or an identifier other than `from`); `import` followed directly by the operand (form `side-effect`); and `export`, optionally `type`, then `*`, `* as` and an identifier, or a brace-delimited export clause, then `from` and the operand (form `re-export`, or `re-export-type` where `type` is written). An import clause is the ECMAScript `ImportClause` production (a default binding; a namespace import, `* as` and a binding; a brace-delimited named-imports list; or a default binding, `,` and one of the other two), with TypeScript's `type` written before the clause or before a named import, a binding being any identifier, `from`, `type` and `as` among them. A statement ends at its operand's closing quote, and a `;` after it is not part of it. These are outside the grammar and counted in `outsideGrammar`, never resolved: a top-level import-equals declaration of a module (`import`, optionally after `export`, then an identifier, `=`, `require` and `(`); an `import` token followed by `(`, wherever it stands outside the texts named next, a type query `typeof import(` among them; an identifier `require` followed by `(`, wherever it stands outside those texts, except where it is preceded by `function` or is the `require` of an import-equals declaration; an `import` or `export` token that is not top level and begins a form above, or an `import` token that is not top level and is followed by an identifier, `{`, `*` or a string literal (an import inside a `declare module`, `declare global` or `namespace` block, for example), counted and never dropped; a top-level `import` token that begins no form above and no import-equals declaration and is followed by neither `(` nor `.`; and a top-level form above whose operand is not such a literal, whose operand is followed by an import-attributes clause (`with` or `assert`, then a brace), or whose operand a rule below calls outside the grammar. No `import`, `export` or `require` token preceded by `.` or `?.` (a property, such as `obj.require(x)`) is a statement or an outside-grammar form; neither is an `import` token followed by `.` (`import.meta`), nor an import-equals declaration whose right side is not `require(` (a namespace alias). A `require` a file binds for itself is still counted where it is called, so `outsideGrammar` can count a form that loads no module, and never leaves one uncounted. Text inside a line comment, a block comment, a string literal, a template literal outside its substitutions or a regular-expression literal is never a statement and never an outside-grammar form, where each such text begins being decided by this token rule alone: a `/` that does not begin a comment begins a regular-expression literal where the significant token before it is none, punctuation or an operator other than `)`, `]`, `}`, `>`, `!`, `++` and `--`, one of the words `return`, `typeof`, `case`, `default`, `do`, `else`, `in`, `instanceof`, `new`, `delete`, `void`, `throw`, `extends`, `break` and `continue`, or a `)` that closes the parenthesised head of `if`, `while`, `for` or `with` (the token before its matching `(` being that word, or `await` after `for`); and it is a division operator where that token is any other identifier or word, a number, a string, the end of a template or regular-expression literal, `]`, or any other `)`. Where the rule cannot tell the two readings apart, the scan does not guess: a `/` that begins no comment after `}`, `>`, `!`, `++`, `--`, `of`, `yield` or `await`, or after an identifier that directly follows `break` or `continue`, is a point where the scan cannot establish a token boundary, as are an unterminated comment, string, template or regular-expression literal and a code point the TypeScript lexical grammar does not allow outside them. At such a point the scan stops and the file is `incomplete`: the statements and outside-grammar forms whose last token ends before that point are kept, and no later text of the file is read for them. A body the scan reads to its end is `scanned`. A statement's lines are the first and last lines holding a code point of it, numbered as the path-and-range check numbers them, so `[first, last]` is a range that check accepts as a citation of its path.

Each statement is collapsed to units, from the tree listing and the `manifestFacts` member alone; no target body is read. A file's unit is the `package.json` entry of the nearest directory enclosing the file, its own directory first and the root last. Where no enclosing directory holds an entry named `package.json`, the file lies in no unit. Where the nearest such entry is not a blob of mode 100644 or 100755, or is one whose path is not citable, whose path or any of whose directory prefixes a secret detector matches, read as a path or as text, whose path the screen's path outcome denies, or which the vendored-path, generated-path or tests-path rule above sets apart, the file's unit is undecidable, as version 2.19 withholds such a `package-manifest` unit. Rule `typescript-relative-import` version 1 takes an operand that is `.` or `..` or begins with `./` or `../`. Such an operand is outside the grammar where it is `.` or `..`, ends in `/` or holds an empty segment; otherwise it is joined to the importing file's directory and normalised by segments, a `.` segment being dropped and a `..` segment removing the segment before it, and it is outside the grammar where that would leave the root. Where a `..` removes a segment whose path the listing holds as a symbolic link (mode 120000), which a file system follows before it applies the `..`, the target is undecidable. Its candidate addresses are that path where its last segment ends in `.ts`; that path with a final `.js` replaced by `.ts` where it ends in `.js`; and `<path>.ts` then `<path>/index.ts` where its last segment holds no `.`; an operand of any other form (one ending in `.json`, `.mjs` or `.css`, for example) is outside the grammar. The rule takes the addresses in that order. An address that the listing does not hold, and none of whose directory prefixes it holds as a symbolic link, is passed over, as is one it holds as a tree or a submodule (mode 040000 or 160000), since a resolver takes no directory for a file named by a `.ts` path; where every address is passed over the target is absent. The rule stops at the first address not passed over: where the listing holds that address, or any of its directory prefixes, as a symbolic link, the target is undecidable, since where the link leads is not read and could change the result; where it holds it as a blob of mode 100644 or 100755, that address is the target where its path is citable, no secret detector matches it or any of its directory prefixes, and the screen's path outcome and the class gate admit it as they would admit a cited blob, and otherwise the target is undecidable. An undecidable target names nothing. A relative statement's target unit is the unit of its target, by the unit rule above. Rule `typescript-package-import` version 1 takes an operand whose first segment is not empty and begins with none of `.`, `/`, `#` and `_`, and whose second segment, where the first begins with `@`, is present and not empty; every operand that neither rule takes (`''`, `'#internal'`, `'/abs/f'` or `'@scope'`, for example) is outside the grammar. Its package name is its first segment, or its first two where the first begins with `@`. Where the `manifestFacts` member is `unknown`, the target is undecidable. Otherwise the rule decides only where the `npm-package-manifest` version 1 entry of that member has `secretPathsLeftOut`, `setApart`, `unknown`, `valuesLeftOut` and `filesNotListed` all zero and the member's `factsNotListed` is zero, and elsewhere the target is undecidable, whatever the listed facts hold, since a package that a left-out, set-apart, Unknown or unlisted `package.json` declares could hold the name: one known declarer is never read as the only one. Where the rule decides, exactly one `package` fact of that recognizer with that `name` makes the target unit that fact's `path`, two or more make the target ambiguous, and none makes the operand name no unit of the tree.

Each statement takes exactly one outcome, the first of these that applies: `unknown` with the reason `source-unit-undecidable` where its file's unit is undecidable, `target-undecidable`, `target-ambiguous` or `target-absent` where its target is so, and `target-unit-undecidable` where its target's unit is undecidable; `no-unit` where its file, or its relative target, lies in no unit; `external` where its operand names no unit of the tree; `same-unit` where its source and target units are one; and otherwise `edge`, from its file's unit to its target unit. An `unknown` statement names no target, no target unit and no identity the screen or a secret detector withheld.
