The member is `{"format": "polaris-dossier-import-facts/1", "state": "derived", "screeningPolicy": {"policyId", "policyVersion", "sha256"}, "rules": [{"rule": "typescript-relative-import", "ruleVersion": 1}, {"rule": "typescript-package-import", "ruleVersion": 1}], "label": "inferred", "languages": [{"language": "typescript", "state": "assessed"}, {"language": "python", "state": "unevaluated"}], "coverage", "candidates", "secretPathsLeftOut", "setApart", "scanned", "incomplete", "unknown", "statements": {"edge", "sameUnit", "external", "noUnit", "unknown"}, "outsideGrammar", "edges": [...], "edgesNotListed", "files": [...], "filesNotListed", "facts": [...], "factsNotListed"}`, with the bound policy's identity, or, where the pinned tree cannot be listed, `{"format": "polaris-dossier-import-facts/1", "state": "unknown", "why": "the pinned tree could not be listed (<reason>)"}` with the object reader's refusal reason and nothing else. `candidates` equals `secretPathsLeftOut` + `setApart` + `scanned` + `incomplete` + `unknown`, the last three counting every named file by its outcome, listed or not; `statements` counts every statement of every scanned or incomplete file by its outcome, listed or not, and `outsideGrammar` every outside-grammar form they hold. `edges` holds one entry per ordered pair of distinct units that some `edge` statement joins, `{"from", "to", "statements"}`, the two unit paths and how many `edge` statements join them, ordered by `from` and then `to` in code-unit order of the raw path. `files` holds one entry per named file, first every scanned or incomplete file and then every other, each in path order, `{"path", "outcome", "unknownReason", "statements", "outsideGrammar"}`, `outcome` being `scanned`, `incomplete` or `unknown`, `unknownReason` null except for an Unknown file, and the two counts null for an Unknown file and otherwise its counts, never a zero for a file not scanned. `facts` holds one entry per statement, `{"id", "rule", "ruleVersion", "path", "lines": [<first>, <last>], "form", "outcome", "from", "to", "unknownReason", "epistemic"}`, `rule` being the rule that took its operand, `from` and `to` its source and target unit paths where its outcome establishes them and otherwise null, `unknownReason` its reason for an `unknown` statement and otherwise null, and `epistemic` `unknown` for an `unknown` statement and otherwise `inferred`; facts are ordered by path, then first line, then last line, then position in the file, and `id` is `_import-<k>`, `<k>` counting from 1 in that order. A path is the raw repository path, escaped only as JSON escapes a string, except that a `path-not-citable` file's entry gives it in the census's shown form, as version 2.7 does. `coverage` is `complete` where `secretPathsLeftOut`, `incomplete`, `unknown`, `outsideGrammar` and the `unknown` statements are all zero, so that every candidate not set apart was scanned to its end, every statement found was decided and no form the grammar leaves undecided was found; otherwise `partial` where some file is `scanned`, or some statement or outside-grammar form of an `incomplete` file was kept; and otherwise `unknown`. A count of zero edges is a claim about the candidates not set apart only where `coverage` is `complete`, and is otherwise never read as the tree having no import between units. The three lists are listed as prefixes, `edges` first, then `files`, then `facts`, each in its order: an entry's size is the UTF-8 bytes it takes in the check record's text, its indentation and a separating comma and newline included, counted as though every entry, the last of its list among them, were followed by both, and entries are listed while the sizes of all entries listed, in the three lists together, stay within 1,900,000 bytes; the first entry of a list that would pass that bound, and every later entry of that list, is counted in its `edgesNotListed`, `filesNotListed` or `factsNotListed`, and the next list is then filled. So the member, encoded as the check record encodes it, takes fewer than 2,000,000 bytes whatever the tree holds, its other members being counts, fixed words and the screening policy's identity, which Syzygy writes; with `manifestFacts`, the two members take fewer than 15,000,000 bytes. The member is deterministic: its bytes are a function of the pinned tree, the bytes of the blobs read, the screen the run loads, the run's content classes and the same check's `manifestFacts` member, so two checks of one run in which the reader refuses no read write byte-identical members whatever their drafts, and no clock, environment, locale, concurrency or traversal order enters it.

Every check record that `syzygy dossier check` and `syzygy dossier inventory-check` write SHALL lie within that read bound (version 2.10). Before writing a record, the step SHALL measure the record as it would write it, by the UTF-8 bytes of its two-space JSON with its one trailing newline, its values and its deepest level, as a reader measures them; where any of the three is past the bound, the step SHALL write the record's bounded form in its place and SHALL NOT write the full record. Apart from `manifestFacts` and, since version 2.39, `importFacts`, no member of a check record has a size bound of its own, and the subject's own bounds do not bound the record: measured by a script built from the stated caps, a draft within its 4,194,304 bytes and 500,000 values, every one of whose quotations verifies, can give a record of at least 19,946,240 bytes (2,191 claim blocks of twenty one-word quotations each, every block identity at its 100-character cap, every quotation found at the end of a blob at the object reader's 1,073,741,824-byte bound in a repository of SHA-256 object identities); an inventory of 2,000 entries of twenty quotations each, within the same bounds, one of at least 16,995,454 bytes; and one cited file that screening retains with lines withheld adds at least 60 bytes per withheld line range, so 263,852 such ranges take a record past the bound alone: one file at that bound whose withheld lines, at most 4,068 bytes each with its newline, alternate with empty lines. The value and depth arms of the bound are not shown to be reachable by any subject within its own bounds (the largest measured record holds 482,069 values at depth 4); they are kept because a reader applies them, and are tested through an injected measure. The bounded form holds, in the full record's order, only the members whose size does not grow with the subject: `format`, `runId`, `revision`, `supersedes`, `pinnedRevision`, `checkedAt`, `outcome`, which is `findings`, the subject's file entry (`draft` or `inventory`), `session`, `findings`, `screeningPolicy`, `credential`, `limits`, for an inventory `counts` and `completeness`, and `label`, each as the full record holds it but `outcome` and `findings`; it holds no `executionFlags`, `quotations`, `excludedContent`, `citedBlobs`, `figureFit`, `readingLedger`, `importFacts` or `manifestFacts`, and its format identifier is the full record's. Its `findings` holds exactly one repair finding, `{"kind": "check-record-bound", "at": "$", "detail": <text>}`, whose detail reads `the check record would take <b> bytes, <v> values and <d> levels below its root, past the read bound of 16,777,216 bytes, 2,000,000 values and 16 levels; it would hold <q> verified quotations, <x> excluded quotations, <c> cited files and <f> other findings, which this record leaves out: quote and cite less, then check again`, each count the full record's. So a draft or inventory whose full record would be past the bound does not pass, whatever its other checks find, and its revision counts against the repair-cycle limit as any revision with findings does. Measured the same way, with every count a safe integer, the session identifier at its 200-character cap, and each of the screening policy's identifier and version, which Syzygy writes, charged as 4,096 six-byte escapes each, and the credential probe's reason at its widest, which names every listed path of a credential list the agent sessions can write and which is read under 65,536 bytes, charged as 65,538 bytes of escapes after its fixed words, the bounded form of a draft check record takes 117,082 bytes, 48 values and 3 levels, and of an inventory check record 121,398 bytes, 51 values and 3 levels, so a bounded form lies within the bound. Check's report for such a revision carries the bounded form as the record it wrote, and, as `findingsLeftOut`, every finding the full record would have held, which is written to no file; it carries no `figureFit` or `mechanismAdvice`, and its `readingDifference` is `unknown` with the words `this check's record was written in its bounded form, which keeps no reading ledger` (version 2.11: version 2.10 computed it as for a draft that does not parse, whose words are untrue of a draft that parsed); the advice it computes from the draft itself (`readerAdvice`, `reviewReach`, `reviewVisibility`, the census and the structure record) is unchanged, and the revision's derived record is written as before. Wherever this requirement says a draft check record carries `figureFit`, `readingLedger`, `importFacts` or `manifestFacts`, that the check record holds every advice entry, or that check's report carries `figureFit` or `mechanismAdvice`, a bounded form and its report are the exception; `run-advice.json` counts a bounded form among the records it read, one with no `figureFit`, and the step log counts the one finding the bounded form holds. A record within the bound is written exactly as version 2.9 writes it.

Every reader of an inventory check record, `inventory/checks/rev-<n>.json`, SHALL read it under the same read bound (version 2.10): Syzygy deciding whether an inventory counts (REQ-polaris-generation-035), and the launched run's supervisor taking the latest inventory revision to learn whether its repair cycles are spent. Every reader of a draft or inventory check record SHALL open its path once, without blocking and following a symbolic link; SHALL refuse, without reading any byte of it, anything whose opened descriptor is not a regular file (a directory, a device or a FIFO) or gives a size larger than 16,777,216 bytes; and SHALL read from that descriptor at most 16,777,217 bytes, a record of which it reads more than 16,777,216 being past the bound. So a device or a FIFO at the path, or a file grown after its size was taken, is a record that cannot be read, and no reader blocks on it, reads past that many bytes or exhausts its memory. The run directory lies within the agent sessions' write reach, so no reader trusts a record's size, values or depth. An inventory check record past the bound, or one that does not parse, is one that cannot be read: the inventory does not count, with the reason `the result of inventory revision <n> cannot be read`, and the supervisor takes the inventory as having no checked revision, never as one passed or one refused at the repair-cycle limit. Before version 2.10 the supervisor read that record with no bound, and Syzygy deciding whether an inventory counts read it under 262,144 bytes, 4,096 values and 8 levels, which an inventory check record of 368 one-word quotations already passes (109,399 bytes and 4,097 values, measured as above with every member check writes), so such an inventory, passed, could never count; under this version it counts wherever the other rules by which an inventory counts let it.

Since version 2.14 the same reader rule SHALL hold for four more files of this requirement, each within the agent sessions' write reach and each under a bound of its own. Check SHALL read the draft it is given under the draft's bound of 4,194,304 bytes: it opens the path once, without blocking and following a symbolic link; refuses, without reading any byte of it, anything whose opened descriptor is not a regular file or gives a size larger than 4,194,304 bytes; and reads from that descriptor at most 4,194,305 bytes, a draft of which it reads more than 4,194,304 being past the bound. Check refuses a draft that is not a regular file with the reason `<path> is not a regular file`, and one past the bound, by its size or by bytes read after its size was taken, with `<path> is larger than the 4194304-byte draft bound`, `<path>` being the path it was given, resolved; such a refusal freezes no revision and writes no check record. Check freezes exactly the bytes it read, so every frozen draft revision, `drafts/rev-<n>.json`, that check wrote takes at most 4,194,304 bytes, and every reader of one SHALL read it by the same rule under that bound: review, render and close taking the latest passed draft revision, which, for a frozen revision that is not a regular file or is past the bound, give the reason `the frozen draft drafts/rev-<n>.json cannot be read (not a regular file)` or `the frozen draft drafts/rev-<n>.json cannot be read (larger than 4194304 bytes)` and take no revision as passed; Syzygy collecting the session identifiers the frozen revisions declare, for an inventory check, a review check and Syzygy deciding whether an inventory counts, for which such a revision is counted as frozen and declares no identifier, as a revision that does not parse declares none today; and the stored-draft re-render measurement, which counts a revision past the bound, or one that is not a regular file when opened, as a revision it could not read, a directory entry that is not a regular file staying counted among the other files of `drafts/` as before. Every reader of the brief record, `brief.json`, SHALL read it by the same rule under 262,144 bytes, the bound under which every one of its readers already parses it (a bound of the readers, not one derived from the brief's writer): check, for which a brief record past the bound or not a regular file is one it cannot read, refusing with `the run holds no readable brief.json: no brief was issued, so the deadline clock has not started and no <noun> is checked`, `<noun>` being `draft` or `inventory`; the reverification each step runs, which refuses it with `declared-changed` and the reason `the brief record exists but it cannot be read (not a regular file); the declared provider binding cannot be checked`, or, past the bound, `the brief record exists but it is not bounded JSON; the declared provider binding cannot be checked`; and every other reader (the readers of its execution rule and the render's run disclosure), which takes it as a brief record it cannot read, as it takes one that does not parse today. Syzygy's reader of `run-advice.json` SHALL read it by the same rule under the check record's bound of 16,777,216 bytes, and a file past that bound, or not a regular file, is read as no run file. Check writes that file with no bound of its own, since it gathers the advice of every revision of the run, so a file past the bound can exist; no check outcome, render, review or close reads it, so the only result this changes is that of the operator's reader. No reader of these four files blocks on its path, reads more than one byte past its bound, or takes a file past its bound, or anything but a regular file, for a draft, a passed revision, a brief record or a run file. This version states the rule for no other file Syzygy reads from the run directory.

These are the bytes version 2.10 changes. A draft or inventory check record whose full form would be past the read bound is written in its bounded form, and that check's report, the run's `run-advice.json` (that record counted as read, where it was unreadable), the step log's findings count and the close record's digests of those files change with it. An inventory whose check record lies past 262,144 bytes, 4,096 values or 8 levels and within the read bound now counts where its other rules hold, so the fidelity packet, the reviews and the render of that run proceed where they stopped. For a render whose draft revision's `derived/rev-<n>.json` holds an entry that is no readable record: the `derived-structure` region's sentence on `index.html` and the directory map's sentence on `maps/directories.html`, `derivedStructure.state` and `computedMaps.directories.state` in `machine.json` (`unusable` for `missing`), the size report's measures of those two pages, and, through them, the rendered-design packet. No other byte changes: every check record within the bound, every page and machine view of every other render, the brief, the draft and inventory schemas, and every provider-mode byte stay as version 2.9 writes them.

These are the bytes version 2.11 changes. Its corrections of the CI pipeline map's lists, of the run file's unreadable records, of `machine.json`'s `pages` list, of the singular sentences and of the figure-fit text state what the code of versions 2.9 and 2.10, and the readable layout, write, and change no byte that code writes. Two of its rules do change bytes. A manifest map whose recognizers count an Unknown file they do not list ends its `Files whose facts are Unknown` list with the count of those files, in place of `Every candidate file not left out or set apart was read for facts.` where the list was empty: so `maps/units.html` or `maps/ci.html` of such a render, the size report's measures of those pages and, through them, the rendered-design packet change. And check's report for a revision whose record is the bounded form carries the bounded form's words in `readingDifference.why`; the report is written to no file. The readable layout's stacking, which this version states, changes the drawing of every graph of two or more layers it lays out: each such draft figure and the derived region's figure on an operator-agent page, with the size report's measures and the rendered-design packet, and the `figureFit` entries, reading ledger counts, check's report and `run-advice.json` of a draft whose graph figures that layout laid out wider than 261 px before it. The map layout's stacking, which this version also states, changes a map figure whose layers would lay out wider than 648 px, which on the CI pipeline map is a job graph of three or more layers: it is stacked where version 2.9's code continued it on two-column bands, so `maps/ci.html` of such a render (that figure's drawing, its `data-figure-width` and the container query the page carries for its width), the size report's measures of that page and the packet change. Wherever this requirement states that a render's pages equal those an earlier version renders, these stacked drawings and the measures they change are excepted. No other byte changes: the brief, the draft and inventory schemas, every check record of a draft with no such figure, `computedMaps`, and every provider-mode byte stay as version 2.10 writes them.

These are the bytes version 2.14 changes. In the operator-agent mode: every draft check record's `manifestFacts` member, its format, its five further recognizer entries and its `remainder`, and, for a tree holding a file of those recognizers that is read, their facts, which take their places in the fact order, so the `id` of a later fact and, under the 1,000-fact listing cap, which facts are listed can change; with it, check's report and any digest of a check record; every render's `derived-maps` line on `index.html` and the map items of `contents.html`, which gain the infrastructure map; the new page `maps/infrastructure.html`; the `coverage-remainder` section of `maps/directories.html`; `machine.json`'s `computedMaps` (its `pages`, `manifests.recognizers`, `manifests.remainder` and `infrastructure`) and its `pages` list, and `dossier.json`'s `pages` list, each gaining the new page; `maps/units.html` and `maps/ci.html` where a fact's `id`, or which facts are listed, changes as above; and the size report's page count and its measures of every page so changed, and, through them, the rendered-design packet. Wherever this requirement states that a render's pages, `dossier.json`, `machine.json`, size report or check records equal those an earlier version renders or writes, these additions are excepted. Its sentence on the reading difference of a draft that does not parse and whose record is the bounded form states what the code of version 2.11 gives, and changes no byte. The reader rule's extension changes no byte for a regular file within its bound; for a file past its bound or not a regular file at the draft's path, a frozen revision's or the brief record's, it gives the refusals and reasons stated above where a reader before this version read past the bound, blocked on the path or gave the open's error code. No other byte changes: the brief, the draft and inventory schemas, the structure record on check's report, `derived-structure.json`, every inventory check record, and every provider-mode byte stay as version 2.12 writes them; the provider mode renders no map page, link, member or remainder.

These are the bytes version 2.39 changes. In the operator-agent mode every draft check record that check writes in its full form gains the member `importFacts`, immediately before `manifestFacts`, whatever the tree holds (a tree with no `.ts` file gives a member with zero candidates and `coverage` `complete`); with it, check's report, the close record's digest of such a record and any other digest of it change. The bounded form of version 2.10 holds no `importFacts`, as it holds no `manifestFacts`, and its bytes are unchanged; a record whose full form this member takes past the read bound is written in that bounded form, by version 2.10, with its one `check-record-bound` repair finding. So a draft whose full record version 2.38 writes within the read bound, and which passes check there, fails check under this version where the member takes that record past the bound: the run's repair cycle counts that check as one with findings, every later step that takes the latest passed draft revision takes another or none, and that check's report (which then carries no `figureFit`), the run's `run-advice.json` (the bounded form counted among the records it read), the step log's findings count and the close record's digests of those files change with it. For every other draft no outcome changes. No other byte changes: the structure record (`derived/rev-<n>.json` and `derived-structure.json`), the `manifestFacts` member, its facts, their identities and its remainder, every inventory check record, the brief, every draft schema, every rendered page, `machine.json`, `dossier.json`, the size report, the review packets, every relation witness state and every provider-mode byte stay as version 2.38 writes them, and the member adds no object read to any other step. A check record written before this version holds no `importFacts` and keeps its bytes and digest: every reader of a draft check record (review, render and close taking the latest passed draft revision, the reading difference reading an earlier run's records, the run's end screen counting outcomes) reads it under the same read bound and takes nothing from `importFacts` at this version, so a record with the member and one without are read alike.

Every declared manifest fact is labelled Inferred, with the provenance of its rule, version, path and lines, and so is every recognizer's entry; an Unknown file is Unknown with its reason. No manifest fact is labelled Observed at this version, whatever evidence exists. A recognizer qualifies for an upgrade only once it has, at a stated commit, golden fixtures, each with expected facts written as literals and never read from the recognizer's code (for each of the three package recognizers a workspace, a single package with dependencies in every section it reads, a malformed file and an empty file; for `go-module-manifest` a module with single-line and block `require` directives, an indirect requirement, a malformed file and an empty file; for `github-actions-workflow` a file of several jobs with `needs` in both forms and `on` in each of its three forms, a malformed file and an empty file; for `compose-file` a file of several services with `image`, `ports` in each of its three element forms and `depends_on` in both forms, a malformed file and an empty file; for `kubernetes-manifest` a stream of several documents, one a Kubernetes object with a name, one without, one empty and one that is no object, a malformed file and an empty file; for `terraform-configuration` a file of `resource` and `module` blocks beside skipped blocks and a heredoc, a malformed file and an empty file; for `makefile` a file of rules, special targets, pattern rules, assignments, target-specific assignments, conditionals and a `define` body, a malformed file, a file of a function call, and an empty file; and for `cmake-lists` a file of a project and targets of the three kinds with quoted, bracket and unquoted arguments, a malformed file and an empty file) and a recorded mutation run: an evidence record under `docs/evidence/` naming its rule and version, the commit it ran at, the SHA-256 of the recognizer's source at that commit, the name and version of any parser package its reader uses, and, for each mutant, its old and new fragment and its outcome, with no mutant surviving unexplained. That record changes no label by existing. The label of a recognizer's facts changes only by a later version of this requirement that names the record, per rule and version, by path and SHA-256, as the currency policy names its bounds; Syzygy computes the label from what that version names, and nothing a draft, a model, an agent session or a file in the run directory carries can set it. The check record lies within the agent sessions' write reach, so no reader at this version takes a manifest fact from a stored check record; a later consumer re-derives the facts, or reads them under that version's own rules.

In the operator-agent mode each render SHALL also publish three computed map pages (version 2.9), and since version 2.14 a fourth, which Syzygy computes from the pinned tree alone and which need nothing from the draft or its author: the directory map, `maps/directories.html`, titled `Directory map`; the unit map, `maps/units.html`, titled `Unit map`; the CI pipeline map, `maps/ci.html`, titled `CI pipeline map`; and the infrastructure map, `maps/infrastructure.html`, titled `Infrastructure map` (version 2.14). No page of a draft may take these paths. Each is a page of the site as every other page is, at reading depth 1: it carries the pages' navigation, the run's one sentence and the link to the appendix, no script and the pages' content security policy, is measured by the size report and is carried in the rendered-design packet as every file of the render is. Its main content is `<section id="computed-map" data-map="<map>" data-reading-level="1" data-topics="">`, `<map>` being `directories`, `units`, `ci` or `infrastructure`, holding, in order: its heading, the page's title; its note, which names on the surface the rules it was computed by and the revision it was computed at (RFC9-38); its coverage line, which states what the map covers and counts, with its reason, everything its rules could not read or did not list, so that no part of its denominator disappears (RFC9-44); and its figures and lists, as each map states below, the lists holding every fact the map rests on, not collapsed, as the figures' text equivalent (RFC7-34). A map's facts come only from Syzygy's own derivations at that render, never from the draft, a model, an agent session or a stored check record; no element of a map is Observed unless the currency rule of version 2.3 makes its fact Observed, and no map element is ever upgraded by a draft or upgrades one. The `derived-structure` region on `index.html` SHALL end, after its figure or its `Not established.` sentence and inside the region, with `<p class="derived-maps">Computed maps: <a href="maps/directories.html">Directory map</a> · <a href="maps/units.html">Unit map</a> · <a href="maps/ci.html">CI pipeline map</a> · <a href="maps/infrastructure.html">Infrastructure map</a>.</p>` (version 2.14 adds the last link), and the first list of `contents.html` SHALL carry, immediately after its `Sources` item, one item linking each map by its title, in that order. The provider mode is unaffected: it renders no map page, line, item or member.

A map figure is `<figure class="computed-map" data-visual-provenance="computed" data-map="<map>">`. Its drawing is made by the renderer's pure graph layout with the readable spacing's box and gap sizes, laid out left to right where its layers lay out at most 648 px wide (two columns) and otherwise stacked top to bottom one box to a row as the readable layout stacks a graph (version 2.11; version 2.9's code continued a longer chain on two-column bands), so it is never laid out in three columns, and it carries no hue and no text of the draft. The figure holds, in order: its caption; its two forms, in `<div class="map-forms" data-figure-width="<W>">`, W being the drawing's laid-out width in CSS pixels, which hold the drawing in `<div class="diagram-scroll map-drawing">` and then the text form, `<ol class="map-list">`; the legend of the line styles its drawing uses, as an operator-agent figure's legend lists them; and its tally, `<b> boxes and <a> arrows, derived by Syzygy: <o> observed, <i> inferred, <u> unknown.`, counting the drawn elements, a count of one taking the singular (`1 box`, `1 arrow`). Every drawn box and arrow carries the identity of the fact it draws, `data-derived-id` for a derived structural fact and `data-manifest-id` for a declared manifest fact, except where a rule below draws a box Unknown with no fact; an Observed element is drawn solid, an Inferred one dashed, and an Unknown one dotted with its label ending `?`. The text form has one item per drawn arrow, in the order the drawing takes them, `<li class="<mark>"><span class="map-head"><from label></span> <arrow label> <to label></li>`, then one per drawn box that no drawn arrow touches, `<li class="<mark>"><span class="map-head"><label></span></li>`, `<mark>` being `observed`, `inferred` or `unknown` and each label the box's or arrow's whole label, escaped as HTML text and never wrapped or cut; it carries no identity, citation, anchor or label token, because each element's claim is its entry in the page's list. The pages' stylesheet SHALL display exactly one form at any width of the `map-forms` box, with no script: the drawing, never scaled, while the box is at least W px wide, and the text form, its text the 13 px figure text wrapped within the box (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`), each item's left border solid, dashed or dotted by its mark, while the box is narrower, by a container size query on that box. A map page carries that query for each W its figures take, and the style rules of the two forms, and no other page carries a map style rule. A map drawing holds at most 25 boxes, so a large map is a sequence of figures, never one shrunk drawing. Taken as figure fit takes a sequence or swimlane figure (where W <= C, the drawing, D = W and T = 13 px; where W > C, the text form, D = C and T = 13 px), every map figure fits at 1440 and at 390. No map figure is a figure of the draft: check's figure fit does not list it, the reading ledger does not count it, and it takes no entry in the fidelity verdict.

The directory map draws the derived structure the region on `index.html` states at that render, from the same readable record and the same re-derivation, with the same marks and reasons, and is complete over the record. Where the region states `Not established.`, the map's section holds its heading and the sentence `Not established. The top-level directories of the tree at revision <full revision> are Unknown at this render: <words>.`, carrying `data-epistemic="unknown"` and `data-unknown-reason`, with the region's reason and words, and no note, coverage line, figure or list. Otherwise its note is the region's note, and its coverage line is `<p class="map-coverage" data-epistemic="<mark>">`, with `data-unknown-reason` where Unknown, reading `Coverage: <F> files in the tree at that revision: <R> at the root; <L> under the <n> top-level directories listed here; <X> under <m> more top-level directories counted and not listed; <S> left out and not named, because a secret detector matches their path; and <G> submodules, not followed. Directories below the top level are not mapped: no rule of this version derives them.`, where every count is the render's own: the re-derivation of the tree at that revision by which the currency rule of version 2.3 judges the record gives F (its `files`), R (its `tree-root` fact's count), n (its `top-level-unit` facts) and L (the sum of their counts), m (its `unitsNotListed`), S (its `secretPathsLeftOut`) and G (its `submodules`), and X = F - R - L - S, which that derivation makes at least zero, so every blob entry of the tree is counted once; a clause whose count is zero is kept, and a count of one takes the singular (`1 file`, `1 submodule`, `1 more top-level directory`, and `under the 1 top-level directory listed here`). No count is taken from the record, which lies within the agent sessions' write reach and whose `files`, `secretPathsLeftOut` and `submodules` the currency rule does not compare. `<mark>` is the mark and reason of the record's facts, which share one currency, where the record's `files`, `secretPathsLeftOut` and `submodules` each equal the re-derivation's; where any differs the line is Unknown with `stale-beyond-currency-bound` and ends `The derived record's counts differ from the tree's at this revision.` Its figures take the record's `top-level-unit` facts in their order, by path, twelve at a time: figure k draws the box `Repository root`, the boxes `<path>/ (<count> files)` of its twelve or fewer facts, and, from the root to each, the arrow labelled `contains` of that directory's `contains` fact, each element carrying its fact's `data-derived-id` and mark, and is captioned `Top-level directories <a> to <b> of <n>, by path`, a and b counting from 1 in that order; a record with no `top-level-unit` fact has no figure. Its list, under `<h2>Every derived fact</h2>`, holds one entry per fact of the record, in its order, each exactly the entry the region's list holds for it. So the directory map names no path or rule that the region could not name, and every count it adds is one the render re-derives from the tree itself.

The directory map's page (version 2.14) SHALL hold in its main content, after its `computed-map` section and whatever that section holds, a second section, `<section id="coverage-remainder" data-reading-level="1" data-topics="">`, which states the render's member's coverage remainder. It holds its heading, `<h2>What no rule read for facts</h2>`, and then, where the render's member is `unknown`, the sentence `Not established. Which files at revision <full revision> no rule read for facts is Unknown at this render: the pinned tree could not be listed.`, carrying `data-epistemic="unknown"` and `data-unknown-reason="source-uncaptured-or-unreachable"`, and nothing else; and otherwise `<p class="map-coverage" data-epistemic="inferred">` reading `Files in the tree at revision <full revision>: <files>. Candidates of a manifest or infrastructure rule, counted on the unit, CI pipeline and infrastructure maps whether read or not: <candidates>. Left out and not named, because a secret detector matches their path: <secretPathsLeftOut>. Candidates of no rule, from which Syzygy took no declared fact, whatever they hold: <notCandidates>. The rules are <rules>, each at version 1.`, `<rules>` being the ten rule ids in the member's order, separated by commas and the last by ` and `, then `<ul class="map-remainder">` holding one item per listed place, `<li data-remainder-place=".">Repository root: files <files>; candidates of no rule <notCandidates>.</li>` for the root and `<li data-remainder-place="<place>"><place>/: files <files>; candidates of no rule <notCandidates>.</li>` for a directory, and, where `placesNotListed` is not zero, a last item, `<li>More top-level directories, counted and not listed: <placesNotListed>; their files <filesInPlacesNotListed>; candidates of no rule among them <notCandidatesInPlacesNotListed>.</li>`. Every count is the render's member's, written as a numeral after its words, so no count takes a singular form; each place is shown as the member shows it, escaped as HTML text. Its items carry no epistemic mark, like a manifest map's count of Unknown files not listed, being Syzygy's counts of what its rules claimed, not claims about the files counted. The section rests on the render's member, never on the derived record, so it is the same whether the directory map states `Not established.` or not; it holds no figure and no `computedMaps.directories.figures` counts it.

The unit map and the CI pipeline map rest on declared manifest facts, which the render SHALL derive itself by the rules of version 2.7 above, from the tree it lists at the pinned revision, the blobs it reads through its pinned object reader, the bound screen it loads and the run's content classes, as check derives the `manifestFacts` member, with the same caps, and SHALL never take from a stored check record, which lies within the agent sessions' write reach. The member so derived is the render's member. Every element and entry either map takes from it is Inferred, as every manifest fact is at this version, and is never drawn solid or labelled Observed: these maps show what manifest files declare, never what any code imports, calls or runs, or that a declaration takes effect. Where the render's member is `unknown`, each of the two maps holds its heading and the sentence `Not established. What the manifest files at revision <full revision> declare is Unknown at this render: the pinned tree could not be listed.`, carrying `data-epistemic="unknown"` and `data-unknown-reason="source-uncaptured-or-unreachable"`, and nothing else. Otherwise each map's note reads `Computed by Syzygy at this render from the manifest files of the tree at revision <full revision> that the bound screening policy <policyId> <policyVersion> admits, read by rule <rules>, each at version 1. That revision fixes every input, so the map holds as of that revision at any instant. What a file declares, never what runs.`, `<rules>` being `npm-package-manifest, cargo-manifest, python-project-manifest and go-module-manifest` for the unit map and `github-actions-workflow` for the CI pipeline map, and its coverage line, `<p class="map-coverage" data-epistemic="inferred">`, reads, for each of its recognizers in the member's order, `<rule>: <c> candidate files; <i> read for facts, <u> Unknown with no facts taken; <s> left out and not named, because a secret detector matches their path; <a> set apart as vendored, generated or test paths; <v> values left out by the value checks; <f> facts.`, with that recognizer entry's `candidates`, `inferred`, `unknown`, `secretPathsLeftOut`, `setApart`, `valuesLeftOut` and `facts`, then `<j> files of <rule> are counted and not listed.` for each of its recognizers whose `filesNotListed` is not zero, and then, where the member's `factsNotListed` is not zero, `<k> facts of the manifest files are counted and not listed, so a figure or list may lack them.`; a count of one takes the singular, exactly `1 candidate file`, `1 value left out by the value checks`, `1 fact.`, `1 file of <rule> is counted and not listed.` and `1 fact of the manifest files is counted and not listed, so a figure or list may lack it.`, the line's other counts (`<i>`, `<u>`, `<s>`, `<a>`) taking no noun and so no singular form (version 2.11). Each fact a map lists is the entry `<li data-manifest-id="<id>" data-manifest-fact="<fact>" data-epistemic="inferred" data-manifest-rule="<rule>" data-manifest-rule-version="1" data-manifest-path="<path>" data-manifest-lines="<first>-<last>">`, whose visible text is the fact in Syzygy's words, then `Derived by Syzygy`, and whose DOM text, clipped in the reading view by the renderer's visually-hidden class and shown in the evidence view, is `Evidence: label Inferred; declared in <path>, lines <first> to <last>; read by Syzygy by rule <rule> version 1 at revision <full revision>.`; the words are `Package <name>.`, `Module <name>.`, `Workspace member pattern <pattern>.`, `Declares a dependency named <name> in <section>.` (with ` (<group>)` before the period where its group is not null), `Workflow <name>.` or `Workflow with no name.`, `Triggered by <event>.`, `Job <job>.` and `Job <from> needs <to>.` Each map's page ends with `<h2>Files whose facts are Unknown</h2>` and a list with one entry per listed Unknown file entry of its recognizers, in the member's order, `<li data-epistemic="unknown" data-unknown-reason="<reason>" data-manifest-path="<path>" data-manifest-file-reason="<word>"><path>: Unknown, no facts taken (<word>).</li>`, whether or not its body was read (a file read and then refused for its bytes is Unknown as one never read is), `<word>` being the entry's `unknownReason`, followed by `, <fault>` for `unparsed`, and `<reason>` its RFC2-24 reason: `excluded-content` for `denied-path`, `unknown-extraction-class`, `not-utf8-text`, `secret-detector-match`, `active-content` and `redacted-body`; `unconsented-source-or-provider` for `class-not-consented`; `source-uncaptured-or-unreachable` for `object-read-refused`; and `mapping-coverage-absent` for `path-not-citable`, `symbolic-link`, `over-read-limit`, `too-large` and `unparsed`; then, where the `unknown` counts of its recognizers sum to more than the Unknown file entries they list, which happens only where a recognizer's `filesNotListed` is not zero, one further item, `<li><d> files whose facts are Unknown are counted and not listed.</li>` (`1 file whose facts are Unknown is counted and not listed.` for one), `<d>` being that difference (version 2.11); like the list's other sentences it carries no epistemic mark, being Syzygy's count of what its recognizers counted, not an Unknown claim of its own. Where the list then has no item, it holds the one item `No candidate file.` where its recognizers have no candidate file, and otherwise `Every candidate file not left out or set apart was read for facts.`, which is then true: every recognizer's `unknown` count equals its listed Unknown entries, none. Version 2.9 gave that sentence wherever no Unknown file entry was listed, which is untrue where a recognizer counts an Unknown file among those it does not list. A path is shown exactly as the member shows it, and a path a secret detector matches is counted and named nowhere. Every name, pattern, event and job a map shows is a value the member holds, which passed its value checks, escaped as HTML text.

The unit map places each listed `package` and `module` fact of the render's member: a fact whose path has one segment belongs to the repository root, and any other to the top-level directory its first segment names; places are ordered the root first, then by that segment in code-unit order. For each place holding at least one such fact, in that order, its figures take its facts in the member's order twelve at a time: each draws the place's box, a box `<name>` for each of those facts, and an arrow labelled `declares` from the place's box to each, and is captioned `<place>: packages and modules <a> to <b> of <n>, as their manifests declare, placed by path`, `<place>` being `Repository root` or `<segment>/`. The place's box is labelled `Repository root` or `<segment>/`; it carries the mark, reason and `data-derived-id` of the derived structural fact for that place in the derived structure the region states (`tree-root` for the root, the `top-level-unit` fact of that path for a directory), and where the region states no such fact it is drawn Unknown with `missing-evidence` and carries no identity. Each package or module box and its arrow carry that fact's `data-manifest-id` and are Inferred. The unit map draws no arrow between two packages or modules: no rule of this version states that a declared dependency names a package of this tree, a name match being a claim that a declaration resolves, which no manifest fact makes; so its coverage line ends `Places are path-derived: each package or module sits under the top-level directory its manifest's path begins with, and may move on refactor. Arrows between packages are not drawn: no rule of this version states that a dependency names a package of this tree.` (RFC9-44's path-derived placement marker). Its list, under `<h2>Every declared fact</h2>`, holds one entry per Inferred file entry of its four recognizers, in the member's file order, `<li data-manifest-path="<path>" data-epistemic="inferred"><path>: <f> facts.<ul>...</ul></li>`, `<f>` being the entry's `facts` (`<path>: 1 fact.` for one), holding every listed fact of that file in the member's order, or, for a file of zero facts, `<path>: no fact of the fields read.` with no inner list.

The CI pipeline map takes the render's member's `github-actions-workflow` facts. For each Inferred file entry of that recognizer, in the member's file order, it SHALL hold `<h2><path></h2>`; then `<ul class="map-workflow">` holding, as its items, the entry of its `workflow` fact and, for its `workflow-trigger` facts, each fact's entry, or `<li>No trigger is declared.</li>` (a list, never a `<p>`: each entry is an `<li>`, whose start tag closes an open `<p>` in an HTML parser, so version 2.9's `<p>` form could not hold its entries (version 2.11)); then, where the file has from 1 to 24 listed `job` facts, one figure, captioned `<path>: jobs and what each needs, as declared`, that draws a box `<job>` for each `job` fact in the member's order and, for each `job-needs` fact of the file whose `to` is the `job` of a `job` fact of that file, an arrow labelled `needs` from the box of its `from` to the box of its `to`; and then, only where the file has at least one listed `job` or `job-needs` fact, `<ul class="map-facts">` holding the entries of those facts in the member's order (version 2.11), where a `job-needs` fact whose `to` names no job of the file draws nothing and its entry's words are `Job <from> needs <to>, which names no job of this file.` A file with more than 24 listed `job` facts has no figure and, in its place, the note `<n> jobs are declared: too many to draw in one figure, so they are listed below.`; a file with no `job` fact has no figure, and a file with neither a listed `job` nor a listed `job-needs` fact has no list. An arrow states only what `needs` declares: never that the jobs run, in what order, or on which event.

The infrastructure map (version 2.14) takes the render's member's facts and file entries of the five recognizers of version 2.14, and rests on that member by every rule the paragraph before the unit map's states for the unit map and the CI pipeline map: its `Not established.` sentence where the member is `unknown`, its note, its coverage line with its singular forms, its fact entries, and its closing list of Unknown files with its count of Unknown files not listed, its `No candidate file.` and its `Every candidate file not left out or set apart was read for facts.`. It differs in three things: its `<rules>` are `compose-file, kubernetes-manifest, terraform-configuration, makefile and cmake-lists`; its note and its `Not established.` sentence say `infrastructure files` where those of the unit map and the CI pipeline map say `manifest files`; and its coverage line ends `No figure is drawn: no rule of this version draws one from these facts.` It holds no figure. Its list, under `<h2>Every declared fact</h2>`, holds one entry per Inferred file entry of its five recognizers, recognizer by recognizer in the member's order and each recognizer's files in the member's order, in the unit map's form (`<path>: <f> facts.`, `<path>: 1 fact.`, or `<path>: no fact of the fields read.` with no inner list), each holding the entries of that file's listed facts in the member's order. Its facts' words are `Service <service>.`, `Service <service> names image <image>.`, `Service <service> declares port <port>.`, `Service <from> depends on <to>.`, `Kubernetes object of kind <kind> named <name>.`, or `Kubernetes object of kind <kind> with no name.` where its name is null, `Terraform resource <type> named <name>.`, `Terraform module <name> from source <source>.`, `Make target <target>.`, `CMake project <name>.` and `CMake <targetKind> target <target>.` Every entry and count it shows is Inferred, as every declared fact is at this version: it states what files declare, never what is deployed, runs, is reachable or is built; a figure drawn from these facts is a later version's.

`machine.json` SHALL carry, beside `derivedStructure` and outside `reviewStatus`, a member `computedMaps`: `{"pages": ["maps/directories.html", "maps/units.html", "maps/ci.html", "maps/infrastructure.html"], "directories": {"state", "figures"}, "manifests": {"state", "screeningPolicy", "recognizers", "factsNotListed", "remainder"}, "units": {"figures", "facts", "files"}, "ci": {"figures", "facts", "files"}, "infrastructure": {"figures", "facts", "files"}}` (version 2.14 adds the fourth page, `manifests.remainder` and `infrastructure`), where `directories.state` is `derivedStructure`'s `state`, whose `facts` are the directory map's; `manifests` holds the render's member's `state`, `screeningPolicy` (null where `unknown`), `recognizers` (each entry without its `files`; empty where `unknown`) and `factsNotListed` (null where `unknown`); `manifests.remainder` holds the member's `remainder` exactly (null where `unknown`); `units.facts`, `ci.facts` and `infrastructure.facts` hold the member's listed facts of each map's recognizers and `files` those recognizers' `files` entries, each exactly as the member holds it and in its order, empty where `unknown`; and each `figures` counts that map's figures, which for the infrastructure map is 0. Two renders of one draft revision in which the reader refuses no read compute the same maps and publish the same map pages and `computedMaps`. These are the bytes version 2.9 adds to or changes in a render: the three map pages; the `derived-maps` line that ends the region on `index.html`; the three map items on `contents.html`; `computedMaps` in `machine.json`; the three map pages' entries in the `pages` lists of `dossier.json` and of `machine.json`; `size-report.json` and `size-report.html`, whose page count, their entries for the three pages and their measures of `index.html` and `contents.html` change; and, through them, the rendered-design packet. Wherever this requirement states that a render's pages, `dossier.json`, `machine.json` or size report equal those an earlier version renders, or makes a change to a page byte a falsifier, these additions are excepted, `computedMaps` and the three entries of `machine.json`'s `pages` list among them (version 2.11 names the latter, which version 2.9's code writes).

From version 2.42 each render in the operator-agent mode SHALL also derive declared test names: what four versioned recognizers read in the test files of the pinned tree that the bound screening policy admits, each named by its rule id at version 1. Their candidates are blob entries named by path alone: for `pytest-test-name`, a basename ending `.py` that begins `test_` or ends `_test.py`; for `js-test-call`, a basename ending one of `.js`, `.jsx`, `.mjs`, `.cjs`, `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.mts` and `.cts` whose stem before that ending ends `.test` or `.spec` and is longer than it, or which lies under a directory segment `__tests__`; for `go-test-function`, a basename longer than `_test.go` that ends with it; and for `junit-test-method`, a basename ending `.java` whose stem begins `Test` or ends `Test` or `Tests`. No path is a candidate of two. Each recognizer reads a body lexically, after replacing every comment and the interior of every string literal by spaces and keeping every line break, so that a name written inside a comment, a docstring or a string is never a name: `#` comments and Python string literals with any prefix of r, b, u and f in single, double or triple quotes; `//` and `/* */` comments, quoted strings, and, for JavaScript and TypeScript, template literals and regular-expression literals, for Go, raw strings in backquotes, and, for Java, text blocks. A body that ends inside a comment or a string, or in which a line break comes inside a quoted string that cannot hold one (any but a Python triple-quoted string, a template literal, a Go raw string and a Java text block), is Unknown `unparsed` with the parse fault `unterminated`. The replacement keeps the offset of every UTF-16 code unit, so a character outside the Basic Multilingual Plane in a comment or a string moves no later name or line. `pytest-test-name` takes, at any indentation, each `def` or `async def` whose name begins `test` (kind `test`) and each `class` whose name begins `Test` (kind `suite`); `js-test-call` takes each call of `describe`, `fdescribe`, `xdescribe`, `context` or `suite` (kind `suite`) or of `it`, `test`, `fit`, `xit` or `specify` (kind `test`), each optionally through any of the modifiers `only`, `skip`, `todo`, `concurrent`, `fails` and `sequential`, that is no member of another object and whose first argument is a string literal holding no substitution, no line break and no escape but a backslash before a backslash or a quotation mark, that literal's value being the name; `go-test-function` takes each `func` at the start of a line whose name is `Test` or is `Test` followed by a character that is not a lower-case letter and whose one parameter is of type `*testing.T`; and `junit-test-method` takes the name of each method carrying an annotation `Test`, `ParameterizedTest`, `RepeatedTest`, `TestFactory` or `TestTemplate`, simple or qualified, with or without arguments. A name is what one file declares, never a claim that a test runs, passes or pins a behaviour; no other part of a test, its body, its assertions or its arguments, is read into the member.

Each candidate takes the disposition, in the order, that the manifest recognizers give theirs, with three differences: a path that rule `vendored-path` or `generated-path` names is counted in `setApart` and named nowhere, and no candidate is set apart for being a test path; each recognizer reads and lists at most 250 files; and a name the value checks refuse (empty, longer than 256 characters, holding a control character, or matched by a secret detector) is left out and counted in its file's `valuesLeftOut`. The render SHALL hold the result as the member `testNames`, of format `polaris-dossier-test-names/1`: `{"format", "state": "derived", "screeningPolicy", "unitRule": {"rule": "top-level-unit", "ruleVersion": 1}, "recognizers", "units", "unitsNotListed", "names", "namesNotListed"}`, or `{"format", "state": "unknown", "why"}` where the pinned tree cannot be listed. Each recognizer entry holds the members a manifest recognizer entry holds, with `names` in place of `facts`, and so does each file entry. Each name is `{"id", "name", "kind", "rule", "ruleVersion", "path", "line", "unit", "epistemic": "inferred"}`, `line` being the 1-based line of the name in its file under the line rule that ends a line at CRLF, CR or LF, and `unit` the first segment of its path, or null for a file at the root, as rule `top-level-unit` places files. The first 2000 names in order of path, line and name, code unit by code unit, are listed with the identities `_test-name-<k>`, and `namesNotListed` counts the rest. `units` holds one row `{"unit", "files", "names", "byRule"}` for each unit holding a file read for names, the root first and then the directories in code-unit order, the first 250 listed and `unitsNotListed` counting the rest: the files read for names there, every name taken there whether listed or not, and those names per rule. Every name and every entry is labelled Inferred, whatever evidence exists, under the upgrade rule the manifest facts follow; this version names no record that changes the label. The render derives the member itself, as it derives the manifest facts; check does not write it, and no reader takes it from a stored record.

Where a render holds both the derived structure and the member, it SHALL publish one reference page, `reference/test-names.html`, titled `Test names`, linked in the reading order and on `contents.html` immediately after the computed maps, and opening with a legend of its own: the view switch a map page's legend carries, and what `Derived by Syzygy` and `Not established.` mean there. Its section `<section id="test-names" data-reading-level="1" data-topics="">` holds: a note naming the revision, the bound screening policy, the four rules each at version 1, that a rule reads names alone and skips every comment and string, that a name is never a claim that a test runs or passes, and rule `top-level-unit`; a coverage line giving, for each recognizer, its candidate files, the files read for names, the files Unknown, the paths left out because a secret detector matches them, the paths set apart, the names the value checks left out, and its names; under `<h2>Per unit</h2>` a table with one row per listed unit and the column headers `Unit`, `Files read`, `pytest`, `JavaScript and TypeScript`, `Go`, `JUnit` and `All names`, the root named `Repository root` and a directory `<dir>/`, whose caption says how many units it lists and how many it counts and does not list, or, where no file was read for names, the sentence `No test file was read for names, so no unit has a row.`, followed, where any candidate file is Unknown, by a `Not established.` sentence giving their number and saying that what they declare is counted in no row, so each count is of the files read and never a total (VIS-2: a file not read is Unknown, never zero names); under `<h2>Every name, by unit</h2>` each listed name grouped by unit in the table's order, in Syzygy's words `Test <name>, in <path> at line <n>.` or `Suite <name>, in <path> at line <n>.`, marked `Derived by Syzygy` and carrying in its DOM text its label Inferred, its path and line, its rule and version, the revision, and that it is a declared name, never a claim that the test runs or passes; and under `<h2>Files whose names are Unknown</h2>` each listed Unknown file, opened by `Not established.`, with its reason and the RFC2-24 reason the manifest maps give that reason. Where the member is `unknown` the section holds only its heading and a `Not established.` sentence saying that which test names the test files at that revision declare is Unknown at this render because the pinned tree could not be listed. `machine.json` SHALL carry, outside `reviewStatus`, a member `testNames` holding `"page": "reference/test-names.html"` and the member's own members exactly. A render without the member or the derived structure, and every provider-mode render, publishes no such page, link or member, so its bytes are those the replaced version renders.

Each item of check's advice to the author, each reader advisory and each figure-fit entry, has one class (version 2.9): `author`, where a change to the draft can clear it, or `mechanism`, where no change to the content of the draft's figures can and the remedy lies in Syzygy's renderer, layout, stylesheet or schema. At this version every reader advisory is `author`, and a figure-fit entry is `mechanism` in exactly two cases and `author` otherwise: an entry `{"diagram", "unknown": <reason>}` whose reason is that no figure layout was supplied to the check (rule `no-figure-layout`, version 1); and an entry with numbers for a graph-kind figure (any figure of a draft in schema v6 or earlier, or one of the six graph kinds in a v7 draft) whose `columnPx` C is less than 648 and whose `laidOutPx` W is at least 648 (rule `graph-without-narrow-form`, version 1). Only a graph figure's entry carries numbers, since a figure of the other v7 kinds is drawn at the column's width and never fails, so the class is a function of the entry alone. 648 px is the width of a drawing laid out in two columns at the readable spacing (two 24 px margins, two 200 px boxes and one 200 px gap), the least a layout at the readable spacing that draws a graph in columns gives a figure with an arrow whose two ends lie in different columns, and the rule's threshold whatever layout is supplied: no change confined to the figure's graph members makes such a layout draw such a figure within a narrower column, and the one change open to the author, restating its content as a `sequence` or `swimlane`, is advice about the renderer's forms, not the figure's content, so routing it to the run is this version's judgment. So at this version's column widths every entry at 390 of a graph figure laid out in two or three columns is `mechanism`. Since version 2.11 the readable layout, which the `syzygy` binary supplies to check and with which an operator-agent render draws a draft's graph figures and the derived region's figure, stacks every graph whose layers would lay out wider than 261 px, which is every graph of two or more layers, top to bottom one box to a row: a chain or a fan-out is laid out 248 px wide, and a graph within the draft schema's bounds (at most 40 boxes and 80 arrows) less than 648 px wide. It lays out no graph in two or three columns. So, with that layout, rule `graph-without-narrow-form` matches no entry of a graph within those bounds: it fires only where check is supplied another layout that draws a graph in columns, as a test may supply, or for a graph past those bounds, and it is kept so that such an entry still goes to the run and not to the author. Version 2.9's further reason, that the author could reshape a graph until the layout stacked it, no longer holds: every readable graph of two or more layers is stacked already. Check's report, the packet the author repairs a draft from, SHALL carry only `author`-class advice: its `figureFit` is the member the check record holds with every `mechanism`-class entry left out of `entries` and `examined` unchanged, and it SHALL carry `mechanismAdvice`: `{"file": "run-advice.json", "thisRevision": <count>, "inRun": <count>}`, the number of `mechanism`-class entries in this revision's member and the run file's `occurrences`, or, where that file could not be written, `file` null and `inRun` the count this check computed for it. The check record is unchanged: its `figureFit` holds, and its `readingLedger` counts, every entry, so no advisory is dropped, and the report's entries and `thisRevision` together are the record's entries. The report's `readingLedger` and `readingDifference` stay the record's and keep counting every entry, `mechanism` ones included: they measure what the reader meets, which those figures are part of, and a difference compares records with records; so a page's `notFittingAt390` may count an entry the report's `figureFit` leaves out, which `mechanismAdvice.thisRevision` accounts for. After writing its check record, each draft check SHALL write the run file `run-advice.json` in the run directory, in place of any earlier one (an inventory check writes none), as JSON with two-space indentation and one trailing newline: `{"format": "polaris-dossier-run-advice/1", "runId", "rules": [{"id": "no-figure-layout", "version": 1}, {"id": "graph-without-narrow-form", "version": 1}], "checkRecords": {"read": <count>, "unreadable": <count>}, "occurrences": <count>, "advisories": [...]}`, computed from every draft check record `checks/rev-<n>.json` of the run, each read under the read bound above: a record past it, one that does not parse, and one that is not the check record of its revision (one JSON object whose `format` is `polaris-dossier-check/1` and whose `revision` is the `<n>` of its file name) is counted in `unreadable` and contributes nothing (version 2.11 states the last), and a record without `figureFit` is counted in `read` and contributes nothing. `advisories` holds one `{"rule", "advice": <entry>, "revisions": [<n>, ...]}` per distinct pair of rule and entry, the entry compared as a JSON value, its revisions ascending, ordered by its first revision and then by the entry's place in that revision's member; `occurrences` is the total length of the `revisions` lists. So each `mechanism` advisory is written once for the run, however many revisions repeat it, with how often it occurred. The file depends only on the run's check records; it is Inferred advice for the operator and Syzygy's maintainers, gates nothing, is read by no check outcome, render, review, close or reading difference, and enters no page, packet or published file. A check that cannot write it keeps its outcome and its record.

`syzygy dossier check` SHALL also compute inventory-coverage advice for every draft that parses, and SHALL carry it on its report as the member `inventoryCoverageAdvice`; check SHALL write it to no file: not the check record, the step log, a packet or a rendered page. It tells the authoring session, before any reviewer is asked, which entries of the independent inventory (REQ-polaris-generation-035) no block of its draft covers. It is advice: it SHALL NOT be a repair finding, SHALL NOT change the check's outcome, its exit status or any refusal, and no gate, verdict validation or readiness reads it; a draft that passed before version 2.12 passes with it. A check of an inventory computes none, and its report carries no such member. The classes of version 2.9 are those of reader advisories and figure-fit entries: this advice carries no class member, each entry it lists being one a change to the draft clears, it is on the report beside the `author`-class advice, and nothing of it enters `run-advice.json`. A failure to compute it, at any point and for any cause, SHALL NOT refuse or fail a check: every such failure is the Unknown form below, and this sentence governs wherever another seems to give the compared form.

The inventory compared is the one a fidelity packet built at the instant of that check would carry, which this requirement calls the inventory of record: the highest-numbered checked inventory revision, and only if its check passed (an earlier revision that passed never counts while a later one has findings), its frozen bytes equal to the digest that check recorded, and counting under every condition REQ-polaris-generation-035 sets for an inventory to count: a declared session identifier that no frozen draft revision declares, the recorded launch form of the session it was written in, and the statement that session was handed over under still in force. Where one counts and its frozen bytes are one JSON object, the advice SHALL be `{"state": "compared", "rule": {"id": "overlap-or-path-named", "version": 2}, "inventory": {"revision", "sha256", "label": "Inferred", "basis"}, "counts": {"entries", "covered", "omitted", "uncovered"}, "uncovered": [...], "omissions": {"rows", "recorded", "duplicated", "unmatched", "alsoCovered"}, "note"}`; `inventory` names the revision and the digest of the bytes read, labelled Inferred and never Observed because the run directory is within the agent sessions' write reach, with that reason as its `basis`. In every other case (no inventory counts, the frozen inventory cannot be read or is not the bytes counted, it is not one JSON object, or reading or comparing fails in any other way) the advice SHALL be `{"state": "unknown", "why", "note"}` with no list and no count: coverage that was not compared is Unknown, never zero uncovered entries (VIS-2). `why` is Syzygy's own sentence naming the case; it carries nothing of any inventory entry, and where no inventory counts it gives the reason REQ-polaris-generation-035's conditions give.

Rule `overlap-or-path-named` version 2 decides, for each entry of the inventory, in the inventory's order, exactly one of covered, omitted and uncovered. It reads the two documents alone and no file of the repository. An entry is covered when (a) one of its citations and one citation of the draft's reading content name the same path, compared code unit by code unit, and their line ranges, each inclusive of both ends, share at least one line, or (b) the string in the `text` member of a claim block of the draft contains, as a plain substring compared code unit by code unit, a path one of the entry's citations names; a path found inside a longer path so counts, and an empty path is never so found. The draft's reading content is every citation under its `introduction`, `sections`, `diagrams`, `deepDives` and `unresolved` members and, from version 2.15, its `taskRoutes` member, a code excerpt's one citation among them; the citations of the understanding record, the discovery account and the clarifications are the authoring session's ledger and cover nothing. The claim blocks are those this requirement checks as claim blocks: the introduction, every section and deep-dive paragraph and child, every claim a presentation element carries and, from version 2.15, every step of a task route; an understanding item is not one, and an element's short labels are not searched. A citation whose `startLine` and `endLine` are not two integers from 1, the first not after the second, counts as its whole file. A member of either document that is not of the shape its schema gives is read as empty. An entry that is not covered is omitted when an object of the draft's `inventoryOmissions` names its identifier in `entryId` and carries a `reason` that is a non-empty string, and uncovered otherwise; an object without both records nothing. Because the rule reads no file, an entry whose every cited span screening withholds, which a fidelity verdict marks `withheld` and which blocks nothing, is decided like any other and may be listed. `counts` gives the number of entries and how many are covered, omitted and uncovered, and those three sum to `entries`. Version 2 of the rule is version 1, which version 2.12 stated, with the citations under `taskRoutes` added to the reading content and a route step added to the claim blocks: a route step is shown to the reader and reviewed as any claim block is. For a draft without `taskRoutes` the two versions decide every entry alike.

`uncovered` SHALL list every uncovered entry, in the inventory's order, as `{"entryId", "paths", "unnamedPaths"}`: the entry's identifier (null only where the frozen document gives the entry none, which an inventory that passed its check never does); the distinct paths its citations name, in the entry's own order; and the number of distinct paths left out of `paths`. The advice SHALL carry no other member of any entry: not its statement, kind, label, Unknown reason, quotations, citation identifiers or line ranges. That is all the clause promises: an identifier is the inventory session's own wording and may describe its entry, and the outcomes of successive checks can narrow where in a file an entry points, each such check being a draft revision counted against the repair-cycle limit. A path that a secret detector of the bound screening policy matches, read as a path or as text and whether or not a denied-path rule also names it, SHALL NOT be named: it is left out of `paths` and counted in `unnamedPaths`. An entry that cites nothing is covered by no citation and is listed with no path. `omissions` reports the draft's omission record against this inventory, by identifier alone, over the objects that record something: `rows`, the number of those objects; `recorded`, the number of distinct identifiers they name; `duplicated`, the list of identifiers more than one of them names, in the record's order of second mention; `unmatched`, the list of those that are no entry of this inventory, in the record's order of first mention; and `alsoCovered`, the list of those naming an entry the rule finds covered, in the inventory's order; such an entry counts as covered. The `note` is Syzygy's own text: it SHALL say that the advice sets no outcome and is not stored, what the rule compares, that only identifiers and paths are given, how an omission is recorded, that an entry the draft does represent needs no record even while it is listed, and that the rule is mechanical, so a listed entry may be represented in words the rule cannot see and an entry not listed may still be judged unrepresented. The fidelity review, the verdict's `inventoryCoverage` and readiness are unchanged (REQ-polaris-generation-035), and the packet's criteria change only by the one statement the next paragraph requires: the advice decides nothing a review decides, and the report member is named `inventoryCoverageAdvice` so that it is never read as the verdict's member.

A draft in schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v5` or a later version MAY carry a top-level member `inventoryOmissions`, the authoring session's justified-omission record: a list of at most 2000 objects `{"entryId", "reason"}` with no other member, `entryId` a string of 1 to 100 Unicode code points matching `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_.:-]*$`, the form of a draft identity, and `reason` a string of 1 to 8000 Unicode code points in the session's own words, saying why a reader of the dossier does not need the entry. It records for one inventory entry the outcome the fidelity reviewer may give it as `justified-omission`; it is for an entry the draft leaves out, and an entry the draft represents takes no record, whether or not the rule above lists it. The member adds no schema version, because it is inert: it is optional, no label, citation, quotation or finding rests on it, and it is never rendered, so a v5 or later draft without it is checked and rendered exactly as before version 2.12. From version 2.12 the identifiers of schema v5 and of every later schema therefore each name a schema with this optional member, the schema file a brief issues describes it, and the digest of that file, which the brief states, differs from the digest a brief issued before version 2.12 states, under an unchanged brief version and with no other change to the brief. Check SHALL accept the member in a v5 or later draft whichever of the two schema files its run was briefed with. An `entryId` is not an identity of the draft: Syzygy SHALL NOT count it among the draft's identities or resolve it at check, since an inventory may not yet count or may be revised, and a repeated `entryId` is no finding; the advice above reports the ones that match no entry. A member of any other shape is a schema repair finding, and the member in a draft of schema v2, v3 or v4 is a `field-version` repair finding. The record takes no label, citation or quotation, and Syzygy checks no claim in a `reason` and verifies no text in one as a quotation: a `reason` is the authoring session's unverified statement. Syzygy SHALL NOT render it: no page, source page, machine view or other file of a rendered site carries the record or any `reason`. The fidelity packet carries the draft with the record, as it carries every member but the discovery account, so the review session reads the author's reasons; each entry's disposition stays the reviewer's own judgement, the record binds none, and an entry the author omitted may still be found unsupported. The fidelity packet's criteria SHALL tell the review session, in their coverage criterion, that the draft may carry the record, that each `reason` is the author's unverified statement and settles nothing, that the reviewer judges each omission against the packet alone, and that the changed-block list does not show a change to the record; the criteria are otherwise those of the predecessor. The changed-block list of REQ-polaris-generation-035 compares claim blocks, inventory entries and the understanding record and nothing else, so it does not show an added or changed record. The provider mode is unaffected.

From version 2.32 a draft in schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v13` MAY carry a top-level member `questionKinds`, the authoring session's declaration of which kinds of newcomer question its blocks answer: a list of at most 2000 objects `{"blockId", "kinds"}` with no other member, `blockId` a string of the form of a draft identity, and `kinds` a list of one to six distinct values of the six question kinds, in any order: `purpose` (what the project is for), `structure` (how its parts fit), `mechanism` (how it works), `rationale` (why it is built this way), `change` (where to change something) and `limits` (what it does not do and how it fails), the six kinds and spellings of the comprehension regression's key. The member adds no schema version, because it is inert, as `inventoryOmissions` is: it is optional, no label, citation, quotation, gate or rendered byte rests on it, and no finding but those of its own shape, and it is never rendered, so a v13 draft without it is checked and rendered exactly as before version 2.32. From version 2.32 the identifier `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v13` therefore names a schema with this optional member, the schema file a brief issues describes it, and that file's digest differs from the one a brief issued before version 2.32 states. Check SHALL accept the member in a v13 draft whichever of the two schema files its run was briefed with. A `blockId` is not an identity of the draft: Syzygy SHALL NOT count it among the draft's identities or resolve it at check, and a `blockId` naming no block, or named by more than one object, is no finding; the advice below reports both. A member of any other shape in a v13 draft is a `schema` repair finding at the path of the fault, a repeated kind among them, and the member in a draft of schema v12 or earlier is one `field-version` repair finding at `$.questionKinds` with the detail `` `questionKinds` needs draft schema polaris-dossier-local-draft-v13 ``, in place of the `schema` finding there; wherever this requirement says that a draft of an earlier schema is checked or rendered exactly as an earlier version checks or renders it, that exception holds too. Syzygy checks no claim in a declaration: which kinds a block answers is the authoring session's unverified statement. Syzygy SHALL NOT render it: no page, source page, machine view or other file of a rendered site carries the member. The fidelity packet carries the draft with the member, as it carries every member but the discovery account; no verdict rule reads it. The provider mode is unaffected: the provider stages' closed draft schema does not gain the member, and the rendered bytes the provider golden pins do not change.

`syzygy dossier check` SHALL also compute question-kind advice for every draft that parses, and SHALL carry it on its report as the member `questionKindAdvice`; check SHALL write it to no file: not the check record, the step log, a packet or a rendered page. It is advice, under every rule the inventory-coverage advice of version 2.12 is: no repair finding, no change to the outcome, exit status or any refusal, no class member, nothing of it in `run-advice.json`, read by no gate, verdict validation or readiness, and none for an inventory check; a failure to compute it never refuses or fails a check.
