Where the draft's schema is v13, the pinned tree was listed at this check and at least one object of `questionKinds` names a `blockId` that is a non-empty string and at least one of the six kinds, the advice SHALL be `{"state": "compared", "rule": {"id": "question-kind-floor", "version": 1}, "majorUnits": {"rule": {"id": "major-unit", "version": 1}, "share": "1/20", "limit": 8, "unitsNotListed"}, "kinds", "units", "declarations", "label": "Inferred", "basis", "note"}`; in every other case it SHALL be `{"state": "unknown", "why", "note"}` with no unit and no count, because kinds no one declared are Unknown, never six missing kinds (VIS-2). `why` is Syzygy's own sentence naming the case. Rule `major-unit` version 1 takes the facts of rule `top-level-unit` that this check derived from the pinned tree: the major units are the whole repository, named `.`, with every listed file, then each listed top-level directory holding at least one twentieth of the listed files, by files descending and then by name code unit by code unit, at most eight; the listed files leave out every path a secret detector matches, and no such path or directory is named. `unitsNotListed` is the count of directories that rule did not list. Rule `question-kind-floor` version 1 counts the claim blocks this requirement checks as claim blocks, other than understanding items, that are labelled Inferred and whose `id` some object of `questionKinds` names; each such block, counted once, its kinds the union of the kinds every object naming it lists, answers each of its kinds for the whole repository and for each major directory that one of its citations names a path inside, its first path segment being that directory's name. `kinds` lists the six kinds in the order above; each item of `units`, in the order of the major units, is `{"unit", "files", "answered", "missing"}`: `answered` maps each kind to the number of counted blocks answering it for that unit, and `missing` lists, in the order of `kinds`, those it maps to zero. `declarations` is `{"rows", "counted", "unmatched", "duplicated", "unnamed"}`: the objects that name a block and a kind, the distinct blocks counted, the named identifiers that are no counted block, in order of first mention, those named by more than one object, in order of second mention, and the number of distinct identifiers left out of those two lists because a secret detector of the bound screening policy matches them; such an identifier is never named. A failure to compute the advice, its detector among them, is the Unknown form. The advice is labelled Inferred, with `basis` saying the declarations are the authoring session's own and the units derived from the tree, and its `note` SHALL say that it sets no outcome and is not stored, what the six kinds ask, which blocks count and where, that a missing kind is a gap a reader will ask about and never a quota, and that a block is declared only for what it answers.

Brief v21 SHALL issue schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v13`. For the same run it SHALL equal brief v20 except for its `polaris-dossier-brief-v21` version line, its draft-schema line, which names the schema file's digest, and one paragraph placed immediately after the worth-knowing paragraph, before `## Task routes`, which reads: "A newcomer asks six kinds of question about each major part of a project: `purpose` (what it is for), `structure` (how its parts fit), `mechanism` (how it works), `rationale` (why it is built this way), `change` (where to change something) and `limits` (what it does not do and how it fails). In `questionKinds`, list each Inferred claim block that answers one: `{"blockId": "<the block's id>", "kinds": [...]}`, naming only the kinds it answers. Check counts these rows for the whole repository and for each top-level directory holding at least one twentieth of the files, a block counting for each such directory it cites a file inside, and advises the kinds no block answers there. It is advice, never a finding: write a block for a missing kind where the part has something to say, and never declare a kind a block does not answer. The rows are never rendered." Every statement above of what brief v20 carries, equals or differs by holds of brief v21 apart from those three differences. The success report and the brief record SHALL name brief v21. The paragraph names the six kinds and none of the regression key's questions or required facts, which stay out of every authoring text (the comprehension-regression rule of `CONTRIBUTING.md`).

A draft in schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v14` (version 2.33) joins the two schemas that descend from schema v10. It is a schema-v12 draft in every respect, with the containment kind of version 2.23 and the model and views of version 2.24, that MAY also carry every member a schema-v13 draft may carry, the worth-knowing mark of version 2.26 (`worthKnowing` on a claim block and `worthKnowingNone` on a Pole) and the `questionKinds` of version 2.32, each where and as a v13 draft may and under every rule this requirement states for it there, and it SHALL carry one member more, a top-level member `figurePlan`, the authoring session's figure plan, written before the prose: for each place a figure can be homed, the one question a picture there answers better than text and the kind of figure it needs, or why that place has none. The places are the overview, each section and each deep dive of the draft, each written as a v5 `home` names it: `{"kind": "overview"}`, `{"kind": "section", "target": <a section's identity>}` or `{"kind": "deep-dive", "target": <a deep dive's identity>}`, each `target` a string, and nothing else is a place form; two places are the same where their `kind` and their `target`, compared by exact code units, are. `figurePlan` is a list holding exactly one entry for each place, in any order, and each entry is exactly one of two forms. `{"home": <place>, "question": <text>, "kind": <figure kind>, "figure": <a diagram's identity>}` is a planned figure: `kind` is one of the nine figure kinds of versions 2.5 and 2.23, `flow`, `lifecycle`, `state-machine`, `boundary`, `dependency`, `placement`, `sequence`, `swimlane` and `containment`, and `figure` names the diagram that answers the question, which SHALL be homed at that place and be of that kind. `{"home": <place>, "omitted": <text>}` says why the place has no planned figure: no question there is answered better by a picture, or the planned figure could not be drawn. `question` and `omitted` are each a string of 1 to 300 Unicode code points holding at least one that is not white space (U+0009 to U+000D and U+0020). A planned figure names one diagram, and its place may hold other diagrams that the plan does not name. A graph or containment diagram of a v14 draft carries the `view` version 2.24 gives it, so a planned figure of one of those kinds names the diagram whose view draws it; the plan names diagrams alone, never an element, a relationship or a view, a view having no identity of its own, and the view's selection is checked by version 2.24 and never compared with the plan. The plan is the authoring session's working record, read by check alone: it is no claim block and carries no label, citation or quotation; no page renders it; `machine.json`, `dossier.json`, `derived-structure.json` and the size report carry none of it; and it enters no claim count, claim list, fidelity entry or reading ledger. Whether a planned question is the right one, and whether an omission's reason holds, are the authoring session's judgement and the rendered-design review's, never a check. Every rule this requirement and REQ-polaris-generation-035 state for a schema-v12 draft holds for a schema-v14 draft, and so does every rule they state for a schema-v13 draft's worth-knowing mark, its findings, its advice and its callout, and for `questionKinds` and its advice: wherever such a rule names schema v13 alone, as the worth-knowing advice, the callout and the question-kind advice do, it SHALL read schema v13 or v14. The v14 schema document admits `worthKnowing`, `worthKnowingNone` and `questionKinds` where and as the v13 document does, and the containment kind, `model` and `view` where and as the v12 document does. A plan entry's `question` is the authoring session's own words and none of the six question kinds of version 2.32: rule `question-kind-floor` reads no plan entry and counts no diagram, a diagram being no claim block it counts, and a `figurePlan` entry is never a `questionKinds` object.

For a v14 draft Syzygy SHALL report these repair findings, each of kind `figure-plan` unless another kind is named, with the literal detail given. At `$.figurePlan` where the member is absent, with the detail `` a v14 draft plans its figures: one `figurePlan` entry for the overview, each section and each deep dive ``; and where it is present and is not a list, with the detail `` `figurePlan` is a list of plan entries ``. At `$.figurePlan[<k>]`, `<k>` being the entry's index, for an entry that is not an object, that does not carry `home` and exactly the further members of one of the two forms, or whose `home` is not one of the three place forms, whose `question` or `omitted` is not a string of 1 to 300 Unicode code points holding at least one that is not white space, or whose `kind` is not one of the nine figure kinds, with the detail `` a plan entry is {"home", "question", "kind", "figure"} or {"home", "omitted"} ``; an entry with this finding is not well formed, and no other finding of this paragraph reads it. A finding of kind `unresolved-reference` at `$.figurePlan[<k>].home.target` for a well-formed entry whose `target` names no section, or no deep dive, of the draft, as its `kind` says, with the detail `` no section or deep dive has the identity named by `figurePlan[<k>].home.target` ``, `<k>` written as the entry's index. Such an entry names no place: it is a second entry for no place, the `home` of the diagram it plans is not compared with it, and it plans no place; every other finding of this paragraph reads it as any well-formed entry. At `$.figurePlan[<k>].home` for a well-formed entry naming the same place as an earlier well-formed entry that names a place, with the detail `` <place>: a second plan entry for this place ``. For a well-formed planned figure: a finding of kind `unresolved-reference` at `$.figurePlan[<k>].figure` where `figure` names no diagram of the draft, with the detail `` no diagram has the identity named by `figurePlan[<k>].figure` ``, `<k>` written as the entry's index; and, where it names a diagram, one at `$.figurePlan[<k>].figure` where that diagram's `home` is not the entry's place, with the detail `` <identity>: a planned figure is homed at its plan entry's place ``, and another where that diagram's `kind` is not the entry's `kind`, with the detail `` <identity>: a planned figure is of the kind its plan entry names ``, `<identity>` being the diagram's identity; a diagram that fails both raises both, the home finding first. Then, at `$.figurePlan`, one finding for each place that no well-formed entry names, the overview first, then the sections and then the deep dives, each in draft order, with the detail `` <place>: no plan entry for this place ``. `<place>` is written `overview`, `section <identity>` or `deep dive <identity>`, with the section's or deep dive's identity. The findings of the entries come in entry order, each entry's in the order stated here, before the findings for places no entry names, and all of them follow every finding version 2.32 reports for the draft. The v14 schema document admits any value at `figurePlan`, so that the generic schema check reports nothing at or below that path and every failure of the plan's form is a finding of this paragraph. A draft with such a finding does not proceed to review until a resubmitted revision passes. A planned figure whose diagram has a disposition other than `produced` raises no finding for that: the disposition is the draft's reasoned account of a figure not drawn.

For a v14 draft whose check reports no finding of kind `schema`, `syzygy dossier check` SHALL compute figure advice and SHALL carry it on its report as the member `figureAdvice`; check SHALL write it into no check record and no other file. The member is `{"rules": [{"id": "figure-information-floor", "version": 1}, {"id": "figure-duplicate", "version": 1}, {"id": "figure-mixed-levels", "version": 1}], "examined": <count>, "advisories": [...]}`, where `examined` counts the draft's diagrams whose disposition is `produced`, which are the figures the rules read; no rule reads a diagram of another disposition, or compares one with another figure. Each advisory is `{"rule", "diagram", "at", "detail"}`, naming the figure it advises on by its identity and, as `at`, by `$.diagrams[<i>]`, its index in the draft; advisories are ordered by that index and, for one figure, by the rule order above, and a rule gives at most one advisory for a figure. A graph or containment figure of a v14 draft, which carries a `view`, is read by every rule as its expansion, the schema-v11 figure version 2.24 renders it as: its nodes, edges and groups are the expansion's, in the expansion's order, a derived relationship's edge among them with the `relation` it carries, and a view entry naming no element or relationship of the model adds nothing to it. That two views select one element is no duplication in itself: their figures are compared by their keys as any two figures are. A containment's groups are read in draft order, or in its expansion's order, each group before its children and its children in their order. Rule `figure-information-floor` version 1 advises on a figure that carries too little to be worth a picture, a chain of three or fewer being a sentence: a figure of one of the six graph kinds with at most three nodes and at most one edge fewer than it has nodes; a `swimlane` with at most three steps; a `sequence` with at most two messages; and a `containment` with at most three groups in all; so a chain or fan of three boxes, or three groups one inside the next, is advised on, and a fourth node, a third edge among three nodes, a fourth step, a third message or a fourth group is not. Its detail is `` <identity>: a chain of three or fewer is a sentence; say it in the prose, or draw what a sentence cannot hold ``. Rule `figure-duplicate` version 1 advises on a figure that draws nothing another figure of its family does not, comparing keys made from labels. A label's key is the label with its leading and trailing white space removed, each run of white space inside it replaced by one space, and each letter A to Z replaced by its lowercase letter, white space being the characters U+0009 to U+000D and U+0020 and no other character being changed. A graph figure's keys are `["box", <key of the label>]` for each node and `["arrow", <key of its from-node's label>, <key of its to-node's label>]` for each edge; a sequence's, `["box", <key>]` for each participant and `["arrow", <key of its sender's label>, <key of its receiver's label>]` for each message; a swimlane's, `["box", <key>]` for each lane and `["step", <key of its lane's label>, <key of its label>]` for each step; and a containment's, `["box", <key>]` for each group and `["in", <key of its parent's label>, <key of its label>]` for each group drawn inside another. An edge's or message's own label is part of no key, and an edge, message or step whose reference names no node, participant or lane of its figure gives no key. A figure's keys are a set: a repeated key counts once, and the order of messages and steps is not compared, so two sequences of the same messages in another order are advised on as duplicates. Two figures are of one family where both are of graph kinds, both are sequences, both are swimlanes or both are containments. A figure A draws nothing another figure B of its family does not where A has at least one `arrow`, `step` or `in` key, every key of A is a key of B, and either B has a key that A has not, or the two have the same keys and B comes before A in the draft. The rule advises on A, naming the first such B in draft order, with the detail `` <identity>: everything it draws is drawn by <other identity>; keep one figure, or draw what the other does not ``; so of two figures with the same keys only the later is advised on, and a figure with one box, arrow or nesting of its own is not. Rule `figure-mixed-levels` version 1 advises on a graph or containment figure that draws a part of the system beside a part within it without saying that one holds the other. It reads only the `unit` members that version 2.19 lets a node carry and version 2.23 lets a group carry and, for a figure drawn from a view, the `parent` members of the model's elements, so every sequence and swimlane is in no comparison. An element X encloses an element Y where X is an ancestor of Y in the model: following `parent` from Y one element at a time, the walk reaches X before it reaches an element with no `parent`, a `parent` naming no element, or an element it has already reached. X also encloses Y where X's unit encloses Y's: where X's is `{"kind": "repository-root"}` and Y's is of any kind but `repository-root` and `dependency-name`; or where X's is `{"kind": "top-level-directory", "path": <P>}` and Y's is a `package-manifest`, `workflow-job` or `compose-service` unit whose `path` begins with P followed by `/`. A graph figure says that X holds Y where a chain of one or more edges of the figure whose `relation` is `contains`, each starting at the node the one before it ends at, leads from X to Y, and a containment says so where Y is drawn inside X, at any depth. The rule advises on a figure holding two elements X and Y, X enclosing Y, where the figure does not say that X holds Y, naming the first such pair, ordered by X's place among the nodes or groups and then by Y's, with the detail `` <identity>: <X's identity> and <Y's identity> stand for a part and a part within it, and the figure does not show that one holds the other; draw one level, or show the containment ``; so a graph of the repository root with its top-level directories joined by `contains` arrows, a containment that draws those directories inside the root, a figure of two top-level directories, and a figure of one top-level directory beside a package manifest under another is not advised on, while a containment that draws the root and one of its top-level directories side by side as two top-level groups, nesting something else, is, and so is a graph view showing an element and its `parent` with no `contains` arrow from the one to the other. The advice is Inferred, a computation over the draft's bytes alone that reads no blob, layout, record or fact, and two checks of the same draft bytes give the same member. A failure to compute it, at any point and for any cause, SHALL NOT refuse or fail a check: the report then carries no `figureAdvice` member. Each advisory is one a change to the draft clears, so it is advice to the author in the sense of version 2.9, and, like the inventory-coverage advice of version 2.12, it carries no class member and is on the report beside the `author`-class advice. It gates nothing: it is no repair finding, sets no outcome, exit status or readiness, is read by no render, review or close, and is counted by no reading ledger, reading difference, `run-advice.json` or page. Whether a figure the rules pass carries enough, says something new or keeps to one level stays the rendered-design review's judgement, and a figure the rules advise on may stand where the author judges it right. A check of a draft of schema v13 or earlier, of a v14 draft with a `schema` finding, or of an inventory carries no `figureAdvice` member, and its absence says nothing about the figures.

Brief v22 SHALL issue schema v14; it is brief v21 changed only in its version line, its draft-schema line, which names schema v14 and that schema's digest, and three paragraphs: immediately after the hero paragraph brief v21 carries, the containment paragraph brief v17 carries, word for word; immediately after that, the system-model paragraph brief v18 carries, word for word; and immediately after that, one paragraph which reads: "Plan the figures before you write the prose, in `figurePlan`: one entry for the overview, each section and each deep dive, its `home` written as a diagram's `home` is. For each place, write the one question a picture there answers better than text in `question`, the figure kind it needs in `kind`, and the diagram that answers it in `figure`, homed there and of that kind; for a graph or containment, that is the diagram whose `view` draws it. Where no picture would answer better than text, or the planned figure cannot be drawn, write `{"home": <place>, "omitted": "<why>"}` instead. The plan is not published. A draft whose plan misses a place, names one twice or names a figure that is not the one drawn there is refused; check only advises where a figure is a chain of three or fewer, draws nothing another figure does not, or draws a part beside a part of it, by `unit` or `parent`, without showing that one holds the other." Every statement above of what brief v21 carries, equals or differs by holds of brief v22 apart from those five differences. The success report and the brief record SHALL name brief v22.

A draft in schema v13 or earlier SHALL be checked and rendered exactly as version 2.32 checks and renders it, the same findings and the same bytes of every page, of `machine.json` and of every record check writes, and its check's report carries no `figureAdvice`, except that a `field-version` finding at `$.figurePlan`, with the detail `` `figurePlan` needs draft schema polaris-dossier-local-draft-v14 ``, stands in place of the `schema` finding at that path for a draft that carries `figurePlan`; wherever this requirement says that a draft of an earlier schema is checked or rendered exactly as an earlier version checks or renders it, that exception holds too. A v13 draft that carries a containment diagram, a `model` or a `view` keeps the `schema` finding version 2.26 gives it there: only schema v14 joins them to the worth-knowing mark. The plan and the advice move no rendered byte: Syzygy SHALL render a v14 draft's pages, `machine.json`, `dossier.json` and size report exactly as it renders, under the rules this requirement states for schemas v12 and v13, the same draft without `figurePlan`, apart from any byte that names the draft's schema or digest, and its reading ledger is that draft's. The provider mode is unaffected: the provider stages' closed draft schema gains no `figurePlan`, and the rendered bytes the provider golden pins do not change. The migration of version 2.24 is unchanged and yields a schema-v12 draft; no migration yields a v14 draft, a figure plan being the authoring session's to write.

A draft in schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v15` (version 2.35) is a schema-v14 draft in every respect but two. First, a graph or containment `view` MAY carry a member `base`, `{"map"}` with, optionally, either `hide` or both `select` and `restReason`, and, optionally, `group` and `names`: `map` is `directories` or `units`, naming the directory map or the unit map of version 2.9; `hide` lists one to 20 entries `{"paths": [<base path>, ...], "reason"}`, one to 40 paths each, which leave out the base elements they name; `select` lists one to 40 base paths and shows only the base elements they name, every other base element being left out for the one reason `restReason`; `group`, allowed in a containment view only, lists one to five entries `{"element", "paths"}`, `element` an element of the model and `paths` one to eight base paths, which draws those base elements inside that element's box; and `names` lists one to 40 entries `{"path", "label", "epistemic", "citations"}`, each the draft's display name for one base element, a claim of the draft with a label, marking and citations as a node's are, labelled Inferred or Unknown, whose claim identity is `<diagram>:name:<k>`, `<k>` counting the entries of `names` from 1. Each `reason` and `restReason` is one to 200 characters of the draft's own words, at least one of them not white space, saying why the elements are not shown; it is no claim about the system and takes no label or citation. A base path names base elements: for `directories`, `.` names the repository root and a top-level directory's path, written as the record writes its `path`, names that directory; for `units`, a manifest file's path names every listed `package` and `module` fact of that file, and that path followed by `#` and a decimal number `<k>` from 1, with no leading zero, names the k-th of them in the member's order, a base path ending in `#` and digits being read in that form. The `path` of a `names` entry names exactly one base element. Second, a cited relationship's `from` or `to` MAY be, in place of an element's identity, a base end `{"map", "path"}` whose `path` names exactly one base element, so that the draft may join two base elements, or a base element and an element of the model, by a typed arrow; such a relationship is the draft's claim as any cited relationship is, and a view selects it only where it shows both its ends. A `base` whose `map` is neither of the two or that carries any other member, a `hide` beside a `select` (at `hide`), a `select` without `restReason` or a `restReason` without `select`, a `hide` entry without a `reason`, a `reason` or `restReason` outside its bounds or all white space, `group` in a graph view, and a list outside its bounds are each a `schema` finding at that member. In a view with a `base`, the `elements` list takes zero to 40 entries and a graph view's `relationships` list zero to 80, and the view's figure is bounded by the `base-size` finding below. Every rule this requirement and REQ-polaris-generation-035 state for a schema-v14 draft holds for a schema-v15 draft: wherever such a rule names schema v14, or schema v13 or v14, and not v15, as the figure plan, the figure advice, the worth-knowing advice and callout and the question-kind advice do, it SHALL read schema v15 too, and the v15 schema document admits every member the v14 document does, where and as it does.

For a v15 draft Syzygy SHALL report these repair findings, each with the literal detail given subject to the diagnostic screening rule below, `<diagram>` being the view's diagram identity, computed only for a `base` whose members have no `schema` finding and only where the base map is established at check, that is, where the derived-structure record (for `directories`) or the `manifestFacts` member (for `units`) that check derives for the revision is not `unknown`. A finding of kind `base-reference` at each base path, in `select`, `hide`, `group`, `names` or a base end, that names no element of the map as check derives it, with the detail `` <diagram>: <path> names no element of the <map> map at this revision ``, `<map>` being `directory` or `unit`; and at the `path` of a `names` entry or a base end that names more than one, with the detail `` <diagram>: <path> names <n> elements of the unit map; a display name or an arrow end names one, as <path>#<k> ``; at a base end of a relationship each detail has `<diagram>` replaced by `model`. A finding of kind `base-conflict` at the second and each later base path naming the same base element within `select`, within the `hide` entries together, within the `group` entries together or within `names`, and at each `group` path or `names` entry naming a base element the view does not show, with the detail `` <diagram>: a base element is selected, hidden, grouped or named at most once, and grouped or named only where shown ``. A finding of kind `base-root`, in a containment view of the directory map, at a `hide` path or `group` path that names `.`, and at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.base.select` where `select` does not name `.`, with the detail `` <diagram>: the repository root frames a containment of the directory map and is shown, never hidden or grouped ``. A finding of kind `base-group` at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.base.group[<j>].element` for an element the view does not select, or one that version 2.24's expansion of the elements the view selects places at level 3, with the detail `` <diagram>: a group places base elements inside an element this view selects, at level 1 or 2 ``. A finding of kind `base-size` at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.base` for a view whose figure, its shown base elements and the model elements it selects together, holds no box or more than 40 boxes, with the detail `` <diagram>: a view shows one to 40 boxes, its base elements included ``. A finding of kind `base-label` at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.elements[<k>]` for an element the view selects, and at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.base.names[<k>].label` for a display name, whose label equals the name of a base element the view shows (`Repository root` for the root, its path for a top-level directory, its declared name for a package or module) or, for a display name, the label of an element the view selects or of another of the view's display names, and at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.relationships[<k>]` for a cited relationship of a graph view of the directory map whose label equals `contains`, two texts being equal where they are after surrounding white space and one trailing `/` are removed from each and each is case-folded, with the detail `` <diagram>: the draft's words never take the name of an element Syzygy draws in the same figure ``. Syzygy SHALL compute `base-group`, `base-size` and `base-label`, and the `view-shape` and `containment-flat` findings of version 2.24 for a view with a `base`, only for a view with no `base-reference` or `base-conflict` finding, and SHALL compute `view-shape` and `containment-flat` then over the view's figure as the expansion below gives it, base elements included. Where the base map is not established at check, none of these findings is computed and none is reported, the paths are left unresolved, and the figure renders as the render paragraph below states; an unresolved path is never counted as resolved. These findings follow the findings version 2.33 reports for the same view, in the order `base-reference`, `base-conflict`, `base-root`, `base-group`, `base-size`, `base-label`, each kind in the order of its paths (`select`, `hide`, `group`, `names`, then the view's elements and relationships), and the findings at base ends follow the model's relationship findings, in model order. A draft with such a finding does not proceed to review until a resubmitted revision passes. In a draft of schema v12 or v14 Syzygy SHALL report a finding of kind `field-version` at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.base` for a view that carries `base`, with the detail `` <diagram>: `base` needs draft schema polaris-dossier-local-draft-v15 ``, and at a relationship's `from` or `to` that is a base end, with the detail `` <relationship>: a base end needs draft schema polaris-dossier-local-draft-v15 ``; each stands in place of the `schema` finding version 2.33 reports at the same path, and every other finding version 2.33 reports for that draft is kept. A draft of schema v13, or of v11 or earlier, admits no `view` or `model` and takes no finding of this version: a `view` or `model` in it takes the findings version 2.33 states for it.

Apply the existing SEC-5 output screening to any diagnostic detail introduced by the preceding derived-base rules or computed specifically because those rules expand the figure. This includes the six v15 base kinds, the inherited `view-shape` and `containment-flat` details recomputed over the expanded base figure, and both `field-version` details for unsupported base members. Before constructing a detail, Syzygy SHALL require admission at that output of every draft-supplied value used in it, including each occurrence of a submitted path, each diagram identity and any relationship identity. A `base-reference` detail also requires admitted map evidence for its absence or ambiguity claim, including any count. The fixed word `model` at a base end is not a draft-supplied value. With admitted values and any required evidence, retain the literal detail. If a required screening result or supporting evidence is withheld or Unknown and the ordinary check can issue a scoped finding, retain its kind and structural field location and use only `Base reference unavailable under source screening.` for `base-reference` or `Derived-base detail unavailable under source screening.` for the other affected details. An existing fail-closed refusal takes precedence. Apply the same qualified detail before writing the check record or preparing any form of the check report. Keep the existing eligibility and ordering of findings. Findings neither introduced nor computed specifically by these derived-base rules retain their existing behavior. The late render branch uses the existence of a finding to choose its already specified fixed notice and emits no diagnostic detail. Read no additional source and disclose no unadmitted value or population count.

Syzygy SHALL draw a view with a `base` from the map as the render derives it, by the same derivation the map's page takes at that render and never from a stored check record, as follows. The base map is established at the render where the map's page draws its figures at that render: for `directories`, where the region does not state `Not established.`; for `units`, where the render's `manifestFacts` member is not `unknown`. A record whose facts render Unknown by the currency rule of version 2.3 is established, and its elements are drawn Unknown as the map draws them. The base elements are, for `directories`, the repository root and each top-level directory the record lists, in the record's order, each labelled as the directory map labels it, `Repository root` or `<path>/ (<count> files)`, with the mark, reason and `data-derived-id` of its fact; and, for `units`, each listed `package` and `module` fact, in the member's order, labelled `<name>`, Inferred, with its `data-manifest-id`. The view shows the base elements `select` names, or, where it has no `select`, every base element but those `hide` names. A graph view's figure is the graph whose nodes are the shown base elements, then the elements the view selects, and whose edges are, for `directories`, each `contains` fact of the record joining two shown base elements, drawn as the directory map draws it, in Syzygy's words with the fact's mark and `data-derived-id`, then the relationships the view selects. A containment view's figure is version 2.24's expansion of the elements the view selects, with base elements added: inside an element of the model, after the children that expansion gives it, the base elements its `group` entries name, in the entries' order and each entry's order; after that expansion's top-level groups, for `directories`, the repository root as one more top-level group, holding each shown top-level directory no `group` entry names, in the record's order, the nesting being the record's `contains` fact, stated in Syzygy's words and with that fact's mark in the figure's list as version 2.23 states a witnessed nesting; and, for `units`, each shown base element no `group` entry names, in the member's order, as a top-level group of its own. A base element is never a parent of another element of the draft. Each nesting of a base element inside an element of the model is the draft's claim, read by rule `relation-witness` version 1 as version 2.24 reads an element's `parent`, the base element taking the unit its map gives it (`repository-root`, `top-level-directory` or `package-manifest`), and is drawn as the draft's nesting is drawn. A base end of a cited relationship is drawn at that base element's box, and rule `relation-witness` reads a cited relationship with a base end as it reads one whose end is an element of the model carrying that base element's unit. No base element is ever a claim of the draft: its box and list item carry Syzygy's label, mark, reason and fact identity and never the draft's, it carries no claim anchor and takes no entry in the fidelity verdict, and it is drawn solid only where its fact renders Observed by the currency rule of version 2.3. Every base element's box is drawn with a double border, two lines 3 px apart, each in the line style of the element's mark, which no element of a draft takes, and its item in the figure's phone form begins `Syzygy: `. Where `names` gives a shown base element a display name, the name is never drawn inside the box: the drawing draws, immediately below that box and outside its border, the line `called <name>` in the figure's 13 px text, in italic, carrying `data-visual-provenance="curated"`, the name's `data-epistemic` and `data-name="<claim identity>"`, an Unknown name's line ending ` ?`, the box's layout slot growing by that line's height; the element's phone-form item ends `, called <name>`; and its list item carries, after Syzygy's words for it, `Called <name> by this dossier.` with the name's claim anchor, marking and citations, the name being the draft's claim and Syzygy's label staying first.

Every figure of a view with a `base` SHALL keep `data-visual-provenance="curated"` and carry `data-base-map="<map>"` on its `<figure>` element, and each base element's box, arrow and list item SHALL carry `data-visual-provenance="computed"` beside its fact identity, so that curated and computed provenance is machine-readable on every element as RFC7-28 requires. Its legend, which lists the line styles its drawing uses, SHALL add the entry `Double border: computed by Syzygy from the <map title>, in its words and marks.` Immediately after its caption the figure SHALL hold the chip, which Syzygy computes and no draft member writes: for `directories`, `<p class="base-chip" data-visual-provenance="computed">Showing <s> of <t> top-level directories, <with> the repository root, from the <a href="<href>">Directory map</a>; <h> hidden by this dossier.</p>`, `<s>` being the top-level directories the figure shows, `<t>` those the record lists, `<h>` = `<t>` - `<s>` and `<with>` `with` where the figure shows the root and `without` where it does not; and for `units`, the same element reading `Showing <s> of <t> packages and modules from the <a href="<href>">Unit map</a>; <h> hidden by this dossier.`, `<s>` being the packages and modules the figure shows, `<t>` those the member lists and `<h>` = `<t>` - `<s>`. A count of one takes the singular (`1 top-level directory`, `1 package or module`), and where `<h>` is 0 its clause reads `none hidden.`. `<href>` is that map's page path written relative to the page holding the figure, `maps/directories.html` or `maps/units.html` from a page at the root and `../maps/directories.html` or `../maps/units.html` from a page under `poles/` or `deep-dives/`. Where the map counts what it does not list, the chip adds one sentence, every count in it being the one the map's page takes at that render: for `directories`, where any of `<m>`, `<S>` and `<G>` is not zero, ` The map also counts <m> more top-level directories it does not list, <S> files it leaves out and does not name because a secret detector matches their path, and <G> submodules it does not follow.`, with the `<m>`, `<S>` and `<G>` of the directory map's coverage line; and, for `units`, where any of `<k>`, `<u>` and `<S>` is not zero, ` The map also counts <k> facts of the manifest files it does not list, <u> manifest files it read with no facts taken, and <S> manifest files it leaves out and does not name because a secret detector matches their path, so this figure may lack some.`, `<k>` being the member's `factsNotListed` and `<u>` and `<S>` the sums of the `unknown` and `secretPathsLeftOut` of the unit map's four recognizer entries; every clause is kept, and a count of one takes the singular (`1 more top-level directory`, `1 file`, `1 submodule`, `1 fact`, `1 manifest file`). The figure's list SHALL end with one item per `hide` entry, in their order, or, where `select` leaves base elements out, one item for `restReason`, each `<li class="base-hidden" data-hidden-count="<n>">Not shown here: <what>: <names>. This dossier's reason: <reason></li>`, `<n>` being the base elements it leaves out; `<what>` reading, for `units`, `<n> packages and modules`, and, for `directories`, `<d> top-level directories`, preceded by `the repository root and ` where the root is among them, or `the repository root` alone where it is the only one, `<d>` counting the directories, a count of one taking the singular; `<names>` the left-out elements' names in Syzygy's words (`Repository root`, `<path>/`, a package's or module's `<name>`), in the map's order, joined by `, `, the first 12 and then `, and <j> more` where there are more; and `<reason>` drawn from the screened author reason as stated below and escaped as HTML text. The `<n>` of the items sum to the base elements the figure leaves out. These items are the author's words about the figure, never a claim, and carry no anchor, label or citation. The figure's tally SHALL count the draft's elements and relationships alone, as version 2.24's does, its counts being zero where the view selects none, and be followed by ` <b> boxes and <a> arrows computed by Syzygy from the <map title>: <o> observed, <i> inferred, <u> unknown.`, counting the base elements the figure draws and the `contains` arrows a graph view draws, a count of one taking the singular (`1 box`, `1 arrow`). The figure's note SHALL follow its tally: the note the map's page carries at that render, word for word, which for the directory map is the region's note (`Derived by Syzygy from the paths and modes of the tree at revision …`) and for the unit map is that map's note (`Computed by Syzygy at this render from the manifest files …`), so that the embedded computed elements carry the rule and revision they were computed by (RFC9-38). `<map title>` is `Directory map` or `Unit map`. Where the base map is not established at the render, the figure SHALL be replaced by the notice `<p class="figure-notice" data-epistemic="unknown" data-unknown-reason="<reason>">Not established. This figure starts from the <map title>, which is Unknown at this render: <words>. <a href="<href>">See the map</a>.</p>`, `<reason>` being the map's own reason and `<words>` the text its page's `Not established.` sentence gives after `at this render: `, without its final period, and no element of the view is drawn: a figure resting on an unknown base is Unknown, never drawn from the draft's elements alone (VIS-2). Where the base map was not established at check and is at the render, the render computes the findings above for the view as check would; where any would be reported, the figure SHALL be replaced by the notice `<p class="figure-notice" data-epistemic="unknown" data-unknown-reason="source-uncaptured-or-unreachable">Not established. This figure starts from the <map title>, which this dossier's check could not read, and its base does not resolve against the map at this render. <a href="<href>">See the map</a>.</p>`, and no element of the view is drawn, so no path of the draft that the map does not list is applied, rendered or carried. `machine.json` SHALL carry, in the view's figure entry, a member `base`: where the base map is established at the render and the figure is drawn, `{"map", "established": true, "shown", "total", "hidden", "root", "notListed", "select", "restReason", "hides", "elements", "names"}`, `shown`, `total` and `hidden` being the chip's `<s>`, `<t>` and `<h>`, `root` `"shown"` or `"hidden"` for `directories` and null for `units`, `notListed` the chip's counts (`{"directories", "secretPathsLeftOut", "submodules"}` or `{"facts", "unknownFiles", "secretPathsLeftOut"}`), `select` the shown elements' paths or null, `restReason` `{"reason", "count"}` or null, `hides` one `{"paths", "reason", "count"}` per `hide` entry, both using the same admitted author reason as the corresponding HTML item, `elements` one `{"path", "factId", "provenance": "derived", "mark", "name"}` per shown base element in the figure's order, and `names` one `{"id", "path", "label", "epistemic", "citations", "provenance": "authored"}` per display name, `name` being that claim identity or null; and otherwise `{"map", "established": false, "reason"}` alone. Every path in it is written in the form the render resolves it to, `.` or a top-level directory's path for `directories` and `<manifest path>#<k>` for `units`, names an element the map lists at that render, and is never one the draft wrote that names none. Every element of the model and cited relationship in the figure carries `"provenance": "authored"` there, so a reader of the machine form tells derived from authored without the page. No base element and no chip renders on a map page, in `computedMaps` or in the derived-structure region, and a map's page, figures and `computedMaps` member are byte for byte those the render gives with no draft. Figure fit, the reading ledger and the figure's two forms take the figure as drawn, base elements and display-name lines included, the chip and the hidden items counting as the figure's text.

Before constructing a derived-base figure or its machine entry, Syzygy SHALL apply the existing SEC-5 output screening to each `hide.reason` and `restReason` it would present. Both output forms SHALL use the same admitted author text. HTML text escaping is applied to that admitted text. If a required reason is withheld, or its screening result is unavailable, refuse the affected figure in both forms through the ordinary screening refusal route, with a fixed withheld or unavailable reason. The affected HTML figure and machine figure entry are absent. Neither refusal carries submitted text or a derived `base` claim. An existing fail-closed refusal of the containing output takes precedence. Where the ordinary boundary permits the rest of the output, the map page and unrelated figures retain their existing behavior. Read no additional source and make no attribution to wording that was not admitted.

This version states the one exception to the rule that a draft cannot name, place, alter, suppress or add to a computed map, and states it narrowly. A view's `base` and a relationship's base ends name a map and paths of its elements; they never write to the map, its record, the `manifestFacts` member or `computedMaps`, never give a base element a label, mark, reason, identity or provenance other than Syzygy's, never draw a derived element the map does not draw, and never take a derived element out of the map page or its counts. Hiding is a choice of what one figure shows, and the chip and the hidden items state it on the figure, with what the map itself counts and does not list, so no left-out element disappears from the denominator (RFC9-44). The draft's own words about a base element, its display name, its group and the arrows it draws to it, are claims of the draft, checked, reviewed and marked as every claim is, and drawn beside Syzygy's words, outside its box and never in their place; no element of the draft takes a base element's double border or, in the same figure, its name.

A v15 draft's `figurePlan` is checked as version 2.33 checks a v14 draft's: a planned figure MAY name a graph or containment diagram whose view carries a `base`, and its home and kind are compared as any diagram's are, the base changing neither. The figure advice of version 2.33 is a computation over the draft's bytes alone that reads no record or fact, and a view with a `base` draws boxes it can read only from a computed map; so its rules SHALL read no figure whose view carries a `base`: `examined` does not count it, no rule advises on it, and rule `figure-duplicate` compares no other figure with it. Every figure whose view carries no `base` is read, counted and compared exactly as version 2.33 states. Whether a figure with a base carries enough, says something new or keeps to one level stays the rendered-design review's judgement, as it is for every figure.

Brief v23 SHALL issue schema v15; it is brief v22 changed only in its version line, its draft-schema line, which names schema v15 and that schema's digest, and one paragraph placed immediately after the figure-plan paragraph brief v22 carries, which reads: "A graph or containment `view` may start from a computed map: give it `base` with `map` `directories` or `units`, and Syzygy draws that map's elements in its own words and marks, with a double border. Leave elements out with `hide` entries, each with its `paths` and a `reason` in your words (a reason is required), or instead list the ones to show in `select` with one `restReason` for the rest; the figure will print how many it shows of how many, and link to the full map. A unit-map path names every package and module of that manifest file; add `#<k>` to name the k-th alone. In a containment view, `group` places named paths inside one of your elements at level 1 or 2; a containment of the directory map always shows the repository root. `names` gives a base element a display name, which is your claim and is drawn below its box, never inside it. A cited relationship may join a base element by giving `{"map", "path"}` as its `from` or `to`. You cannot relabel, re-mark or add to a map's elements, and none of your elements or names may take the name of a base element the same figure shows." Every statement above of what brief v22 carries, equals or differs by holds of brief v23 apart from those three differences.

A draft in schema v14 or earlier SHALL be checked and rendered exactly as version 2.33 checks and renders it, the same findings, the same `figureAdvice` and the same bytes of every page, of `machine.json` and of every record check writes, except, for a draft of schema v12 or v14, the `field-version` findings above in place of the `schema` findings at their paths; wherever this requirement says that a draft of an earlier schema is checked or rendered exactly as an earlier version checks or renders it, that exception holds too. A view of a v15 draft that carries no `base` SHALL be checked and rendered byte for byte as the same view in a v14 draft, and so SHALL every sequence and swimlane diagram. The provider mode is unaffected: the provider stages' closed draft schema gains no member, and the rendered bytes the provider golden pins do not change.

A draft in schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v16` (version 2.40) is a schema-v15 draft in every respect but one: a section or a deep dive MAY carry a member `walk`, one reading path through one figure drawn in that place, in numbered steps; a place carries at most one walk, so a place with two graph figures walks at most one of them. A walk is `{"figure": <a diagram's identity>, "steps": [<step>, ...]}`. `figure` names a diagram whose `home` names the section or deep dive that carries the walk and whose `kind` is one of the six graph kinds, `flow`, `lifecycle`, `state-machine`, `boundary`, `dependency` and `placement`; since a diagram has one home and a place one walk, no diagram is the figure of two walks that pass check. `steps` is a list of 2 to 12 steps in reading order, each `{"element": <identity or base end>, "block": <identity>}`; a step names one element, never several. A step's `element` names an element the figure draws: a node of the diagram, which for a diagram that carries the `view` of version 2.24 is an element of the model that the view selects, a node of the view's expansion, or, for a view that carries a `base` (version 2.35), a base element the figure shows, which the step names, in place of an identity, by a base end `{"map", "path"}` as a cited relationship's `from` or `to` names one, its `map` the view's base map and its `path` naming exactly one base element the figure shows; no element is named by two steps of one walk. A base element stays Syzygy's and never the draft's claim: a step that names one ties the step's paragraph to it as every step ties its paragraph to its element, and gives its box nothing but the step's id and badge. A step's `block` names one of the place's paragraphs, a claim block that the section's or deep dive's `paragraphs` list holds or, for a section with subsections, one that a subsection's `paragraphs` list holds, never a block inside a presentation element or a subsection's `answer`; and that block carries at least one citation. A citation suffices whatever becomes of it at render: a block whose citations are all withheld, or whose block renders Unknown, may be a step's paragraph, and the step's link then lands on that block as it renders, Syzygy's words in place of withheld text, as every other link to that block does. Several steps may name one block. A walk is presentation: it carries no claim, label, citation or quotation of its own and states nothing about the subject beyond the claims it ties together, and the order of its steps is presentation as the order of an ordered list's steps is, within the same review residual; that a step's paragraph speaks of its element is the authoring session's report and the fidelity and rendered-design reviews' judgement, never a check. A walk enters no claim count, claim list, fidelity entry, reading ledger, `machine.json`, `dossier.json` or `derived-structure.json`. Every rule this requirement and REQ-polaris-generation-035 state for a schema-v15 draft holds for a schema-v16 draft, the figure plan, its findings and its advice among them; wherever such a rule names schema v15, alone or in a list of schemas, it SHALL read it as naming schema v16 too. The v16 schema document admits any value at a section's or deep dive's `walk`, so that the generic schema check reports nothing at or below that path and every failure of a walk's form is a finding of the next paragraph.

For a v16 draft Syzygy SHALL report these repair findings for each walk, each of kind `walk` unless another kind is named, at the walk's path, `$.sections[<i>].walk` or `$.deepDives[<i>].walk` with the place's index, or below it, with the literal detail given; in a path or detail `<k>` is a step's index in `steps`, counting from 0. At the walk's path, for a walk that is not an object carrying exactly the members `figure` and `steps`, whose `figure` is not a string, whose `steps` is not a list of 2 to 12 entries, or one of whose steps is not an object carrying exactly the members `element` and `block`, `block` a string and `element` a string or a base end, an object carrying exactly the members `map` and `path`, each a string, with the detail `` a walk is {"figure", "steps"}, its steps a list of 2 to 12 {"element", "block"} ``; a walk with this finding is not well formed, and no other finding of this paragraph reads it. For a well-formed walk: a finding of kind `unresolved-reference` at `<walk>.figure` where `figure` names no diagram of the draft, with the detail `` no diagram has the identity named by `walk.figure` ``; where it names a diagram, one at `<walk>.figure` where that diagram's `home` does not name the walk's place, with the detail `` <identity>: a walk's figure is drawn where the walk is ``, and another where its `kind` is not one of the six graph kinds, with the detail `` <identity>: a walk's figure is of a graph kind ``, `<identity>` being the diagram's identity and the two, where both hold, in that order. Then, for each step in order: where the figure names a diagram of a graph kind, one at `<walk>.steps[<k>].element` where `element` names no element that diagram draws, with the detail `` `walk.steps[<k>].element` names no element drawn in <identity> ``, a base end that names no base element the figure shows, or names one in a figure whose view carries no `base` or another map, being such a step, and, where it names one that an earlier step of the walk names, with the detail `` <element>: a second step for this element ``; then one at `<walk>.steps[<k>].block` where `block` names no paragraph of the walk's place as the paragraph above defines one, with the detail `` `walk.steps[<k>].block` names no paragraph of this section or deep dive ``, and, where it names one that carries no citation, with the detail `` <block>: a step's paragraph cites its sources ``; `<k>` written as the step's index, `<element>` and `<block>` as the identities named, and `<element>`, for a step whose `element` is a base end, written as `` `walk.steps[<k>].element` `` with that step's index, so that no path the draft writes in a base end enters a finding's detail. A base end resolves against the base elements the figure shows by the derivation version 2.35 states for the view's own base ends at check; where the view's base map is not established at check, a step naming a base end takes no finding of this paragraph for its `element`, and the render resolves it as check would: where the render finds such a step that names no base element the figure shows, or one an earlier step names, the walk renders nowhere and no byte of that page moves for it. The walks are taken in draft order, the sections' before the deep dives', each walk's findings in the order stated here, and all of them follow every finding version 2.39 reports for the draft. A draft with such a finding does not proceed to review until a resubmitted revision passes. A walk whose figure has a disposition other than `produced`, or whose place has one, raises no finding for that disposition: the disposition is the draft's reasoned account of what is not drawn.

A walk renders exactly where its figure renders as a drawing whose width is not 1048 px: the figure is produced, its place renders the draft's content, the draft layer is not withheld, its drawing passes the inertness check, and the drawing is not one that version 2.2's rule for a 1048-px drawing lets shrink to its column; otherwise, as where the drawing renders the notice that takes its place, the walk renders nowhere and no byte of that page moves for it. Where it renders, Syzygy SHALL render, with no script, three things, `<diagram>` being the figure's identity and `<n>` a step's number, its index plus 1. First, the drawing in the figure's `<div class="diagram-scroll">` is wrapped in `<div class="walk-figure">`, which holds the drawing, byte for byte as Syzygy draws it without a walk, and after it the walk layer, `<div class="walk-layer">`, an HTML layer laid over the drawing with its top-left corner at the drawing's top-left corner. The layer holds one box for each node of the figure, in the order the drawing draws its nodes: `<span class="walk-node" style="left:<x>px;top:<y>px;width:<w>px;height:<h>px">`, where `<x>`, `<y>`, `<w>` and `<h>` are the position and size of the box the layout draws that node in, in the drawing's own coordinates. Version 2.11's readable layout draws every graph figure at its laid-out width, never scaled, save a drawing 1048 px wide, which this paragraph leaves without a walk, so those coordinates are CSS pixels and the layer's boxes cover the drawing's boxes at every viewport; a change that scales a drawing of any other width changes this paragraph. The box of a node that a step names also carries `id="walk-<diagram>-<n>"` and holds first the badge `<span class="walk-badge" aria-hidden="true"><n></span>`. A node's label, here and in the walk's list, is its label as the figure's list of every component and relationship shows it, or, for an element version 2.24 renders in an earlier figure, as that first figure renders it; it is that text alone, escaped as HTML text, never a link the list item carries, and a withheld element's label is the Syzygy words its list item shows for it; a base element's label is Syzygy's label for it as its box shows it (`Repository root`, `<path>/ (<count> files)`, or a package's or module's `<name>`), never a display name a `names` entry gives it, and its box is the double-bordered box the layout draws, without the `called` line below it. The box of a node that is not withheld and whose first citation has a link among the citation links of the figure that carries the node's claim (the figure itself, or for such an element its first figure) holds, after any badge, `<a class="walk-source" href="<href>"><span class="visually-hidden"><label>: cited lines</span></a>`, `<href>` being the address that citation's link carries there, resolved against the page that holds that figure and written relative to the page that holds this one, so that it lands where that link lands, on the cited lines of the source page. A withheld node, or one whose first citation has no such link, holds no link, and so does a base element's box, since a base element carries no citation: a step naming one gives its box the id and the badge, and the `:target` outline marks it as it marks every other box. Second, immediately after the `diagram-scroll` div, before the legend, the walk's list, `<ol class="figure-walk" aria-label="Walk through this figure">`, with one item per step in step order, `<li><a href="#walk-<diagram>-<n>"><label></a> <a class="walk-text" href="#block-<block>">Read step <n></a></li>`, `<label>` being the step's element's label and `#block-<block>` the fragment of the `id="block-<identity>"` that version 2.15 gives the step's paragraph, which the walk does not change: the walk adds no id to a paragraph and no byte of one. Every id the walk adds is `walk-` followed by a diagram identity, a hyphen and a step number; a diagram identity is a handle, the number is decimal digits, and Syzygy SHALL give no other element of a page that holds a walk an id beginning `walk-`, so each such id, read back from its last hyphen, names one figure and one step and is unique on its page, however many walked figures the page holds and whatever their identities share. The walk's list, read in order, is an ordered reading of the figure: each element in reading order, with a link to the paragraph that explains it; it sits beside the drawing and its list of every component and relationship, which stay the drawing's text alternative, unchanged. Every fragment target this paragraph adds or links lies outside every collapsible section. In the order of the page the boxes' links come before the walk's list, in the drawing's node order; after a reader follows a step's link, the page's target is that step's box, so the next element that takes the focus is that box's own `walk-source` link where it has one. The pages' stylesheet SHALL position the layer as stated, highlight the box whose id is the page's target, by the `:target` pseudo-class alone, with an outline that does not rely on colour alone, outline a box under the pointer as the drawing's own rules emphasise a node under the pointer, which the layer covers, set each badge at the top left of its box in the 13 px figure text, make each `walk-source` link fill its box and show the pointer cursor over it, and leave the drawing visible through every box; a page holding at least one walk SHALL carry those style rules, and a page holding none SHALL NOT, as the figure-kind rules of version 2.5 are carried only by a page holding such a figure, save that from version 2.45 a page holding no walk but a `zoom-link` element carries the rules that position the layer, as version 2.45 states, and no other of these rules. A walk adds no read, no source, no label and no claim, and no text of the draft but the element labels the figure's lists already show.

Brief v24 SHALL issue schema v16; it is brief v23 changed only in its version line, its draft-schema line, which names schema v16 and that schema's digest, and one paragraph placed immediately after the computed-map paragraph brief v23 carries after its figure-plan paragraph, which reads: "Where a section or deep dive explains a graph figure drawn there step by step, you may give it one `walk`: `{\"figure\": <that diagram's id>, \"steps\": [{\"element\": <a node's id, for a view an element it shows, or for a view with a `base` a base element it shows as {\"map\", \"path\"}>, \"block\": <the id of one of that place's paragraphs, which cites its sources>}, ...]}`, two to twelve steps in reading order, each element once. The page then shows each step's number on its element, lists the steps beside the figure, highlights an element when its step is chosen, and links each box whose element is not withheld to the lines its first citation cites; a base element's box takes the number and the highlight, never a link. The walk states nothing itself: each step's paragraph is what the reader reads about that element." Every statement above of what brief v23 carries, equals or differs by holds of brief v24 apart from those three differences. The success report and the brief record SHALL name brief v24.

A draft in schema v15 or earlier SHALL be checked and rendered exactly as version 2.39 checks and renders it, the same findings and the same bytes of every page, of `machine.json` and of every record check writes, except that a `field-version` finding at `$.sections[<i>].walk` or `$.deepDives[<i>].walk`, with the detail `` `walk` needs draft schema polaris-dossier-local-draft-v16 ``, stands in place of the `schema` finding at that path for a draft whose section or deep dive carries `walk`; wherever this requirement says that a draft of an earlier schema is checked or rendered exactly as an earlier version checks or renders it, that exception holds too. A v16 draft that carries no walk SHALL be checked and rendered exactly as the same draft in schema v15, apart from any byte that names the draft's schema or digest. A walk moves the bytes of the page that draws its figure (the wrapper, the layer, the list and the style rules), that page's measures in the size report and the rendered-design packet that carries them, and nothing else: no finding but this version's, no `machine.json`, `dossier.json`, check record, `run-advice.json`, reading ledger, figure-fit entry or figure advice, no byte of a paragraph, and no byte of the drawing. On a walked figure the layer, not the drawing, receives the pointer, so the drawing's own emphasis of a node under the pointer gives way there to the box's outline. The provider mode is unaffected: the provider stages' closed draft schema gains no `walk`, and the rendered bytes the provider golden pins do not change. No migration yields a v16 draft.

A draft in schema `polaris-dossier-local-draft-v17` (version 2.45) is a schema-v16 draft in every respect but one: the `view` of a graph or containment diagram MAY carry two members more, `level` and `scope`, the two together or neither. `level` is one of `context`, `container` and `component`, the view's level of abstraction: a context view shows a system with the people and the other systems it deals with, a container view shows the parts a system is built of, and a component view shows the modules inside one of those parts. `scope` is the identity of one element of the model, the element the view is about: the system at the centre of a context view, the system whose parts a container view shows, or the part whose modules a component view shows. Each of the nine element types of version 2.24 stands at one level: `system`, `external` and `person` at the context level; `directory`, `package`, `service`, `job` and `store`, the five container types, at the container level; and `module` at the component level. A view's level admits these types: a context view, the three of the context level; a container view, those three and the five container types, every type but `module`; and a component view, the five container types, `module`, `external` and `person`, every type but `system`. The scope of a context or container view is of type `system`, and the scope of a component view of one of the five container types. A base element of version 2.35 has, for this version, the type of the element it stands for: the repository root `system`, a top-level directory `directory`, a listed `package` fact `package` and a listed `module` fact `module`; a display name that a `names` entry gives a base element changes no type. A `scope` names an element of the model and never a base element, so a base element is never a view's scope: a context view over a base map, which always shows the repository root, is about a `system` element of the model, which it must select and so draws beside the root's box. `level` and `scope` are the authoring session's account of what the view shows, read by check and by the zoom links below: they are no claim block and carry no label, citation or quotation, they enter no claim count, claim list, fidelity entry or reading ledger, and `machine.json`, `dossier.json`, `derived-structure.json` and the size report carry none of them. A `level` that is none of the three, a `scope` that is not a string, and a view carrying one of the two members without the other are each a `schema` finding at that member, the last at the one it carries. Every rule this requirement and REQ-polaris-generation-035 state for a schema-v16 draft holds for a schema-v17 draft, the figure plan, the figure advice, the derived bases and the walks among them; wherever such a rule names schema v16, alone or in a list of schemas, it SHALL read it as naming schema v17 too, and the v17 schema document admits every member the v16 document does, where and as it does.

The word "level" has carried two meanings in this requirement, and from version 2.45 they are kept apart. A view's `level` is its level of abstraction, one of the three above; it is a property of a view, never of an element, an element's type standing at a level and a view's level admitting types. Rule `figure-mixed-levels` of version 2.33, and the words "draw one level" in its detail, speak of enclosure instead: one element being a part of another, by `parent` or by unit, as that rule states. The depth at which a containment draws a group, level 1, 2 or 3 in versions 2.23, 2.24 and 2.35, is a depth of drawing, which neither names. Neither reads the other: the checks of this version read element types and the scope's `parent` chain, never a unit; and the advice reads enclosure, never a view's `level` or `scope`, its rules, versions, details and outputs unchanged. So a container view that shows its scope beside the parts within it passes the level check and is advised on by rule `figure-mixed-levels` unless the figure shows that the scope holds them, by `contains` arrows or by drawing them inside it in a containment; and a view at one abstraction level that shows a package and a package within it passes the level check and is advised on as two levels of enclosure.

For a v17 draft Syzygy SHALL report these repair findings, each with the literal detail given, `<diagram>` being the view's diagram identity and `<level>` the view's `level`, computed only for a view whose `level`, `scope` and lists have no `schema` finding; every identity a detail names is a handle, and no detail names a base path. A finding of kind `unresolved-reference` at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.scope` for a `scope` that names no element of the model, with the detail version 2.24 gives that finding for an entry that names no element: `` <identity>: no element of the model has this identity ``, `<identity>` being the scope, where the scope is a well-formed identity, and, as version 2.24 names an entry with no well-formed identity, the path `$.diagrams[<i>].view.scope` in its place where it is not; so the detail repeats the scope only where it is a well-formed identity, and a scope written as any other string, a base path among them, enters no detail. Where the scope names an element: a finding of kind `view-scope` at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.scope` for a scope whose type the view's level does not take as a scope, with the detail `` <diagram>: a context or container view is about a system, and a component view about a directory, package, service, job or store ``, and, for a context view that does not select its scope, one with the detail `` <diagram>: a context view shows the system it is about ``, the two, where both hold, in that order. A finding of kind `view-level` at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.elements[<k>]` for each element the view selects whose type the view's level does not admit, with the detail `` <diagram>: a <level> view shows no element of type <type> ``, `<type>` being the element's type; and one at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.base` for a view with a `base` that shows a base element whose type the view's level does not admit, with the same detail, `<type>` being the first such type in the order `system`, `directory`, `package`, `module`. A finding of kind `view-scope-outside` at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.elements[<k>]` for each element the view selects that is, in a container view, of a container type, or, in a component view, of type `module`, and that is neither the scope nor below it, with the detail `` <diagram>: <element> lies outside <scope>, the element this view is about ``, `<element>` and `<scope>` being the two identities. An element is below the scope where, following `parent` from the element one element at a time, the walk reaches the scope before it reaches an element with no `parent`, a `parent` naming no element, or an element it has already reached, as rule `figure-mixed-levels` walks; only an element of the model is so read, never a base element or a unit, and a base element that a `group` entry draws inside an element is not thereby below it. Syzygy SHALL compute `view-scope`, `view-level` and `view-scope-outside` only where the scope resolves; `view-level` at an element only for an element whose `type` has no `schema` finding; `view-scope-outside` only where the draft holds no `element-parent` finding and no `unresolved-reference` finding at a `parent`; and `view-level` at `base` only where the base map is established at check and the view has no `base-reference` or `base-conflict` finding, as version 2.35 computes its own findings, so that where the map is not established the finding is neither computed nor reported. These findings follow every finding version 2.44 reports for the draft, the views in diagram order, and each view's in the order `unresolved-reference`, `view-scope`, `view-level` (its elements in list order, then its base) and `view-scope-outside`, in list order. A draft with such a finding does not proceed to review until a resubmitted revision passes. In a draft of schema v12, v14, v15 or v16 Syzygy SHALL report a finding of kind `field-version` at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.level` and one at `$.diagrams[<i>].view.scope` for a view that carries that member, with the detail `` <diagram>: `level` and `scope` need draft schema polaris-dossier-local-draft-v17 ``; each stands in place of the `schema` finding version 2.44 reports at the same path, and every other finding version 2.44 reports for that draft is kept. A draft of schema v13, or of v11 or earlier, admits no `view` and takes no finding of this version.

An element has its own view where the view of a graph or containment diagram of a v17 draft carries a `scope` naming it and a `level` that shows what lies inside it: `container` for an element of type `system`, and `component` for an element of one of the five container types. A context view is about its scope but shows it from outside, among the people and systems it deals with, so a context view is never its scope's own view and never a zoom target. An element's zoom target is the first diagram that is its own view, in draft diagram order, whose figure renders as a `<figure>` on a page outside every collapsible section, a notice standing in a figure's place not counting, so that a context view about the element, and a view of the element whose figure renders inside a collapsible section or as a notice, are passed over wherever they stand in draft order; an element with no such figure has no zoom target, and a base element, which no `scope` names, never has one. Syzygy SHALL give a zoom link, with no script, to each element with a zoom target that a figure of a v17 draft draws as a box, a graph node or a containment group, in every such figure but the zoom target itself. `<href>` is `#figure-<target>` where the target figure is on the same page and otherwise the target figure's page path written relative to the page holding the link, followed by `#figure-<target>`, as version 2.24 writes the address of an element's first figure; `<target>` is the target's diagram identity, whose figure carries `id="figure-<target>"` (version 2.15); and `<title>` is the target's diagram title as its figure renders it, escaped as HTML text. The link is given in two places. First, in the figure's list of every component and relationship, the element's item gains, after everything it holds, one space and `<a class="zoom-text" href="<href>">Zoom in: <title></a>`, in the figure that carries the element's claim and in each later figure that draws it alike. Second, where the figure, of a graph kind or `containment`, renders as a drawing under the conditions version 2.40 states for a walk's figure, the figure produced, its place rendering the draft's content, the draft layer not withheld, the drawing passing the inertness check and its width not 1048 px, the drawing carries the layer version 2.40 states: a wrapper holding the drawing byte for byte and after it `<div class="walk-layer">`, the wrapper being `<div class="walk-figure">` for a figure that has a walk, byte for byte as version 2.40 writes it, and `<div class="walk-figure zoom-only">` for a figure that has none. The stylesheet draws a containment figure, as it draws a graph figure, at its laid-out width, never scaled, save a drawing 1048 px wide, which this paragraph leaves without a layer, so the layer's coordinates are CSS pixels for both. For a graph figure the layer holds the boxes version 2.40 states; for a containment figure it holds one box `<span class="walk-node" style="left:<x>px;top:<y>px;width:<w>px;height:<h>px">` for each group, in the order the expansion lists the groups, each group before its children, at the position and size of the box the layout draws that group in, in the drawing's own coordinates. The box of an element with a zoom target holds, after any badge and any `walk-source` link a walk gives it, `<a class="zoom-link" href="<href>"><span aria-hidden="true">+</span><span class="visually-hidden">Zoom in to <label>: <title></span></a>`, `<label>` being the element's label as version 2.40 takes a node's label. A figure with a walk and zoom links has one wrapper, the walked one, and one layer, each box holding what both give it; a figure with zoom links and no walk gives its boxes no id, badge or `walk-source` link, so a box with no zoom link is empty, and its layer passes the pointer to the drawing everywhere but over a zoom link, so the drawing keeps its own emphasis of a node under the pointer. A figure that draws no element with a zoom target is rendered as this requirement renders it without this paragraph. In the order of the page each box's zoom link follows that box's other links, and every link of the layer comes before the figure's walk list and its list of every component and relationship, so a keyboard reader moving forward from the figure reaches each zoom link in the drawing's node or group order; following one makes the target `<figure>` the page's target. A view that carries `level` and `scope` gives its figure's `<figure>` element `data-view-level="<level>"` and `data-view-scope="<scope>"`, after every attribute version 2.35 gives it and before its `id`, which stays the last attribute. A page holding at least one `zoom-link` element SHALL carry three sets of style rules.
